# US-10.1 - Open Decisions (v8)

## v8 revision note (CLARIFICATION attempt 2: the human's answers to the two blockers and to OD-38 to OD-47, OD-49, SD-25, SD-1 to SD-40)

**Revision history (this file is revised in place; the prior revision's status becomes SUPERSEDED, `artifact-schema.md`).**

| Revision | Status | Note |
|---|---|---|
| v1 to v6 | SUPERSEDED | See the older revision notes below. |
| v7 | **SUPERSEDED** (by v8, through the `supersedes` pointer) | Recorded the approval-comment decisions and opened OD-36 to OD-49; ended `open_decisions_blocking: true` (OD-36, OD-37). |
| v8 | DRAFT (current) | This revision. Records the human's answers (below); ends `open_decisions_blocking: false`. |

**Trigger.** `so-orchestrator` ran CLARIFICATION attempt 2 after the human answered, in one message,
OD-36 and OD-37 (the two blockers), OD-38 to OD-47, OD-49, SD-25, and accepted SD-1 to SD-40. Each answer is quoted verbatim (Ukrainian,
with an English rendering) at the head of its entry as a "v8 resolution" block. The Story (v1), Specification (v4) and review (v4) were read
and not edited. Every claim was checked against the repository, the Story, `AGENTS.md` and the numbered conditions of the earlier
RESOLVED decisions. **Nothing from v1 to v7 is dropped.** The human is the decider: where a statement is clear it RESOLVES the entry; where
the decision has a residual flaw the Specification can carry, the entry is RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS with numbered conditions; the readings that are
the Specification's or this stage's proposals, not the human's words, are labelled "Proposal (not a decision)" and collected in **OD-50**.

**Outcome: `open_decisions_blocking: false`.** OD-36 and OD-37 are decided; no remaining entry makes the Specification writer guess a
contract (each open point has a labelled proposal in OD-50). The Specification can be revised (v5) against this log.

Parent entries OD-12, OD-14, OD-15, OD-16, OD-18, OD-20, OD-21 keep the status kind RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS; their headings now say that
every residue is decided, and each carries a short "v8 cross-note". OD-30 C-30.1 is amended by the acceptance of SD-15 (cross-note in OD-30).

**Honest blocking re-assessment (rule unchanged: block only where the Specification writer would have to guess a contract).**
- **OD-36 stops blocking.** The human fixed policy (oldest by last activity), measure (stored sessions) and notice (none). What the decision
  leaves open is not a contract the writer must invent: the protected set, the tie-break, atomicity with the creation, the self-healing count and the
  effect of empty sessions are each stated in OD-36 conditions or OD-50 as a labelled proposal with its alternative. The point that matters: the premise "the session in use is the most recently active" is **false** under the now-confirmed SD-32 (opening or
  viewing is not activity, and a send's stamp moves only at the FR-44 commit), so unconditioned eviction can delete the session the
  operator is standing in, the source of a model switch (FR-50), or the old session of a blocked-state exit (FR-47). C-36.4 therefore
  carries the exclusion as a condition (it follows from the human's word "неактивну", from the confirmed T-23 text "The current session stays
  in the session list", and from FR-47); the tie-break stays a proposal.
- **OD-37 stops blocking, but its recovery premise is not true as written.** The human expects the operator to "add the image again" after
  T-10. The retained first user turn `U1` is protected and resent on every send (AC-21, AC-24, AC-29; OD-7 C-7.4) and still holds the dead
  `file_uri`; with no stored bytes the next send meets `FILE_MISSING` again. The literal consequence of the decided rules is that T-10 ends the
  session's ability to send, and only "New session" (plus a new upload) recovers; viewing and downloading still work. This is **disclosed
  as a visible consequence**, not blocking, because the rules fix the outcome deterministically; the human is asked to confirm or choose another
  in OD-50 (item P-6).
- **OD-39 raises a test-feasibility fact, not a blocker.** `happy-dom` (the logic runner) has no canvas adapter configured: `getContext()` returns
  `null` and `toBlob()` yields an empty Blob; no `canvas` package is installed (`node_modules` listing, `HTMLCanvasElement.js` lines 128-210); the
  Angular `unit-test` builder (component runner) runs on Vitest too and no spec in `src/` touches a canvas. A gate test therefore cannot show that a
  real canvas drops EXIF; the property is a browser fact (check V-8). The Specification must word FR-13's test as an injected-encoder test and the
  real-browser check as V-8 (proposal W-2 in OD-50).

**Repository facts checked this run (read-only).** `node_modules/happy-dom/lib/nodes/html-canvas-element/HTMLCanvasElement.js` (no adapter: `getContext`
returns `null`, `toBlob` returns an empty Blob of the requested type, `toDataURL` returns an empty payload); `node_modules` lists no `canvas`,
`jsdom`, `sharp`, `pngjs` or JPEG package; `package.json` scripts and `vitest.config.ts` (environment `happy-dom`, no canvas), `angular.json` `test`
(`@angular/build:unit-test`, runner `vitest`); no `.browserslistrc` and no browser list in `package.json`; `src/app/app.component.ts` lines 1342-1348 and
1417-1445 (the existing Image Tools mode already encodes through `canvas.toBlob(..., imgTargetFormat(), imgQuality())`, a precedent that includes
`'image/webp'` as an option, but with no check of the returned `blob.type`; the Vision path uses `toDataURL('image/jpeg', 0.9)`); `src/` has no
IndexedDB use (unchanged). Vendor and browser behaviours named below (HTML canvas serialization falling back to PNG for an unsupported type, EXIF
orientation handling, colour management) are **not** verified here; they are the checks V-8.

**Earlier "awaiting confirmation" labels that the SD acceptance now settles** (the Specification flips them; no earlier text is edited because the
older records are kept for history): OD-12 C-12.4 (CF-19), OD-16 C-16.1 (SD-32, SD-33), C-16.3 (CF-13), C-16.4 (SD-34), OD-18 C-18.6 (CF-17), C-18.7
(clipboard), C-18.10 (SD-40), OD-20 C-20.2 (SD-38) and C-20.3 (SD-35, CF-9), OD-21 C-21.3 (CF-10), OD-48 C-48.2 (CF-21, D-18) and C-48.6 (SD-25).
Defaults **still awaiting confirmation** at `HUMAN_SPEC_APPROVAL`: D-1 to D-6 and D-8 to D-13 (the human named none of them), the proposals in OD-50, and
the V-checks.

Blocking rule used (unchanged): `blocking: true` when a wrong guess by the specification writer would breach an AGENTS.md rule (architecture,
security, dependency or CI change that AGENTS.md section 1 / 7.8 reserves to a human), or would make a core criterion unverifiable or
self-contradictory. A line starting "Proposal (not a decision)" is an aid for the human, never an answer.

N-11) in the `/so:approve` comment of 2026-11-01 and two follow-up messages the same day; those decisions exist only inside
Specification v4, while this log (v6) still listed ten entries as OPEN, and the Specification raises questions (Q-12a to Q-21a) and 24
conflicts (CF-1 to CF-24) that no entry records. This run records them. The Story (v1), Specification (v4) and review (v4) were read in full and
not edited. The human's Ukrainian text is quoted verbatim inside each entry. Every factual claim was checked against the repository, the
Story and `AGENTS.md`; the checks are in each entry. **Nothing from v1 to v6 is dropped; every earlier RESOLVED / RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS
decision is intact.** This stage answered none of the new questions; a line "Default (awaiting confirmation)" is a Specification-proposed
value, never a decision.

**Outcome: `open_decisions_blocking: true`.** Two new entries are blocking: **OD-36** (Specification Q-16a: what happens when a 21st
session is created) and **OD-37** (Q-16c: a quota failure while storing an upload's sent bytes). Each leaves a requirement (FR-86 with

default. The human must answer exactly these two (the options are in the entries). Nothing else is blocking.

**Honest blocking re-assessment (rule unchanged: block only where the Specification writer would have to guess a contract).**
- **OD-36 and OD-37 block.** A guess changes behaviour that deletes user data (OD-36: evicting a session deletes its versions, and the human's
  OD-23 confirmations covered trimming, not eviction; a refusal would block the automatic creations of FR-47 and FR-50) or silently
  degrades recovery (OD-37). No default exists in the Specification or in any earlier decision; none is invented here.
- **OD-18 stays non-blocking, and its v6 trigger is retired.** The v6 text said "OD-18 becomes blocking if V-6 (i) records a non-PNG format"
  because the then-open conflict was AC-31 versus AC-32 when the API returns a non-PNG image. The human's rule (output format = input
  format, converted on the client when the API answers differently) settles that conflict by decision: AC-31's "without re-compression" is
  waived exactly when a conversion happens, and the delivered-bytes table of FR-82 covers every pair of formats. A JPG or WEBP input with a PNG
  API answer therefore always converts, regardless of what V-6 (i) records. V-6 (i) still matters, but only as a fact: it says how often the
  waiver applies and which fixtures to build (it is no longer a re-open trigger).
- **OD-38 (re-encode format, animated WEBP) is the closest call and stays non-blocking.** FR-13's observable result (no EXIF/GPS in the sent
  bytes, decodable, source dimensions) is testable without a format, and every later format rule (FR-82) reads the user's original file (D-19),
  not the re-encode. It does have a visible effect through D-7 (a file of at most 5 MB whose re-encode is over 5 MB is rejected, CF-20) and for
  alpha and animation, so the human should answer it with the others; if left unanswered the planner must choose and say so.
- **CF-1 to CF-24:** only CF-15 (= Q-16a = OD-36) blocks. The nine "confirm-and-record" conflicts are recorded as numbered conditions of their
  parent entries; the other fourteen are readings awaiting confirmation (OD-49).

**Repository facts checked this run (read-only).** `uiLanguage = signal<'en' | 'uk'>('en')` at `src/app/app.component.ts:424`, persisted as
`seo_gen_ui_lang` (lines 663, 689), toggled at line 774. `src/prompt-core/prompt-presets.ts` does not exist; `src/prompt-core/**/*.ts` is in the
coverage `include` of `vitest.config.ts` (lines 55-60), so the AGENTS.md section 5 floors apply to it; the FROZEN list (AGENTS.md section 9) names
five other files. `retryTransport` is applied inside `LlmService.post()` (`src/services/llm.service.ts` lines 24-35), described there as "the one
choke point", so an image method that went through `post()` would inherit it, which OD-5 forbids. There is no IndexedDB use anywhere in `src/` (no
storage convention to mirror). `package.json` lists no EXIF or image library; the client already draws to a canvas and exports with
`canvas.toDataURL('image/jpeg', 0.9)` (`src/app/app.component.ts` lines 1342-1348, 1417), a precedent for a canvas re-encode. AGENTS.md has no rule
forbidding a human waiver of an automated test for one criterion; section 6 item 7 only requires that the AC-to-test matrix reflect what was
built (so the waiver must be stated in the matrix: OD-20 C-20.2). AGENTS.md sections 1 and 7.8 reserve new dependencies to a human (OD-39).

**Wording and structure fixes for the Specification are not Open Decisions;** they are hand-offs in the clarification report v7.

**New input.** The human (repository owner, the decider; relayed by the orchestrating agent, Ukrainian text quoted verbatim inside each
entry) answered the five blocking decisions **OD-13, OD-17, OD-19, OD-26, OD-27**, the earlier-open **OD-8** and **OD-22**, and the
non-blocking **OD-28 to OD-35**. The human also explicitly confirmed (1) deletion of the user's own mid-session uploads when a pair is
trimmed (the OD-23 v5 cross-note) and (2) irreversible deletion of trimmed versions with no download offered (OD-23). Those two
confirmations were relayed in English by the caller, without a Ukrainian verbatim, as the OD-23 and OD-24 decisions were in v4.
The Story (v1), the Specification (v1) and its review (v1) are unchanged and were read as context only. Every factual claim in the
answers was checked against the repository, the Story and `AGENTS.md`; the checks are in each entry. Where a decision is clear but has a
residual flaw the Specification can carry as a constraint, the entry is **RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS** and each condition is numbered.
Nothing from v1 to v5 is dropped; every earlier RESOLVED / RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS decision is intact.

**Outcome: `open_decisions_blocking: false`.** None of the ten tension points the caller named needs a new contract from the human:
each is recorded as a numbered condition, a flagged default (awaiting the human at `HUMAN_SPEC_APPROVAL`, never a decision), a
hand-off, or a conditional re-open trigger tied to a pre-implementation check.

## v5 revision note (loop-back from SPEC_REVIEW; historical)

**Historical: the v5 table below records the v5 classification; the v6 table at the top of this file supersedes it.**

**New input.** No new human answer. The independent Specification review (CHANGES_REQUIRED, `changes_required_clarification`) found
that (1) the Specification's five observations O-1 to O-5 are real gaps with no Open Decision; (2) four entries marked
`blocking: false` (OD-13, OD-17, OD-18, OD-19) are, by this log's own text, ones that leave a core criterion untestable or
self-contradictory; (3) FR-54 is the cited failure path for FR-15, FR-31 and FR-35 but defines only upstream 429 and 5xx; and (4)
smaller decision-shaped findings. Each was re-checked against the Story, the Specification, the repository and `AGENTS.md`. This
stage answered none of them.

**Blocking rule applied (unchanged from v4, stated again because v5 applies it more strictly).** `blocking: true` when a wrong guess by
the Specification writer would breach an AGENTS.md rule (architecture; a new dependency, CI or workflow change reserved to a human by
sections 1 and 7.8), or would make a core criterion unverifiable or self-contradictory, or when a decision the human already took
cannot be turned into a requirement without a value the human has not given. A gap that leaves only a narrow, separable part of one
criterion open, and for which a default is derivable from decisions already recorded, stays `blocking: false` but is labelled as a
default awaiting the human, never as a decision.

| ID | v4 | v5 | Blocking now | Why |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| OD-13 | OPEN, non-blocking | OPEN, **re-classified** | **true** | AC-42 cannot be falsified without a definition of "generation" and of the window; its own text says so, and the Specification (FR-59) agrees. The state question (process memory vs persisted vs a library) is also a dependency or storage choice that AGENTS.md section 7.8 reserves to a human. |
| OD-17 | OPEN, non-blocking | OPEN, **re-classified** | **true** | The human-decided OD-24 exit ("new branch/session") cannot be specified without it, and AC-13, AC-23, AC-26, AC-27, AC-33, AC-35, AC-44 and the turn-id scope (C-23.2) all depend on it. FR-41 and FR-44 defer every property of a branch. |
| OD-19 | OPEN, non-blocking | OPEN, **re-classified** | **true** | It decides what the history contains. AC-21 (carry every prior turn) conflicts with AC-40 for a text-only reply, and the trim contract (OD-7 pair units, OD-23 exact `trimmedTurnIds`) is defined only for a strictly alternating sequence ending at the latest version; turns after it are undefined. OD-4 C-4.3 forbids the Specification to pick the usage-row rule. |
| OD-18 | OPEN, non-blocking | OPEN, **kept non-blocking** | false | See the OD-18 entry: the contradiction exists only if the API returns a non-PNG image, which is a vendor fact (V-6, extended in v5) rather than an owner decision. It becomes blocking in the next run if V-6 records a non-PNG format. |
| OD-5, OD-6, OD-8, OD-12, OD-14, OD-15, OD-16, OD-20, OD-21, OD-22, OD-25 | OPEN | OPEN, kept non-blocking | false | Reviewed: each leaves a narrower, separable part of one criterion open. v5 cross-notes and the reason for non-blocking are in the entries (OD-8 and OD-22 are the two the review named as uncovered halves). |
| **OD-26** (new, O-1) | - | OPEN | **true** | The Ukrainian names (and ids) of the five presets are required by AC-16 / AC-17 and supplied by nothing. |
| **OD-27** (new, O-2) | - | OPEN | **true** | How `file_uri` originals count toward the 18 MB / 80% / protected-remainder measures. One reading makes the first legitimate message fail with no recovery. |
| OD-28 (new, O-3) | - | OPEN | false | Whether the "Edit this version" attachment counts toward the 5 of AC-3, and whether AC-4 / AC-5 apply to it. |
| OD-29 (new) | - | OPEN | false | Failure paths FR-54 does not define: non-retryable upstream errors, the outcome after exhausted retries. A derivable lead exists. |
| OD-30 (new) | - | OPEN | false | A non-safety non-image response and a response with more than one image. A derivable lead exists. |

Also in v5: (a) **OD-24 C-24.6 is amended** - the choice of which older versions offer "Edit this version" in the blocked state was
split between "the Specification's call" (OD-24) and "an OD-17 question" (OD-17 v4 cross-note); it is now OD-17 sub-question (g)
and the Specification must not choose it. (b) **V-7 added** and **V-6 extended** (see the verification table). (c) A consequence of the pair unit (OD-7
C-7.4, OD-23) the review surfaced is recorded as an item for the human at `HUMAN_SPEC_APPROVAL` (see the OD-23 v5 cross-note): a
removed pair deletes the user turn too, including any images that user turn carried.

buffer"), FR-20 ("the rest of the UI stays responsive", carried from Story AC-11) and the review's other Specification-local items
belong to the Specification writer; they are listed as hand-offs in the clarification report v5, not here.

## Previous revision note (v4, historical)

**Historical: v4 ended with `open_decisions_blocking: false`; the sentence "No blocking Open Decision remains" below was true of v4 and is
superseded by the v5 table above.**

**New input.** The human (repository owner, decider) made the final decisions: **OD-23** variant A (the BFF reports the turns it
trimmed in every HTTP 200 response; the client permanently deletes them from IndexedDB), **OD-24** (a terminating error when the
protected history alone exceeds the limits), and an explicit acceptance of the RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS items OD-1, OD-3, OD-4,
OD-7, OD-9 and OD-11 "in full". Their text is quoted in each entry. Every factual claim was checked against the repository,
`AGENTS.md` and the Story. No blocking Open Decision remains: **`open_decisions_blocking: false`**.

| ID | v3 | v4 status | Blocking now |
|---|---|---|---|
| OD-23 | OPEN, blocking | **RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS** (lead A; atomic apply, turn ids, headroom arithmetic, permanent-deletion amendments) | false |
| OD-24 | OPEN | **RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS** (413 plus body code `PROTECTED_HISTORY_EXCEEDS_LIMIT`; discriminator against the OD-11 413; not retried; AC-22 exit) | false |
| OD-1, OD-4, OD-7, OD-9, OD-11 | RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS | unchanged status; conditions **accepted by the human**; OD-7 C-7.2 and OD-11 C-11.2 tightened (see entries) | false |
| OD-3 | RESOLVED | unchanged; the 501 / no Retry button reading (N-3.1) is now explicitly the human's | false |
| **OD-25** (new) | - | **OPEN**, non-blocking: client recovery when a generate body is rejected above 30 MB despite OD-23/A | false |
| OD-5, OD-6, OD-8, OD-12 to OD-22 | OPEN | carried forward unchanged and still open; v4 cross-notes added to OD-13, OD-14, OD-16, OD-17, OD-19, OD-21 | false |

**Why no blocker remains.** OD-23/A defines the whole history contract between client and BFF (what is sent, what is cut, who
remembers it) without any BFF state. Its residual edges are constraints or checks the Specification can carry: atomic apply,
stable turn ids, the headroom arithmetic (C-23.7, with measured sizes from V-6; a failure of the arithmetic becomes a new
decision for the human, not a guess), and request granularity. The OD-24 discriminator and retry rules are fully derivable.
The one genuinely unanswered recovery case (OD-25) has a derivable, flagged default.

## Previous revision note (v3, historical)

**New input.** The human (repository owner, sbruhov@gmail.com), who is the decider, answered the three remaining blocking Open
Decisions (OD-4, OD-7, OD-9) and the non-blocking OD-1, OD-11 and the OD-3 residual. The answers are quoted verbatim (Ukrainian,
with an English rendering) inside each entry. Every factual claim in them was checked against the repository, `AGENTS.md` and the
Story; the checks and the evidence are in each entry. Where a decision is clear but carries a residual technical flaw that the
Specification can carry as a constraint, the entry is **RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS** and each condition is a numbered constraint the
Specification MUST carry. This stage chose no business or architecture option on the human's behalf.

**Outcome.**

| ID | v2 | v3 status | Blocking now |
|---|---|---|---|
| OD-4 | OPEN, blocking | **RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS** (server-computed per-image cost returned per response; client accumulates; successful calls only) | false |
| OD-7 | OPEN, blocking | **RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS** (assumption accepted with fallback; AC-28 client-driven re-upload; 404-to-410 rule must be narrowed) | false |
| OD-9 | OPEN, blocking | **RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS** (in-suite Vitest test that builds into `.test-dist`; the literal command is not sufficient) | false |
| OD-1 | PARTIALLY ADDRESSED | **RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS** (IDs human-supplied, still to be verified in AI Studio before IMPLEMENTATION) | false |
| OD-11 | PARTIALLY RESOLVED | **RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS** for the mechanism and 411; residual (b) promoted to **OD-23**; residual (c) stays open | false |
| OD-3 | RESOLVED + R-3.1 | **RESOLVED** (R-3.1 closed: 501) | false |
| OD-2, OD-10 | RESOLVED | unchanged | false |
| **OD-23** (new) | - | **OPEN** - promoted from OD-11(b); not fixed by any of the human's answers | **true** |
| OD-24 (new) | - | OPEN (irreducible request) | false |
| OD-5, OD-6, OD-8, OD-12 to OD-22 | OPEN | carried forward, still open; cross-notes added | false |

**Why OD-23 is blocking although all three v2 blockers are cleared.** It is the sharp form of v2's OD-11(b) (non-blocking, "how do the
30 MB / 18 MB / 20 MB thresholds relate"), made unavoidable by the human's own OD-7 and OD-11 answers taken together: the BFF is
stateless (verified in v1/v2: no session store in `server/`), the client re-sends the full IndexedDB history every turn (AC-21,
AC-25), the BFF trims it (AC-24, AC-29) but the client is never told what was cut, and a pre-parser check rejects any body over
30 MB (AC-30) before the BFF can trim anything. The client body therefore grows monotonically and eventually every request is
rejected. Under the log's own blocking rule ("would make a core criterion unverifiable or self-contradictory") AC-29 and AC-30 collide
in any long session. None of the human's answers fixes it, and every available lead amends an AC (see OD-23), so the Specification
writer would have to choose a contract. This is a promotion of an existing finding, not a new invention.

**Checks made on the six tension points the caller named (details in the entries).**

| Point | Outcome |
|---|---|
| (a) OD-4 "failed/cancelled/blocked not logged" vs AC-43 / AC-19 / AC-44; per-image prices; token path and `FALLBACK_PRICE` | AC-43 must read "every **successful (HTTP 200)** generation request"; AC-19's preset half and AC-44's rows narrow to successful calls; prices are illustrative ("напр.") and keyed by name mismatch with OD-1; `getPrices` falls through to `FALLBACK_PRICE` for the new IDs, so the image path must be separate and tested. RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS. |
| (b) OD-7 404 to 410; 18 MB then 80%; first-turn-and-latest fallback vs AC-22 | "any 404 to 410" would mask a wrong model ID (OD-1 IDs are unverified) - rule must be narrowed and the retry bounded. The fallback does not break AC-22 mechanically (the attached version travels in the new turn), but "latest" and the pair boundaries need explicit definitions. The size-then-tokens order is sound but exposes the stateless-trim gap: OD-23. RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS + OD-23. |
| (c) OD-9 build in `beforeAll` | `ng build --output-path=.test-dist` works (run here: about 12 s builder, 18.6 s wall); `npm run build` alone writes `dist/`; output lands in `.test-dist/browser/`; Vitest default hook timeout is shorter than the build; `.test-dist` is not git-ignored; gate order and §7.8 are not changed (human is right), §5 "stay fast" is a recorded tension. The key value is unavailable to a spec. RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS. |
| (d) OD-11 411 | Browsers send `Content-Length` for string/Blob/FormData/ArrayBuffer fetch bodies, so the client is consistent; the rule must be scoped to body-bearing methods, mounted after `cors()`, and verified through Railway's proxy. RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS. |
| (e) OD-1 IDs | Human-supplied; Flash has no `-preview` suffix, Pro has one, the Story says "Gemini 3 Pro Image" and OD-4 uses a third spelling. Verify in AI Studio before IMPLEMENTATION. Server-side filter and spec changes enumerated. RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS. |
| (f) OD-3 residual | 501 decided. RESOLVED. |

**Story-text overrides and sharpenings recorded for the Specification** (the Story file is owned by so-story-writer and was not
edited; the Specification takes these from this log and must not carry the original wording where one is overridden). Cumulative
from v2: rows marked **NEW** were added in v3, rows marked **NEW (v4)** in v4:

| Decision | Story text affected | Effect |
|---|---|---|
| OD-10 | Context "its own route `/image-studio`"; Scope "Surface: New route `/image-studio`" | **Overridden**: a new `AppMode` value `'image-studio'`; no URL route, no router. |
| OD-3 | Scope "a Gemini image adapter"; Context/Q3 | **Sharpened**: a new `generateImage` method on `GeminiProvider` plus the matching `LlmProvider` interface / `LlmService` method; the mode uses Gemini regardless of `LLM_PROVIDER`. **NEW**: a missing `GEMINI_API_KEY` returns **HTTP 501**. |

| OD-23 | AC-29 / AC-30 (and OD-11 mechanism) | **NEW (v4) - Sharpened**: every HTTP 200 generate response carries the list of trimmed turn ids; the client applies the cut in one IndexedDB transaction, so the next body stays under the 30 MB cap. No BFF session state. |
| OD-23 | AC-22 "Every earlier version has an Edit this version action"; AC-23 "thumbnails of all session versions"; AC-35 "ZIP of the whole session" | **NEW (v4) - Narrowed**: these cover the versions still in the session; versions trimmed by AC-24 / AC-29 are permanently deleted from the device and absent from the strip, the ZIP and the action. |

Failed, text-only and safety-blocked calls therefore consume quota although they write no usage row. `countTokens`, `/api/image-studio/upload` and requests rejected by the pre-parser (411/413) or the 501 are not counted. |

Uk Mk` (accepted image turns only). | OD-36 | AC-25 "kept for 7 days" (already amended by OD-16); AC-26 "the previous session remains available in a session list"; AC-27 "starts a new branch" | **v8 - Amended (human, with conditions)**: the list holds at most 20 **stored** sessions. Creating a session (New session, model switch, blocked-state exit) while 20 are stored silently deletes the stored session with the oldest last-activity stamp (whole record), as part of the creation. AC-26 becomes: the previous session remains in the list until it expires (7 days from its last activity) or is evicted as the least recently active of 20 stored sessions, **never in the same action that leaves it** (C-36.4, proposal D-23). AC-25's 7 days is therefore an upper bound. No refusal path exists. |
| OD-37 | AC-28 "re-uploads it from the session copy, transparently" | **v8 - Amended (human)**: transparent recovery holds only while the sent bytes could be stored; if the store hit the quota, the attachment is still attached and sent, and a later expired file ends in T-10 with no upload request (the consequence for the session is OD-50 P-6). |
| OD-38 | AC-5 "stripped from every image" (mechanism); AC-4 | **v8 - Sharpened (human)**: the EXIF-removing re-encode keeps the original format (PNG to PNG, JPG to JPG, WEBP to WEBP); an animated WEBP becomes a static first frame. |
| OD-39, OD-40 | AC-5 "EXIF metadata, including GPS, is stripped from every image before it is sent to the model"; AC-4; AC-2 | **v8 - Narrowed (human)**: by a native client canvas re-encode of every image added through the Image Studio UI; no BFF size or EXIF check; a direct API call can bypass both (accepted). |
| OD-46 | AC-8 "A message may be text-only ... or text plus images"; AC-47 | **v8 - Extended (human)**: a message with images and no text is valid (a third form); the `alt` of an image whose prompt is empty is "Згенероване зображення" (`uk`) / "Generated image" (`en`). |
| OD-45 | AC-34 "Copy to clipboard (PNG)" | **v8 - Confirmed (human)**: the clipboard copy is always PNG, converting when the API format is not PNG. |

Blocking rule used (unchanged): `blocking: true` when a wrong guess by the specification writer would breach an AGENTS.md rule
(architecture, security, dependency or CI change that AGENTS.md section 1 / 7.8 reserves to a human), or would make a core criterion
unverifiable or self-contradictory. Everything else is `blocking: false` but must still be answered or explicitly accepted before
`HUMAN_SPEC_APPROVAL`. A line starting "Proposal (not a decision)" or "Lead (not a decision)" is an aid for the human who answers,
never an answer.

## OD-1 - Gemini image model IDs (Story Q1) - RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS

**Question.** What are the exact model ID strings for Nano Banana 2 (Flash) and Nano Banana Pro, and in which configuration file do
they live?

**Human decision (v3, verbatim).** "OD-1: ID моделей: gemini-3.1-flash-image та gemini-3.1-pro-image-preview. На сервері у
resolveSlot додати фільтр, що ігнорує моделі з "modalities": ["image"] для текстових запитів. Правила model-catalog.spec оновити,
щоб дозволяли відсутність поля levels для image-моделей." (English rendering: Model IDs: `gemini-3.1-flash-image` and
`gemini-3.1-pro-image-preview`. On the server add a filter in `resolveSlot` that ignores models with `"modalities": ["image"]` for
text requests. Update the `model-catalog.spec` rules to allow a missing `levels` field for image models.) Builds on the v2 proposal
(add both models to `src/prompt-core/model-catalog.json` with `"modalities": ["image"]`, filter on the client).

**Verification.**
- `src/prompt-core/model-catalog.json` exists and is the one catalog read by both runtimes (`src/prompt-core/model-catalog.ts`,
  `server/providers/model-support.js`); not FROZEN. Consistent with the Story ("Model IDs live in configuration, not in code").
- The IDs are **human-supplied and unverified**. No image model id exists anywhere in the repo. They are internally inconsistent in
  spelling (Flash has no `-preview` suffix, Pro has one), the Story's own Q1 says the sources disagree (`gemini-3.1-flash-image` vs
  `gemini-3.1-flash-image-preview`) and tells the implementer to verify in AI Studio, and the Story Context calls Pro "Gemini 3 Pro
  Image" while the supplied Pro ID reads 3.1. This stage cannot establish which strings are real; that is check **V-3**.
- The server filter is the right place: `resolveSlot()` takes the model id from the request body and resolves it with
  `findModel(provider, slot?.model)`, then falls back to `models.find(m => m.tier === preferTier)`, then `defaultModel()` (`models[0]`).
  A client-only filter would not stop a crafted request. `server/llm-request.js` and the Gemini/Anthropic providers call `resolveSlot`.
- Existing invariants the change meets (all in `src/prompt-core/model-catalog.spec.ts` unless noted): the `ALL_MODELS` loops that
  require non-empty `levels` and a `defaultLevel` inside them (lines 9-13), `excludes disabled from every Gemini model` (44-48),
  `objectContaining({ levels })` parity (59-65), the `clampLevel` probes over every model (106-114, 293-302, 340-352), the FR-5
  test pinning `defaultModel('gemini')` to `gemini-3.1-pro-preview` (221), and `ModelSpec` itself. In the app: `clampLevel` in
  `src/prompt-core/model-catalog.ts` (line 74) and `server/providers/model-support.js` dereference `model.levels`;
  `model-settings.service.ts` builds `deepModels` / `fastModels` from `findProvider(...).models` (lines 76-77, 158) and
  `model-settings.component.html` reads `spec.levels.length`.

**Conditions the Specification MUST carry.**
1. The IDs are recorded as human-supplied; the Specification states them as defaults and lists **V-3** (verify in AI Studio) before
   IMPLEMENTATION. The recognition rule in OD-7 must never treat a wrong-model-id 404 as an expired file (see OD-7 C-7.2).
2. The filter covers every path that selects a text model: the `findModel` step, the tier fallback and `defaultModel` in
   `resolveSlot`, and `clampLevel`'s `findModel ?? defaultModel` - on the server **and** the client (`model-settings.service`
   computeds and `model-catalog.ts`). The image route has the inverse rule: it accepts only models whose `modalities` include
   `image` and rejects a text model id.
3. Image entries must never be `models[0]` of the `gemini` provider (the FR-5 test pins the deep default), so they are appended after
   the existing entries.
4. The `ModelSpec` shape for image entries is stated explicitly. The human decided only that `levels` may be absent; `defaultLevel`,
   `tier` and `maxOutputTokens` (required today) are not addressed, and the Specification must say what each is for an image entry
   rather than let the implementer choose.
5. The `model-catalog.spec` change is **scoped to image-modality entries and adds assertions**: every text model still has non-empty
   `levels` with a valid `defaultLevel`; every image entry has the `modalities` field and the new fields the Specification defines.
   Making `levels` optional for all models, or skipping the loops, is an AGENTS.md section 7.7 violation.
6. `.env.example` is updated if an image-model env override is introduced (AGENTS.md section 6, item 7). None is required by the
   decision.

**v4 (human acceptance).** The human accepted this entry's conditions in full: "model IDs rigorously verified in AI Studio before
development; the `modalities` filter applied on both client and server." This is condition 1 (V-3) and condition 2 above, unchanged.
Added: the OD-4 price keys follow the ID that V-3 verifies (one spelling everywhere).

**Blocking: false** (decided; IDs to be verified, V-3).

## OD-2 - Client IP behind Railway's proxy (Story Q2) - RESOLVED

**Question.** How does the BFF derive the client IP for the AC-42 rate limit (a `trust proxy` setting with which hop count or list, or a
specific header), and what is the behaviour when the header is absent or spoofed?

**Human decision (v2, verbatim).** "OD-2: app.set('trust proxy', 1). Railway додає X-Forwarded-For і діє як єдиний зворотний проксі."
(English rendering: `app.set('trust proxy', 1)`. Railway adds `X-Forwarded-For` and acts as the single reverse proxy.)

**Verification.**
- `server/index.js` has no `trust proxy` setting today; nothing in `server/` reads `req.ip`, `req.protocol`, `req.hostname` or
  `req.secure` (grep), so a global `app.set('trust proxy', 1)` changes no existing behaviour. Express in `node_modules` is 5.2.1.
- The repo does record a Railway deployment (`server/index.js` lines 19-20, 40-42, and the SIGTERM comment near line 285), which
  corrects v1. The BFF serves no static files (`express.static` is absent) and `.env.example` `ALLOWED_ORIGINS` names a separate
  frontend origin, so the browser presumably calls the BFF's Railway domain directly.
- **What the repo cannot show:** that Railway is the **only** proxy in front of the BFF. That is a deployment fact. It is accepted on the
  human's authority as the owner of the deployment; it was not independently verified.
- Behaviour with the setting (Express semantics): with `trust proxy` = 1, `req.ip` is the address the last (nearest) proxy appended;
  earlier, client-supplied `X-Forwarded-For` entries are not trusted, so a client cannot reset its bucket by spoofing the header. A
  request that reaches the BFF without a proxy header gets `req.ip` = the socket address.

**Recorded condition (assumption A-2).** The hop count of 1 holds only while Railway is the sole proxy. If a CDN, host rewrite or custom
proxy is ever placed in front, `req.ip` becomes that proxy's address and the per-IP bucket becomes shared. The Specification must state
this assumption; whether to add a deploy-time check is the Specification's/planner's call, not decided here.

**Not decided here.** What counts as a "generation", the window semantics, in-memory vs persisted state and the 31st-request response
remain OD-13. This decision settles only how the key is derived.

**Blocking: false** (decided).

## OD-3 - Rule 1 / `LlmProvider` and image generation (Story Q3) - RESOLVED

**v6 cross-note (OD-29, OD-13).** The 501 is one of the BFF-reserved statuses of OD-29 C-29.1: it carries its own closed code in the `{ error: string }` envelope, and an upstream-derived 501 must not be read as this signal (OD-29 C-29.2). It is not counted by the rate limit (OD-13 C-13.2). The language of its text is still OD-14.


**Question.** How is image generation reconciled with AGENTS.md section 3 Rule 1? (a) extend `LlmProvider`, add a separate image-provider
module, or record an arch-guard exception; (b) does the mode depend on `LLM_PROVIDER` or the per-slot settings, or work regardless;
(c) what does the BFF return when `GEMINI_API_KEY` is absent?

**Human decision (v2, verbatim).** "OD-3: Розширити існуючий інтерфейс LlmProvider новим методом (напр. generateImage(prompt, images,
options)). Якщо LLM_PROVIDER встановлено на інший сервіс (напр. Anthropic), цей метод все одно використовує клас Gemini для
зображень. Якщо GEMINI_API_KEY немає, виклик /api/image-studio/... повертає 501 Not Implemented (або 503), UI показує: "Gemini API key
is required for Image Studio"." (English rendering: Extend the existing `LlmProvider` interface with a new method (for example
`generateImage(prompt, images, options)`). If `LLM_PROVIDER` is another service, this method still uses the Gemini class for images.
If `GEMINI_API_KEY` is missing, a call to `/api/image-studio/...` returns 501 Not Implemented (or 503), and the UI shows "Gemini API
key is required for Image Studio".)

**Human decision (v3, verbatim; closes R-3.1).** "OD-3: Для відсутнього GEMINI_API_KEY повертати 501 Not Implemented." (English
rendering: For a missing `GEMINI_API_KEY`, return 501 Not Implemented.)

**Verification (v2 findings, still valid).**
- Consistent with Rule 1 and AGENTS.md section 7.3: the SDK call lives inside `server/providers/` (a method on `GeminiProvider` in
  `server/providers/gemini.js`); no SDK import is added under `src/`. `arch-guard.sh` Rule #1 greps `src/` only and stays green. No
  arch-guard exception and no new dependency is required.
- Independence from `LLM_PROVIDER` is mechanically possible: `resolveProvider('gemini')` in `server/providers/factory.js` returns a
  `GeminiProvider` regardless of `LLM_PROVIDER`; `warmProviders()` already reports a provider without a key as "not ready" without
  throwing, so a missing key is detectable.
- **Wording correction.** `LlmProvider` is a frontend TypeScript interface (`src/services/providers/llm-provider.interface.ts`) with
  one implementer, `LlmService`. "Extend the interface" means: add `generateImage` to the interface and implement it in `LlmService`
  (HTTP to `/api/image-studio/*`), **and** add the method to `GeminiProvider` on the server. The OpenAI and Anthropic server classes
  are untyped JS and need no method: the BFF route obtains the Gemini provider directly.
- The signature `generateImage(prompt, images, options)` is illustrative ("напр."); it cannot carry the multi-turn history with its
  `thoughtSignature` fields (AC-21), Files API URIs (AC-28), cancellation (AC-11) or the per-response cost (OD-4). The Specification
  defines the real contract; this decision fixes only where the method lives and that the provider is Gemini.

**v3 checks on the 501 decision.** 501 is decided and unambiguous (the "or 503" of v2 is dropped). It is a 5xx, and AC-37 says 429/5xx
are retried with a Retry button, so the Specification notes below apply.

**Notes the Specification MUST carry (consequences of the decision, not new questions).**
- N-3.1: AC-37's retry-with-backoff applies to **upstream** 429/5xx. A BFF-originated 501 for a missing key is not retried by the BFF
  and shows no Retry button (retrying cannot fix it). The client's `isUpstreamTransportFailure` (`src/services/http-retry.ts`) does not
  retry 501 and does not retry any response that carries the `{ error: string }` envelope, so the 501 must use that existing envelope
  (the shape `sendError` produces).
- N-3.2: the UI text "Gemini API key is required for Image Studio" does not contain the literal `GEMINI_API_KEY`. The OD-9 bundle test
  fails on that literal, so no client string may contain the environment variable's name.
- N-3.3: the language of that message is OD-14 (the quoted text is English; AC-18 and AC-46 are Ukrainian). Not decided here.

**v4 (human acceptance).** "A missing key returns hard `501 Not Implemented`, no Retry button." This makes N-3.1 the human's own
decision; the 501 uses the existing `{ error: string }` envelope so `retryTransport` does not retry it.

**Blocking: false** (decided).

## OD-4 - Usage logging rule, prices and session cost total (Story Q4, AC-43, AC-44, AC-19) - RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS

**v6 cross-note (OD-19, OD-13, OD-22, OD-35).** The OD-4 rule "only successful generations (HTTP 200) are logged and billed" is **refined by the human in OD-19(d)**: a row is written, and cost is added to the session total, only for an HTTP 200 that **contains at least one image**. A 200 with text only, a safety block (a 200 warning envelope), a failed turn and a Cancel write nothing and cost 0. This closes C-4.3 (the open sub-question of a 200 without an image). Amendments (also in the cumulative table): AC-43 reads "every HTTP 200 generation response that contains at least one image logs `usageMetadata`"; AC-44's total and rows and AC-19's preset half cover those responses only. Cost = unit price x number of images in the response, **one row per request** (OD-30). The rate limit (OD-13) counts a wider set than the log writes, by design (OD-13 C-13.8). The AC-19 "downloaded" half is a client-only flag (OD-22). The `mode`-marker sub-question of C-4.7 is moot (OD-35). The price keys and V-5 are unchanged.


**Question.** (a) Which prices and billing unit does AC-44's estimated total use, and who supplies them? (b) Where do image-studio usage
rows live? (c) Do failed, safety-blocked, cancelled and retried calls count toward cost and appear in the log? (d) Is the running
session total computed on the server or on the client, and is it persisted with the session?

**Human decision (v3, verbatim).** "OD-4: Сума сесії рахується на клієнті: BFF розраховує вартість кожного успішного запиту і
повертає її у відповіді (напр. поле `cost_usd`); клієнт додає її до локального стану в IndexedDB і показує загальну суму сесії
(AC-44). Одиниця тарифікації — за згенероване зображення, не за токени. У server/usage/pricing.js додаємо фіксовані ціни для
image-моделей (напр. gemini-3.1-flash-image: $0.03 за зображення, gemini-3.1-pro-image: $0.06 за зображення); computeCost на
сервері перевіряє тип моделі і застосовує ціну за генерацію. Запити, що завершилися помилкою, блокуванням безпеки або Cancel, не
тарифікуються і не записуються в usage_log; записуються лише успішні генерації (HTTP 200)." (English rendering: The session total is
computed on the client: the BFF computes the cost of every successful request and returns it in the response (for example a
`cost_usd` field); the client adds it to the local state in IndexedDB and shows the session total (AC-44). The billing unit is per
generated image, not per token. In `server/usage/pricing.js` we add fixed prices for image models (for example
`gemini-3.1-flash-image`: $0.03 per image, `gemini-3.1-pro-image`: $0.06 per image); `computeCost` on the server checks the model type
and applies the per-generation price. Requests that end in an error, a safety block or Cancel are not billed and are not written to
`usage_log`; only successful generations (HTTP 200) are written.) The v2 schema decision stands: preset and resolution in the
existing `task_label` column, no migration.

**Verification.**
- **(d) resolved, and the v2 defect is gone.** v2 recorded that summing `GET /api/usage` cannot isolate a session (no session key, no
  accounts, a shared table). The v3 design (per-response cost, accumulated in the persisted session) avoids that: it needs no
  session key in the shared table.
- **(c) resolved.** The rule matches the existing precedent: in `server/index.js` `/api/llm/generate` writes `insertUsage` only after
  a successful provider call, and a thrown error goes straight to `sendError` with no row. `usage_log` has no outcome column, so
  "not logged" is the only available behaviour without a migration.
- **(b) feasible and unchanged.** `usage_log.task_label` is a nullable TEXT column written by `insertUsage` and returned as
  `taskLabel` by `GET /api/usage`.
- **(a) the unit is decided; the figures are not final.** The prices are explicitly examples ("напр."). Nothing in the repo records
  Google's image pricing, so they are vendor facts: check **V-5**. v3 also drops v2's "depending on resolution": the price here is per
  model only. `server/usage/pricing.js` today is tokens x per-1M rates (`computeCost(model, usage)` has no resolution input and no image
  count).
- **Code facts that make the image path unsafe if added naively.** `getPrices(model)` matches by exact key, then by substring in
  both directions, then returns `FALLBACK_PRICE` ($3 in / $15 out per 1M, Claude-shaped). Neither `gemini-3.1-flash-image` nor
  `gemini-3.1-pro-image-preview` contains, or is contained in, any current key (nearest: `gemini-3.1-pro-preview`), so both would
  silently fall to `FALLBACK_PRICE` and be priced by tokens. The OD-4 text names the Pro key `gemini-3.1-pro-image`, while OD-1
  supplies the catalog ID `gemini-3.1-pro-image-preview`; a price keyed on the former would match the latter only by the substring
  loop, by accident of iteration order.

**Tension points examined (the caller's item (a)).**
- **AC-43 "every request logs `usageMetadata`" vs the new rule.** The Specification MUST adjust AC-43 to "every **successful (HTTP 200)
  generation request** logs a `usage_log` row with input tokens, output tokens, model and resolution". A failed, safety-blocked or
  cancelled request logs nothing, so AC-43 as written would otherwise be false for those.
- **AC-19 (preset used / downloaded).** The "preset used" half is carried by the `task_label` of the success row, so it exists only for
  successful generations; a failed or cancelled attempt leaves no record of its preset. The Specification narrows the half
  accordingly. The "downloaded" half is untouched by this decision (OD-22).
- **AC-44.** "Running estimated cost" becomes the sum of per-response costs the client received, i.e. successful generations only.
  "Image-studio usage rows ... retrievable through `GET /api/usage`" remains true for successful rows. The Specification states that the
  figure under-reports real spend that Google may charge for blocked, cancelled or failed calls, and that it is not derived from
  `GET /api/usage`.
- **Retries (AC-37).** Upstream attempts that fail inside the BFF's backoff loop write no row; the successful attempt writes exactly
  one. The client-driven 410 re-upload retry (OD-7) likewise produces one row, for the attempt that succeeds.

**Conditions the Specification MUST carry.**
- C-4.1 The AC-43, AC-19 and AC-44 amendments above.
- C-4.2 Prices are configuration in `server/usage/pricing.js`, keyed by the **exact catalog model ID** from OD-1 (one spelling
  everywhere), with a comment naming the source and date as the existing entries do. The $0.03 / $0.06 figures are placeholders until
  the owner confirms them (V-5). The price is per image and independent of resolution; if the owner wants per-resolution prices that
  is a new input the human must supply.
- C-4.3 `cost = unit price x number of images in the response`. A response with zero images has cost 0. Whether a **HTTP 200 with no
  image** (AC-39 text-only) writes a row is not decided by "only successful generations (HTTP 200)": that is an open sub-question
  recorded under OD-19; the Specification must not silently pick it.
- C-4.4 `computeCost` must not apply the token path or `FALLBACK_PRICE` to an image model. The Specification names the single source
  of "is this an image model" (the only registry the decisions create is the catalog `modalities` field from OD-1; a second
  hard-coded list would duplicate it) and requires a test that every catalog image model resolves to its per-image price, and that an
  image model with no price entry fails loudly rather than falling back. Token counts are still stored (AC-43) but cost for these
  rows is not tokens x rate.
- C-4.5 The response cost field name is the Specification's to fix (`cost_usd` was an example; the existing API uses camelCase, e.g.
  `costUsd`, `taskLabel`). It is part of the Zod response schema (AC-14) and the client accepts only a finite, non-negative number.
- C-4.6 The session total is persisted with the session in IndexedDB and is **not authoritative**: it can differ from the sum of
  `usage_log` rows (for example when the BFF finished and logged a request whose response the client never received because of
  Cancel or a dropped connection). What "New session" and "clear history" do to the total is OD-16(e).
- C-4.7 The `task_label` format (`image-studio|white-bg|2K` was an example) is fixed by the Specification. Carried caveats from v2:
  `queryUsage` filters `task_label` by exact match, so a prefix cannot be queried; the dashboard (`dashboard.component.ts`) will show
  one bucket per preset x resolution (up to about 5 x 3 labels), a visible change to an existing view. The optional stable marker in
  the existing `mode` column was not addressed by the human and stays an open, non-blocking sub-question.

**v4 (human acceptance).** "Billing only for HTTP 200; prices bound to the exact verified model ID." This is C-4.1 and C-4.2 as
written. Reinforcement: the price key is the model ID verified in V-3, so if V-3 changes a catalog ID the pricing key changes with
it and the per-model price test (C-4.4) fails until it does. The v4 OD-23 and OD-24 decisions do not change billing: the 413 of
OD-24 is not a 200 and writes no row.

**Blocking: false** (decided; V-5 is a vendor-fact check, not a missing decision).

## OD-5 - Timeout, retry and Cancel precedence (Story Q5) - RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS (v7)

### v7 resolution (human decision)

*The text below this v7 block is the earlier record of the question, kept for history. Where it says OPEN or `Blocking: false` as a pending state, this v7 block supersedes it.*

**Human decision (v7, verbatim; `/so:approve` comment of 2026-11-01).** "OD-5 (Таймаут і пріоритет Cancel): Приймаю PD-5 (один загальний дедлайн 120 с на весь процес включно з backoff; Cancel перериває запит на будь-якому етапі). Клієнтський retryTransport не повинен чіпати виклики генерації (вони платні, покладаємося лише на логіку BFF 1/2/4s)." (English rendering: I accept PD-5 (one overall 120 s deadline for the whole process including backoff; Cancel aborts the request at any stage). The client `retryTransport` must not touch generation calls (they are paid; we rely only on the BFF 1/2/4 s logic).)

**Verification.** PD-5 is the "Proposal (not a decision)" the v5 entry recorded (one overall deadline, Cancel valid at every moment including backoff, abort propagated from client disconnect), so the human accepted it unchanged. `retryTransport` exists in `src/services/http-retry.ts` and is applied inside `LlmService.post()` (`src/services/llm.service.ts` lines 24-35), which that file calls "the one choke point"; an image method routed through `post()` would inherit the retry, so the condition below is a real constraint on the implementation, not a formality. `retryTransport` retries status 0 and bare 500/502/503/504 with no `{ error: string }` envelope and is documented as not idempotent; a generation is a paid call, which is the human's reason.

**Conditions the Specification MUST carry** (each mirrors the Specification v4 requirement named).
- **C-5.1** (FR-22, SD-30) One deadline of 120 000 ms from the moment the operator activates Send or Retry, covering that whole user action: the `countTokens` calls, any upload made after that moment, the FR-17 re-upload and repeat, every BFF backoff wait and the Gemini call(s). **Default (awaiting confirmation, not a decision):** the anchor and the reach (SD-30) are the Specification's derivation of "the whole process"; the human gave neither.
- **C-5.2** (FR-21) Cancel is valid at every stage including a backoff wait. A Cancel is a failed turn (OD-19) and its content returns to the composer (D-5).
- **C-5.3** (FR-22, SD-30) The deadline is measured on the client: at 120 000 ms the client aborts, and the BFF aborts the upstream call when the client disconnects. No second, BFF-side timer is required.
- **C-5.4** (FR-85) `retryTransport` never applies to a generate call: the only automatic retry is the BFF loop of FR-60 (1, 2, 4 s). A test with a stubbed server answering status 0, bare 500, 502, 503 and 504 asserts exactly one generate request for each. This closes the part of OD-29 C-29.7 that asked whether `retryTransport` applies to a paid generate call.
- **C-5.5** (SD-31, CF-5) **Reading awaiting confirmation:** a generate request that ends with a transport failure or a bare 502/503/504 is a failed turn shown with T-7 and a manual Retry (a new counted request). `retryTransport` is unchanged for the upload call.
- **C-5.6** (CF-6) **Reading awaiting confirmation:** an answer that reaches the BFF after the client aborted at 120 s is a counted request (it reached Gemini), the client discards it, and no row can be matched to the client total (FR-71 already accepts divergence).
- **C-5.7** Story amendments: AC-11 (Cancel at every stage), AC-12 (the 120 s covers the whole action, not one attempt), AC-37 (no client retry of generate); see the cumulative overrides table.

**Blocking: false** (decided).

**v6 cross-note (OD-29, OD-32, OD-13, OD-19).** New inputs to the precedence rule, none of which settles it: (1) the retry cycle is now 3 retries at 1 / 2 / 4 s on 429 and 5xx for the generate call **and** for `countTokens` and the uploads (OD-29), a non-retryable error ends the action at once; (2) one generation makes k + 1 concurrent `countTokens` calls (OD-32); (3) a Cancel is a failed turn that counts toward the rate limit if the Gemini call had started (OD-19, OD-13); (4) the OD-13 count is taken immediately before the Gemini call, so a Cancel during `countTokens` is not counted. OD-5 stays **OPEN and non-blocking** (the Specification keeps its labelled PROPOSED DEFAULT, FR-21); the decision of whether `retryTransport` applies to a paid generate call is also still here (OD-29 C-29.7).


**Question.** Does the 120 s timeout (AC-12) cover the whole request including the three backoff retries (AC-37), or each upstream
attempt? What does Cancel (AC-11) do during a backoff wait, and does closing the browser connection also abort the upstream call? Is a
client-side timeout or a BFF-side timeout the one that fires?

**Why it cannot be inferred.** Checked AC-11, AC-12, AC-37: each states its rule independently. Checked `server/utils/retry.js` and
`server/utils/timeouts.js`: `withRetry` and the per-mode timeouts are written for the text providers; the Gemini provider's comment shows
the SDK's own retry loop is deliberately dormant, so image retries would be new code with their own interaction rules.

**v3 cross-note.** The OD-7 decision adds an automatic client-side re-upload and retry after a 410, and a `countTokens` call before
each generation; both sit inside or beside the 120 s deadline and must be placed in the precedence rule. Also: the client's
`retryTransport` (`src/services/http-retry.ts`) retries status 0 / bare 500 / 502 / 503 / 504 that carry no `{ error: string }`
envelope, and is documented as not idempotent; whether it applies to image generations (each is a paid call) is part of this entry.

**Impact if unresolved.** Worst-case latency is undefined (120 s versus 4 upstream calls plus about 7 s of backoff); test authors would
invent the precedence and a test could pass for the wrong reason.

**v5 cross-note.** The deadline must also say where the following sit, none of which is defined: the number of `countTokens` calls
before a generation (OD-32), the first-upload calls (one per image, SD-7), and the failure classes of OD-29 (a non-retryable error ends
the action at once; an exhausted retry ends it after the last backoff).

**Blocking: false.** Why it stays non-blocking in v5: the Specification carries a labelled PROPOSED DEFAULT (FR-21) taken from the
proposal below and the precedence is a latency/cancellation rule on a single criterion, not a contract other requirements are built
on. Proposal (not a decision): one overall deadline of 120 s per user-visible generation that includes backoff, Cancel
valid at every moment including backoff, abort propagated from client disconnect.

## OD-6 - Definition-of-Done items from the source note (Story Q6) - RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS (v7)

### v7 resolution (human decision)

*The text below this v7 block is the earlier record of the question, kept for history; this v7 block supersedes its pending state.*

**Human decision (v7, verbatim).** "OD-6 (Ручні DoD-перевірки): Це виключно ручні перевірки (QA) після розгортання, вони не є автоматичними воротами CI/CD і не вимагають механічних артефактів для проходження пайплайну." (English rendering: These are exclusively manual checks (QA) after deployment; they are not automatic CI/CD gates and need no mechanical artifacts to pass the pipeline.)

**Verification.** Consistent with the Story's own `Out of scope` line (E2E on 10 products, keyboard and NVDA verification, first-week spend report are "human acceptance activities, not testable acceptance criteria") and with AGENTS.md section 6 (the mechanical gate is a closed list of commands that contains none of them). The Story's Q6 also lists "at least 80% Vitest coverage" among the source note's items; that one is **not** a manual check: AGENTS.md section 6 item 3 and section 5 define the coverage gate and its scope (logic runner only, `src/utils`, `src/prompt-core`, `src/render`, `src/domain`; global floor 80/80/75/80).

**Conditions the Specification MUST carry.**
- **C-6.1** (NFR-11, Out of scope) The E2E scenario, the keyboard and NVDA verification and the first-week spend report are manual QA after deployment; none is a gate; no mechanical artifact or evidence record is required; the reconciliation stage never blocks on missing manual evidence.
- **C-6.2** (CF-7, NFR-11, NFR-13) **Reading awaiting confirmation:** the human's sentence is about the manual checks. The 80% coverage item is not read as one of them, so the AGENTS.md section 5 / section 6 item 3 gate applies unchanged with its scope; the new file `src/prompt-core/prompt-presets.ts` is inside that scope (OD-15 C-15.4).
- **C-6.3** This decision does not relax the pre-implementation checks V-1 to V-7: those are vendor facts recorded in a human-filled file as a precondition of IMPLEMENTATION (OD-33), a different thing from the Definition-of-Done manual checks. The AC-20 manual check (OD-20) likewise needs no evidence record.

**Blocking: false** (decided).

**v6 cross-note.** Unchanged and still OPEN, non-blocking. The human's OD-33 answer puts the pre-implementation check results in `docs/evidence/US-10.1-pre-implementation-checks.md`; whether any manual acceptance evidence (E2E, NVDA, spend report) needs a record is still this entry's question.


**Question.** Are the source note's items (at least 80% Vitest coverage, E2E on 10 real catalog products, keyboard and NVDA check,
first-week spend report) delivery gates for this Story, or human-acceptance activities outside the pipeline?

**Why it cannot be inferred.** Checked AGENTS.md section 6 and section 5: the mechanical gate and the coverage floors are defined there
(global floor 80/80/75/80 over the logic-runner include list; `src/app/**` and `src/services/**` are not in the coverage scope), so the
note's "80%" has no unambiguous scope. The Story's `Out of scope` already lists the E2E / NVDA / spend-report items as human acceptance
activities and points to this question.

**Impact if unresolved.** RECONCILIATION cannot tell whether missing manual evidence blocks the Story; "80%" is a number without a scope
(AGENTS.md section 5: "a percentage quoted without its scope is theater").

**Blocking: false.** The Story's current wording (out of scope, human activities) is consistent with AGENTS.md; this needs a one-line
confirmation and a decision on whether any evidence record is required.

## OD-7 - Trimming vs `thoughtSignature` fidelity; Files API expiry (Story Q7) - RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS

**v6 cross-note (OD-19, OD-27, OD-29, OD-30).** (1) The pair-unit contract is now exact: the history is always `U1 M1 ... Uk Mk` (OD-19), so no turn sits after the latest model turn; the first-pair and latest-pair boundary questions of C-7.4 remain the Specification's to state and flag. (2) A model turn with several images (OD-30) is trimmed or kept whole. (3) The 410 must carry its own closed code and an upstream 410 that is not the file-missing class must not be read as it (OD-29 C-29.2); the automatic re-send after a 410 is a separate client request that counts again toward the rate limit (OD-13 C-13.2). (4) Originals count in the byte measure only as their `file_uri` reference (OD-27 B).


**Question.** (a) May whole turns be dropped (AC-24, AC-29) without invalidating the signature chain that AC-21 says must be resent
unmodified, and which turns are droppable? (b) AC-24 says "oldest intermediate turns", AC-29 "oldest intermediate model versions":
same set, and in which order do the two rules apply? (c) How does the BFF learn a Files API file has expired, and how does the client
supply the bytes for the re-upload (the BFF holds no session store)?

**Human decision (v3, verbatim).** "OD-7: Припущення про розриви в історії приймаю як припущення; до IMPLEMENTATION розробник
перевіряє на живому API. Fallback: якщо Gemini не толерує точкове видалення проміжних ходів — згортаємо всю стару історію, лишаючи
лише найперше повідомлення користувача (з оригіналами) та останню згенеровану версію. Порядок обрізання: спочатку ліміт розміру
(18 MB), потім ліміт токенів (80%). Завжди недоторканними: оригінальне завантаження (перший хід користувача) та остання актуальна
версія моделі. Files API (AC-28): підтверджую зміну — клієнт ловить 410 Gone, фоново перезавантажує файли на
/api/image-studio/upload і повторює запит. Протермінований файл: якщо Gemini повертає 404 Not Found або тіло помилки вказує на
неіснуючий file_uri, BFF перехоплює це і повертає клієнту HTTP 410 Gone." (English rendering: I accept the gap assumption as an
assumption; before IMPLEMENTATION the developer verifies it on the live API. Fallback: if Gemini does not tolerate removing
intermediate turns point-wise, collapse all old history keeping only the very first user message (with the originals) and the last
generated version. Trim order: first the size limit (18 MB), then the token limit (80%). Always untouched: the original upload (the
first user turn) and the latest current model version. Files API (AC-28): I confirm the change - the client catches 410 Gone,
re-uploads the files in the background to `/api/image-studio/upload` and repeats the request. Expired file: if Gemini returns 404
Not Found or the error body points to a nonexistent `file_uri`, the BFF intercepts it and returns HTTP 410 Gone to the client.) The
v2 text, which this builds on, set the primary strategy: remove old intermediate turns as a user+model pair; remaining turns are
sent with the original `thoughtSignature` unchanged.

**Verification.**
- **Accepted as an assumption, not a verified fact.** v2 found no source for "Gemini models support such gaps" (repo, Story,
  AGENTS.md, and the installed `@google/genai` typings, which describe `Part.thoughtSignature` only as "an opaque signature for the
  thought so it can be reused in subsequent requests"). The human now accepts it explicitly as an assumption with a stated fallback
  and defers the live-API check to the developer before IMPLEMENTATION. This is the "accepted assumption with a stated fallback"
  the resolution rules allow: **V-1**.
- **AC-28 amendment is consistent with the repo.** `server/` holds no session store, so "the BFF re-uploads from the session copy" was
  not implementable; a client that holds the originals in IndexedDB (AC-25) and re-sends them is. Recorded override: AC-28's re-upload
  is client-driven via 410 and `/api/image-studio/upload`; the BFF keeps no bytes.
- **The recognition rule is the weak point (caller's item (b)).** "404 Not Found **or** the error body points to a nonexistent
  `file_uri`" makes every upstream 404 a 410. A Gemini 404 also answers a wrong or unavailable **model id** - and OD-1's IDs are
  human-supplied and unverified. Mapped to 410, a wrong model id would make the client re-upload every original and repeat the
  request, indefinitely if unbounded. Which status/body Google returns for an expired or missing file is not documented anywhere in the
  repo (it may not even be 404): **V-2**.
- **The two trim rules.** Size first then tokens is workable (it also keeps the `countTokens` payload small). Both remove "oldest
  intermediate first", so the second rule continues the first and a single request can trigger both. The Story's AC-24 and AC-29 each
  require a user-visible message; one request can therefore owe two messages (OD-21).
- **Fallback vs AC-22 "Edit this version" (caller's item (b)).** Collapsing to {first user turn, latest version} does not mechanically
  break "Edit this version" of an older vK: AC-22 attaches vK as an input image of the **new user turn**, which is outside the
  trimmed history, so vK's bytes are still sent. What the collapse loses is the intermediate context and the older model turns'
  signatures, which is what trimming is meant to do. The semantic caveat is that the kept "latest version" is the chronologically last
  version in the history, not the version being edited, so the model sees the latest output and an attached older image together; that
  ambiguity exists with or without trimming. The pair rule has two boundary gaps the human did not state: the first pair (the user turn
  is protected, is the model's reply droppable?) and the latest pair (the model turn is protected, is the user turn that produced it
  droppable?). Removing one half could leave two adjacent turns of the same role; whether the API accepts that is part of V-1.
- **The 30 MB cap collides with in-BFF trimming over repeated turns** - promoted to **OD-23** (blocking), not resolved here.

**Conditions the Specification MUST carry.**
- C-7.1 **Assumption and fallback.** The Specification states the pair-removal strategy as the primary design, the
  first-turn-plus-latest collapse as the human's stated fallback, and the selector: the V-1 result recorded in an evidence artifact
  before IMPLEMENTATION selects exactly one. The Specification defines what each branch does; tests cover the selected branch with a
  stubbed Gemini (no live API in the gate; AGENTS.md section 5).
- C-7.2 **Narrow 404-to-410.** The Specification MUST NOT define 410 as "any Gemini 404". The recognition rule is a conjunction: the
  request carried at least one `file_uri`, the upstream error is of the class V-2 establishes for a missing file, and the error
  identifies a file resource (not a model). A model-not-found error is a distinct error, is never mapped to 410, and never triggers a
  re-upload. If V-2 shows Google uses a status other than 404 for expired files, the rule follows V-2, not the human's example.
- C-7.3 **Bounded retry.** Automatic re-upload plus retry is bounded per user action (the number is the Specification's to fix; a
  second 410 after a re-upload is surfaced as an error, never retried again). The retry is accounted for in the OD-5 deadline and the
  OD-13 count; it writes at most one usage row (OD-4).
- C-7.4 **Protected set and boundaries.** Always kept: the first user turn (originals) and the latest model version. The
  Specification defines "latest version" as the chronologically last accepted model image in the history being sent (not the version
  being edited under AC-22), states for the first pair and the latest pair which halves remain and what role sequence results, and
  flags this for the human at HUMAN_SPEC_APPROVAL. The new user turn (text plus any AC-22 attachment) is outside trimming and counts
  toward the size.
- C-7.5 **Order.** Size limit (18 MB) first, then token limit (80% of the model's input limit, via `countTokens`). The Specification
  states how many user notices result (OD-21), and whether the `countTokens` call counts toward the rate limit (OD-13) and the timeout
  (OD-5).
- C-7.6 **AC-28 amendment and the upload endpoint.** `/api/image-studio/upload` is part of the `/api/image-studio/*` surface: the 30 MB
  cap and the 411 rule (OD-11) apply, AC-2 (magic bytes) and AC-5 (EXIF) must say whether they apply (OD-12 cross-note), and the
  Specification defines the **first-upload** path, because the human defined only the re-upload (the same endpoint is the minimal
  reading, to be confirmed at spec approval). Fact for the Specification: the BFF registers only `express.json` and
  `express.urlencoded`, and `package.json` has no multipart parser, so a new parser would be a new dependency (a human decision,
  AGENTS.md section 1); the existing `/api/llm/vision` route sends base64 in JSON. After a re-upload the client replaces the stored
  `file_uri`s; AC-21 "unmodified" is read as "unmodified except `file_uri` after expiry".
- C-7.7 OD-23 and OD-24 are now resolved with conditions (v4): the BFF reports the trimmed turn ids on every HTTP 200 and the
  client deletes them atomically (OD-23); a protected remainder over the limits ends in a 413 with a body code (OD-24). The trimming
  design in this entry is read together with those two entries.
- C-7.8 **v4 recognizer tightening (human).** The human accepted the narrowed 410 rule: the BFF converts to 410 **only** if the Gemini
  response itself directly indicates a missing file (for example the body contains `file_uri` or the Files API). It is read together
  with C-7.2: the conjunction also requires that the request carried at least one `file_uri` and that the exact status/body class is
  the one V-2 establishes. A Gemini 404 that does not name a file (a wrong model id) passes through as the upstream error: no 410, no
  re-upload, no retry. A substring match on prose is fragile, so the Specification pins the recognizer to the V-2 result and tests
  both a file-missing fixture and a model-not-found fixture; a "Files API" mention in an unrelated error (for example a 400) is not a
  410.

**Blocking: false** for OD-7 itself (accepted assumption, stated fallback, conditions above). The trimming design is completed by
OD-23 (resolved with conditions in v4).

## OD-8 - Under 2048 px but over 5 MB (Story Q8) - RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS (v6)

### v6 resolution (human decision, conditions)

*The text below this v6 block is the v5 record of the question, kept for history. Where it says OPEN or `Blocking: true`, this v6 block supersedes it.*

**Human decision (v6, verbatim).** "OD-8: Відхилити (файл ≤2048 px і >5 MB); клієнт показує помилку розміру; не перекодовувати мовчки." (English rendering: Reject (a file of 2048 px or less and over 5 MB); the client shows a size error; do not silently re-encode.) The human is the decider; this stage chose nothing.

**Verification.** Consistent with AC-7 (a rejected file shows its reason; the other attached files remain) and with AC-4 (a larger image is downscaled). The human's rule covers the branch AC-4 left open; it does not say where the 5 MB is measured for an image above 2048 px.

**Conditions the Specification MUST carry.**
- C-8.1 A file whose long side is at most 2048 px and whose size exceeds 5 MB is rejected on the client with the reason "size" (AC-7); the other attached files stay attached. It is never re-encoded to fit.
- C-8.2 **Flagged default (not a decision):** a file above 2048 px is downscaled per AC-4 and the result is then checked as it will be sent; a result over 5 MB is rejected by the same rule, so no upload over 5 MB is ever sent. The alternative (accept a downscaled result over 5 MB) is the other reading; the human's text does not settle it.
- C-8.3 The error text is Ukrainian and is fixed by the Specification (no literal supplied; OD-14 for the English UI). Enforcement is on the client as the human stated; whether the BFF also checks the size of `/api/image-studio/upload` is OD-12(b) (open), so a direct API call can bypass the client rule (recorded).
- C-8.4 **Cross-note to OD-12.** The human's "do not silently re-encode" concerns size-driven re-encoding. AC-5 (strip EXIF from every image) normally needs a decode and re-encode on the client; that mechanism, its output format and its location stay OD-12 (open) and must not be used to shrink an oversize file.

**Blocking: false** (decided).


**Question.** AC-4 downscales when the long side is over 2048 px OR the size is over 5 MB, to a long side of 2048 px. For an image of
2048 px or less that is over 5 MB, resizing cannot reduce it: re-encode (format, quality), reject, or accept?

**Why it cannot be inferred.** Checked AC-4 and AC-7 as written: the branch is not covered, and AC-7 only lists the rejection reasons
"format / size / count" without a limit for size.

**Impact if unresolved.** A falsifiable test for the over-5 MB branch cannot be written. See OD-12 for the connected encode question.

**v5 reassessment (SPEC_REVIEW finding "AC-4 at most 2048 px and over 5 MB").** Confirmed as a genuine decision with no default in the
log; the Specification (FR-11) states no requirement for the branch, and AC-7 lists "size" as a rejection reason without a limit
value. Two further facts the owner needs when choosing: (1) AC-4's "5 MB" is not defined as the file size before or after downscaling
(an image above 2048 px is downscaled and the result may itself be over 5 MB); (2) whether AC-4 applies to a version attached by
"Edit this version" is OD-28.

**Blocking: false.** Why it stays non-blocking in v5: it leaves one narrow branch of AC-4 / AC-7 open; no other requirement is shaped
by it (the Files API upload takes whatever the client sends, one image per request). Re-encode versus reject is the whole question.
Proposal (not a decision): none recorded; the owner must choose between re-encode and reject.

## OD-9 - "Checked automatically" for AC-41 (Story Q9) - RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS

**Question.** What mechanism satisfies "the Gemini key does not appear in the client bundle, and this is checked automatically"?

**Human decision (v3, verbatim).** "OD-9: "Автоматично" = тест, що запускається `npm test` (всередині Vitest). Свіжість dist/: тест
security.spec.ts у beforeAll синхронно запускає збірку клієнта (child_process.execSync('npm run build')) у тимчасову директорію
`.test-dist` і перевіряє саме її — без зміни глобального порядку воріт. Тест Regex-ом шукає і назву змінної "GEMINI_API_KEY", і
шаблон гуглівського ключа ("AIzaSy...") у згенерованих .js файлах бандлу." (English rendering: "Automatically" = a test run by
`npm test` (inside Vitest). Freshness of `dist/`: `security.spec.ts` in `beforeAll` synchronously runs the client build
(`child_process.execSync('npm run build')`) into a temporary directory `.test-dist` and checks that one, without changing the global
gate order. The test uses a regex to look for both the variable name "GEMINI_API_KEY" and the Google key pattern ("AIzaSy...") in the
bundle's generated .js files.) This replaces v2's claim that Vitest runs "before every deploy via a hook", which was false (no hooks,
no husky, no CI, no `pre*` script); the v3 text no longer relies on it.

**Verification (the caller's item (c), each checked here).**
- **Can the build target another output path?** Yes, but not with the command as written. `package.json` `build` is `ng build`;
  `angular.json` fixes `outputPath: { base: "./dist", browser: "." }`. `execSync('npm run build')` therefore writes to `dist/`, not
  `.test-dist`. Run in this review: `npm run build -- --output-path=.test-dist` succeeded (builder reported 12.3 s, wall time 18.6 s
  including `npm`), printed "Output location: ...\.test-dist", and the files landed in **`.test-dist/browser/`** (the string form
  of the option reintroduces the `browser` subfolder), so the scan must recurse. The production configuration (the default) was
  used, i.e. the same bundle as the real build. The temporary `.test-dist` created for this check was deleted.
- **Timeout.** The measured 12-19 s exceeds Vitest's default 10 s hook timeout (`vitest.config.ts` sets neither `hookTimeout` nor
  `testTimeout`), so `beforeAll` needs an explicit, generous timeout or the test fails for the wrong reason.
- **Gate order and AGENTS.md section 6 / 7.8.** The human's assertion is right: a new spec under `test/` is picked up by the existing
  `include: ['src/**/*.spec.ts', 'test/**/*.spec.ts']`, so no change to section 6's order, no hook, no CI edit, no `package.json`
  script edit, no `vitest.config.ts` edit is needed. The check runs inside `npm test` and `npm run test:coverage`, so the gate builds
  the client about three times (`npm test`, `npm run test:coverage`, `npm run build`). That is a recorded tension with AGENTS.md
  section 5 ("it must stay green and stay fast"), not a violation; no `sleep` or network is involved (section 5 "Determinism").
- **`.gitignore`.** `git check-ignore -v .test-dist/browser/main.js` returns nothing: the `dist` entry does not match `.test-dist`
  (confirmed; `dist/main.js` is matched by `.gitignore:11`). A leftover `.test-dist` would show up as untracked. `.test-dist/` must
  be added to `.gitignore` and the directory removed after the run.
- **Baseline.** A fresh production bundle has zero matches for `GEMINI_API_KEY` and for `AIza[0-9A-Za-z_-]{20,}` (checked), and
  `src/` has no `GEMINI_API_KEY` reference. So the test can go RED only by planting a canary, not from today's code.
- **What the test can actually assert.** The key **value** lives in the git-ignored `.env`, is not available in the gate environment or
  to a committed spec, and therefore cannot be compared. The test can assert: (1) the literal name `GEMINI_API_KEY` is absent from the
