  built files; (2) no string matching the Google-key shape (the human's prefix "AIzaSy") is present. It cannot assert "the value is
  absent". AC-41's "does not appear" is therefore verified as "no key name and no key-shaped string", and the Specification must
  say so rather than claim the stronger statement. The existing `arch-guard.sh` Rule #4 still covers `src/*.ts` only.

**Conditions the Specification MUST carry.**
- C-9.1 The build command passes the output path through npm: `npm run build -- --output-path=.test-dist` (or an equivalent that
  builds the same production configuration); the literal `execSync('npm run build')` is not sufficient. The scan root is
  `.test-dist/browser/` and is recursive.
- C-9.2 Non-vacuous: the test fails if the build fails (an `execSync` throw fails `beforeAll`), fails if the scan finds zero `.js`
  files, and never skips when the output is missing. The scanner is also exercised against a synthetic string containing the name and
  a key-shaped string, so the check is proven able to fail (AGENTS.md section 5, section 7.7).
- C-9.3 `beforeAll` has an explicit timeout well above the measured 12-19 s; the Specification fixes the value. `execSync` output is
  captured or discarded in a way that cannot overflow its buffer.
- C-9.4 `.test-dist/` is added to `.gitignore` and removed in `afterAll` (the Specification lists the `.gitignore` edit as a file
  change for the human to see at approval).
- C-9.5 The regex covers both `GEMINI_API_KEY` and the Google-key shape. The exact quantifier after "AIzaSy" is the Specification's
  to fix; it must not be loose enough to match ordinary library text (baseline above has no `AIza...` hit at 20+ characters).
- C-9.6 No client string may contain the literal `GEMINI_API_KEY` (OD-3 note N-3.2), or the test fails on legitimate UI text.
- C-9.7 The spec file lives under `test/` and is a logic-runner spec, not `*.component.spec.ts`. The human wrote
  `security.spec.ts` in this answer (v2 said "for example `test/security.spec.ts`"); the path `test/security.spec.ts` is taken from it.
- C-9.8 AC-41 is restated as: "the check runs inside `npm test` locally; there is no deploy-time hook and no CI". The Specification
  does not claim a deploy guard.

**v4 (human acceptance).** "The key-leak test builds the bundle into the isolated `.test-dist` (`--output-path=.test-dist`), added to
`.gitignore`, with a higher timeout and mandatory cleanup afterwards." This is C-9.1, C-9.3 and C-9.4 as written (the `npm run build
-- --output-path=.test-dist` form, an explicit `beforeAll` timeout, `.gitignore` entry, `afterAll` removal). No change.

**Blocking: false** (decided).

## OD-10 - `/image-studio` route vs a router-less app - RESOLVED

**Question.** The Story requires "its own route `/image-studio`". The app today has no router. Is the new mode (a) a real Angular route,
which makes `@angular/router` a runtime dependency and needs hosting that serves the SPA on deep links, or (b) a new `AppMode` value
switched like the other modes?

**Human decision (v2, verbatim).** "OD-10: Відмовитися від буквального роутера (URL /image-studio). Додати 'image-studio' як нове
значення до AppMode в src/app/types.ts. Перемикання через існуючий механізм appMode(). Для доступу до режиму не вимагатиметься вибір
магазину (Store) — логіка пропускає перевірку STORE_REGISTRY для цього режиму." (English rendering: Drop the literal router (URL
`/image-studio`). Add `'image-studio'` as a new value of `AppMode` in `src/app/types.ts`. Switching goes through the existing
`appMode()` mechanism. Access to the mode will not require choosing a store (Store) - the logic skips the `STORE_REGISTRY` check for
this mode.)

**Verification.**
- `src/app/types.ts` defines `export type AppMode = 'generator' | 'ua-generator' | ... | 'faq-generator'` (a string union); a new
  member is a one-line change to a non-FROZEN file. `app.component.ts` holds `appMode = signal<AppMode>('generator')` and
  `setMode(mode)`; `app.component.html` switches views with `@else if (appMode() === '...')`. The mechanism exists as described.
- No router is used (AGENTS.md section 2: "the app has no router"; `@angular/router` is a test-only pinned peer; no `provideRouter` /
  `RouterOutlet` in `src/`). This decision keeps it that way: no new dependency, no hosting or SPA-fallback change, no AGENTS.md
  section 1 trigger. Consistent with the Story's Scope ("Not store-specific. The mode takes no store input").
- **Wording correction.** There is no central store gate to "skip": `setMode` has none. The store requirement lives in each mode's own
  validity (`isFormValid` requires `selectedWebsite() !== null`, `isCopywriterValid`, the SEO form, and so on). The new mode simply has
  no website requirement; nothing is bypassed and `STORE_REGISTRY` is neither read nor changed.

**Consequences recorded for the Specification (facts from the checks, not new questions).**
- `const TOOL_LABEL: Record<AppMode, string>` in `app.component.ts` is exhaustive over `AppMode`, so a new member is a compile error until
  it gets an entry; `src/app/app.component.template-wiring.spec.ts` pins `TOOL_LABEL` for the FAQ mode and is the pattern for this one.
  The `clearAll()` switch on `appMode()` also needs a case (or an explicit decision that it has none).
- **Side note, non-blocking:** `appMode` is not persisted (initial `'generator'`; no storage key for it). After a page reload the
  session survives (AC-25, IndexedDB) but the user lands on the Generator and must switch back to the studio. If this is not wanted it
  is a new requirement, not part of this decision.
- There is no URL, deep link or browser-history entry for the studio.

**Overrides:** Story Context ("its own route `/image-studio`") and Scope Surface ("New route `/image-studio`") are replaced by "a new
`AppMode` value `'image-studio'`". No acceptance criterion names the route.

**Blocking: false** (decided).

## OD-11 - 30 MB route-level limit under a 50 MB global parser; missing `Content-Length` (AC-30) - RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS

**v6 cross-note (OD-13, OD-29).** The 411 and 413 rejections of this entry are not counted by the rate limit (OD-13(d)); they are BFF-reserved statuses with closed codes (OD-29 C-29.1). The limiter's 429 is mounted after `cors()` like the pre-parser, and the pre-parser runs before the limiter in the request order.


**Question.** How is a 30 MB limit applied to `/api/image-studio/*` only, "global `express.json` limit unchanged", when the global
parser is mounted for every route? What happens to a request with no `Content-Length`? How do the thresholds 30 MB (route body,
AC-30), 18 MB (request-size trim, AC-29) and 20 MB (inline cap, Story Context) relate, and are responses bounded?

**Human decisions.** v2 (verbatim): "Бонус OD-11: BFF перевіряє заголовок Content-Length малою middleware для /api/image-studio/*
перед парсером і відхиляє > 30MB." (English: the BFF checks the `Content-Length` header with a small middleware for
`/api/image-studio/*` before the parser and rejects anything over 30 MB.) v3 (verbatim): "OD-11: Якщо Content-Length відсутній
(chunked), BFF для /api/image-studio/* жорстко повертає 411 Length Required." (English: if `Content-Length` is absent (chunked), the
BFF returns 411 Length Required for `/api/image-studio/*`.)

**Verification.**
- Mechanism confirmed: `server/index.js` lines 35-36 register `express.json({ limit: '50mb' })` and `express.urlencoded(...)` globally
  before any route, so a later route-level parser cannot lower the limit. A middleware on `/api/image-studio` mounted before line 35
  satisfies AC-30's "global limit unchanged". The 411 closes v2's chunked-request bypass.
- **Browser consistency (caller's item (d)).** A browser `fetch()`/`HttpClient` POST whose body is a string (the app's JSON posts),
  `Blob`, `FormData`, `ArrayBuffer` or `URLSearchParams` has a known length and the browser sends `Content-Length`; only a streamed
  `ReadableStream` body (`duplex: 'half'`) is unsized. The app's existing clients post JSON strings, so a normal client is consistent
  with the 411 rule. This is web-platform behaviour, not something the repo tests; whether Railway's proxy preserves the header
  (rather than re-chunking) is not knowable offline: **V-4**.
- **Scope of "/api/image-studio/*".** A GET/HEAD/OPTIONS request has no body and no `Content-Length`. A 411 on every request to the
  prefix would break any body-less route (and a CORS preflight, although `cors()` answers OPTIONS before the new middleware if it is
  mounted after it).
- **Response path.** An error produced before `cors()` runs would carry no CORS headers; the browser then reports status 0, and the
  client's `isUpstreamTransportFailure` (`src/services/http-retry.ts`) treats status 0 / bare 5xx without the `{ error: string }`
  envelope as "nothing answered" and retries, which for a paid image call means paying again.
- **18 / 20 / 30 MB.** They measure different things: 18 MB is the request the BFF assembles for Gemini (kept below the 20 MB inline
  cap), 30 MB is the incoming client-to-BFF body (JSON, base64 inflates binary by about 4/3). The human's answers do not say how the
  inbound cap and the BFF-side trim cooperate over repeated turns; that is the finding promoted to **OD-23**.
- A lying `Content-Length` (smaller than the real body) is still bounded by the parser's own 50 MB cap (unchanged).

**Conditions the Specification MUST carry.**
- C-11.1 The middleware is mounted **after `cors()` (line 34) and before `express.json` (line 35)**, so rejections carry CORS headers
  and the browser can read them.
- C-11.2 Rejections use the existing envelope `{ error: string }` (as `sendError` does) and a status the Specification fixes. The
  human gave no code for "> 30 MB"; 413 is the standard one and is what `express.json` itself would raise, so it is the expected value,
  confirmed at spec approval. The 411 is the human's.
- C-11.3 The 411 rule applies to **body-bearing requests** (for example POST/PUT/PATCH) to `/api/image-studio/*`, including
  `/api/image-studio/upload`; body-less methods are not rejected.
- C-11.4 The limit is stated in bytes with the same unit convention as the existing `'50mb'` (1024-based), not as "30 MB" alone.
- C-11.5 The tests: a 31 MB declared body is rejected on an image-studio route, the same body is accepted by an existing route (Rule
  5), a missing `Content-Length` yields 411, and the existing routes' behaviour is unchanged.
- C-11.6 **V-4** is a pre-implementation check; if Railway re-chunks request bodies, the 411 rule would reject legitimate requests
  and the human must be told before IMPLEMENTATION.

**Still open (non-blocking).** (c) Whether responses are bounded (a 4K image as base64 in JSON), which this middleware does not
address. Residual (b), the relation of the three thresholds across turns, was **OD-23** (resolved with conditions in v4: the
relation is the headroom arithmetic of OD-23 C-23.7).

**v4 (human acceptance).** "Content-Length middleware (411 on chunked) placed after `cors()` and before `express.json()`; response
`{ error: "..." }`." This is C-11.1, C-11.2 and the 411 rule as written. Added consequence of OD-24: both the over-30 MB rejection
and the OD-24 protected-history error are HTTP 413, so C-11.2 now also requires the over-30 MB rejection to carry its own distinct
code in `{ error: string }` (see OD-24 C-24.3); the client never decides on the status alone. The status for "> 30 MB" is still
the one the human did not state (413 is the expected value, confirmed at spec approval).

**Blocking: false** (mechanism and 411 decided; residual (b) was OD-23, now resolved with conditions).

## OD-12 - EXIF strip and re-encode mechanism (AC-2, AC-4, AC-5) - RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS (v7 for (d) and raw files; v8: (a) OD-39, (b) OD-40, (c) OD-38 are decided)

**v8 cross-note.** Every sub-question is now decided: (a) the native client canvas (OD-39), (b) the BFF checks magic bytes only and the bypass is accepted (OD-40), (c) the re-encode keeps the original format and an animated WEBP becomes its first frame (OD-38), (d) and the raw files as in the v7 block. The "reading awaiting confirmation" label of **C-12.4 (CF-19)** is confirmed with the register (OD-49) and the acceptance of SD-1 to SD-40 (SD-24 is among them). C-12.3 and C-12.5 gain the re-encode details of OD-38 / OD-39 (the gate proves the plumbing through an injected encoder, W-2; the real canvas property is check V-8).

### v7 resolution (human decision)

*Partial resolution. The text below this v7 block is the earlier record, kept for history. Sub-question (d) and the raw-file question are decided here; (a), (b) and (c) are **not** decided and are carried as their own entries: (a) = OD-39, (b) = OD-40, (c) = OD-38.*

**Human decision (v7, verbatim).** "OD-12 (EXIF і Magic bytes): На BFF перевіряються magic bytes вже очищеного/перекодованого файлу. Ми відправляємо та зберігаємо для повторних завантажень виключно очищені sent bytes, сирі файли не зберігаються (повністю підтверджую SD-24 та D-7)." (English rendering: The BFF checks the magic bytes of the already cleaned / re-encoded file. We send and keep for re-uploads only the cleaned sent bytes; raw files are not stored (I fully confirm SD-24 and D-7).)

**Verification.** AC-2 (Story) puts the magic-byte check on the BFF and says a `.png` file with non-PNG content is rejected; the human's decision moves the check to the bytes the BFF actually receives (the sent bytes), so that sentence of AC-2 is amended (C-12.4). `server/` has no image library and `package.json` lists none (a new one would be a human decision, OD-39). AC-1 offers button, drag and drop and paste; the Specification (FR-8) feeds all three into one attachment list and one validation, so the original sub-question "does a pasted image follow the same path" is answered by FR-8 and needs no decision. SD-24 (store and re-upload the sent bytes) and D-7 (the sent bytes are checked against 5 MB) are the Specification's own proposals from v3; the human has now confirmed both in full.

**Conditions the Specification MUST carry.**
- **C-12.1** (FR-9, SD-24) The BFF verifies the type from the leading bytes of the already cleaned or re-encoded bytes at every `/api/image-studio/*` endpoint that receives user image bytes, on the first upload and on the re-upload alike. Non-image bytes sent directly get 415 `UNSUPPORTED_IMAGE_TYPE`.
- **C-12.2** (Definitions "Sent bytes", FR-16, FR-17, SD-24) Only the sent bytes (after downscale and after the EXIF-removing re-encode) are sent and stored in IndexedDB for re-upload; the raw file is never stored and never re-uploaded; the re-upload after a `FILE_MISSING` 410 re-sends the stored bytes without a second downscale or re-encode.
- **C-12.3** (FR-11, FR-12, D-7, CF-20) The sent bytes of every accepted upload are at most 5 MB (5 242 880 B, SD-25); a re-encode result over 5 MB is rejected with T-2 and nothing is sent. **Visible consequence the human's confirmation carries (CF-20):** a file of at most 5 MB whose EXIF-free re-encode exceeds 5 MB is rejected. The EXIF re-encode must never be used to shrink a file (OD-8).
- **C-12.4** (FR-9, FR-11, FR-13, FR-15, N-7, N-10, CF-19) A file that cannot be decoded for the downscale or the EXIF-removing re-encode, or from which the metadata cannot be removed, is rejected on the client with the format error T-1 and never reaches the BFF; a decodable file with a false extension is re-encoded and accepted (the extension is never used). AC-2's "a `.png` file with non-PNG content is rejected on the BFF" is therefore vestigial for the UI path (CF-19, reading awaiting confirmation).
- **C-12.5** (FR-13) The fixture is a JPG with GPS EXIF: the bytes handed to the upload endpoint contain none of it, and the bytes re-sent after a forced `FILE_MISSING` are identical to the first upload's.

**Not decided here (separate entries):** the EXIF mechanism (OD-39), BFF-side size and EXIF enforcement (OD-40), the re-encode format and animated WEBP (OD-38).

**Blocking: false** (the decided parts are decided; the open parts are non-blocking, reasons in their entries).

**v6 cross-note (OD-8, OD-28).** (1) AC-4 / AC-5 now apply to **user uploads only** (OD-28); a generated version is sent as is. (2) The human's OD-8 ("do not silently re-encode") forbids size-driven re-encoding; the EXIF-driven re-encode of AC-5, its output format and its location (this entry) are still open. (3) The human placed the OD-8 size check on the client; whether the BFF also checks the size on `/api/image-studio/upload` is sub-question (b) here. Still OPEN and non-blocking: the Specification can carry the client-side mechanism as a labelled default and a BFF magic-byte check (AC-2) without a new dependency; an image library would be a human decision.


**Question.** (a) What strips EXIF/GPS (client canvas re-encode, a library, or BFF-side), and is it applied to every image including
those under 2048 px and 5 MB that AC-4 would otherwise leave untouched? (b) Is it enforced and verifiable on the BFF as well, or is the
client trusted? (c) What output format does the re-encode produce (PNG alpha, WEBP, JPEG), and what happens to animated WEBP? (d) Does
the BFF's magic-byte check (AC-2) run on the original upload or on the re-encoded one, and does a pasted clipboard image (AC-1) follow
the same path?

**Why it cannot be inferred.** Checked AC-2, AC-4, AC-5: they place file-type verification on the BFF but resizing and (implicitly)
stripping on the client, and do not say where EXIF removal is enforced. No image-processing code exists on the server (`server/` has no
image library) and `package.json` lists no EXIF, image or magic-byte library. Any new dependency is a human decision (AGENTS.md
section 1).

**v2 cross-note.** The new `/api/image-studio/upload` endpoint (OD-7 proposal) is a second entry point for image bytes: the magic-byte
check (AC-2) and the EXIF requirement (AC-5) must say whether they apply to it. Not decided here.

**v3 cross-note.** OD-7 is now decided with `/api/image-studio/upload` as a second entry point for image bytes (condition C-7.6):
whether AC-2 (magic bytes) and AC-5 (EXIF) apply to it is still undecided here. The BFF has no multipart parser and no image
library; any new dependency is a human decision.

**Impact if unresolved.** A client-only strip can be bypassed by a direct API call, so "stripped from every image before it is sent to
the model" is untestable at the BFF; a canvas re-encode of every image silently changes format and quality and drops alpha. Needs a
fixture with GPS EXIF to be falsifiable.

**Blocking: false.**

## OD-13 - Rate-limit semantics (AC-42) - RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS (v6)

### v6 resolution (human decision, conditions)

*The text below this v6 block is the v5 record of the question, kept for history. Where it says OPEN or `Blocking: true`, this v6 block supersedes it.*

**Human decision (v6, verbatim).** "OD-13: (a) Рахується кожен успішно прийнятий POST до /api/image-studio/generate, що дійшов до виклику Gemini (незалежно від 200 чи 400/500 від Google). Повторні спроби BFF (AC-37) не рахуються як нові. Retry від користувача — новий запит. Cancel у польоті — рахується. (b) Фіксоване вікно 1 година. (c) Пам'ять процесу (скидається при рестарті/деплої), без нових залежностей чи міграцій. (d) countTokens, /api/image-studio/upload і відхилені пре-парсером запити (411/413/501) не рахуються. (e) HTTP 429 Too Many Requests, текст: "Перевищено ліміт генерацій (30 на годину). Спробуйте пізніше." (Retry-After не обов'язковий)." (English rendering: (a) every successfully accepted POST to `/api/image-studio/generate` that reached the Gemini call counts, whether Google answered 200 or 400/500; BFF retries (AC-37) are not new; a user Retry is a new request; a Cancel in flight counts. (b) Fixed one-hour window. (c) Process memory, reset on restart or deploy, no new dependency or migration. (d) `countTokens`, `/api/image-studio/upload` and requests rejected by the pre-parser (411/413/501) do not count. (e) HTTP 429, text "Перевищено ліміт генерацій (30 на годину). Спробуйте пізніше."; `Retry-After` is optional.) The human is the decider; this stage chose nothing.

**Verification (checked in the repository).**
- *Dependencies and storage (AGENTS.md sections 1 and 7.8).* The decision removes the v5 trigger: process memory, no library, no migration. `server/index.js` has no rate-limiting code and no `trust proxy` setting (grep); `package.json` has no rate-limit library. A hand-written counter keyed by `req.ip` (OD-2, `trust proxy` 1) is the only reading that fits "no new dependencies".
- *Client retry (caller's point 2).* `src/services/http-retry.ts` `isUpstreamTransportFailure` returns `false` for any response whose body is `{ error: string }`, and its status list is only 0 / 500 / 502 / 503 / 504, so **429 is not retried by `retryTransport`**, with or without the envelope. Verified from the source.
- *Server retry (a hazard the caller did not name).* `server/utils/retry.js` `withRetry` treats `status === 429`, and any error message containing `429`, `overloaded`, `RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED` or `rate_limit`, as a retryable policy failure. The limiter's rejection must therefore be produced **outside** every `withRetry`-wrapped operation, or the BFF would retry its own limit.
- *Two 429s share one status.* With OD-29 an exhausted **upstream** 429 is returned with its original status (429) and a Retry button, and the limiter's own rejection is also 429. Both use the `{ error: string }` envelope, so the status alone cannot tell them apart; the same problem OD-24 C-24.3 solved for the two 413s. See C-13.6 and C-13.7.
- *Counting versus logging.* The count (every request that reached Gemini) is wider than the usage log (OD-4 / OD-19: only a 200 with an image). Quota and `usage_log` diverge by design; see C-13.8.

**Conditions the Specification MUST carry.**
- C-13.1 **Where the count is taken.** Check-and-increment is **one synchronous step** immediately before the Gemini generate call, after body validation, the 501 check, the OD-24 check and `countTokens` (none of those is counted). If the key already holds 30 counted requests in the window, the request is rejected (C-13.6) and Gemini is not called. Because the step is atomic, concurrent requests cannot take the key past 30. An earlier read-only pre-check (to avoid wasting `countTokens` calls on a request that will be rejected) is permitted; the authoritative decision is the atomic one. A request aborted before the Gemini call started (for example Cancel during `countTokens`) never reached the call and is not counted.
- C-13.2 **What counts (the human's rule, spelled out).** Counted: a 200 with an image; a 200 with text only; a safety block (a 200); an empty or truncated response (a failed turn); an upstream 4xx or 5xx after the BFF's own retries (all BFF attempts of one request are **one** unit, AC-37); a Cancel after the call started; a user Retry (new request); the automatic re-send after a 410 (OD-7; a new client request that reaches the call again, so one user action can consume two units); a `retryTransport` or OD-23 replay of a request whose response was lost. Not counted: `countTokens`; `/api/image-studio/upload`; a request rejected by the pre-parser (411 / 413) or by the 501; the OD-24 `PROTECTED_HISTORY_EXCEEDS_LIMIT` 413 (rejected before the call); the limiter's own 429.
- C-13.3 **Window.** Fixed, one hour, per client IP. The human said "fixed window" but not its anchor. **Flagged default (awaiting the human at `HUMAN_SPEC_APPROVAL`, not a decision):** a key's window starts at its first counted request and ends exactly one hour later; the next counted request after expiry starts a new window. The alternative (clock-aligned hours) is the other fixed-window reading. The clock is injectable so tests do not wait. Expired entries are purged (a sweep on access or a non-keeping timer) so the map is bounded by the number of IPs active in the last hour.
- C-13.4 **State and its accepted limitations.** Process memory; no dependency, no store, no migration. Recorded as **accepted limitations**, not defects: (i) a restart or Railway deploy resets every counter; (ii) the counter is per process, so with N replicas the effective limit is up to 30 x N per hour (the repository records a Railway deployment, but nothing in it fixes the replica count); (iii) the key is the client IP (OD-2, assumption A-2: Railway is the only proxy): users behind one egress address, which for an internal editorial team may be the whole team, share one pool of 30; (iv) a client that can rotate addresses (for example across an IPv6 prefix) is not limited more strictly. AC-42 itself says "per client IP address (no user accounts exist)", so (iii) and (iv) follow the Story.
- C-13.5 **No library.** Because the decision says no new dependency, the limiter is code in `server/` (a small module with a test), not an added package; adding a package would be a new human decision (AGENTS.md section 1).
- C-13.6 **The rejection.** HTTP 429 in the `{ error: string }` envelope, mounted after `cors()` (so the browser can read it), carrying a **closed code constant** distinct from every upstream-derived 429, following OD-24 C-24.3 (`error` doubles as the machine code; the client maps it to the human's Ukrainian text and never renders the code). The text shown is the human's literal "Перевищено ліміт генерацій (30 на годину). Спробуйте пізніше." `Retry-After` is optional (human); if the Specification sends it, the value is the seconds left in the window. Not retried by `retryTransport` (verified) or by the BFF (C-13.1: outside `withRetry`). **Flagged defaults, not decisions:** no Retry button on this rejection (retrying cannot succeed before the window ends; same reasoning as OD-3 N-3.1 and OD-24 C-24.4), and the user's text and attachments stay in the composer (OD-19(f) applied by analogy).
- C-13.7 **Upstream 429 versus limiter 429.** An exhausted upstream 429 (OD-29) is a different closed code with a Retry button; the client dispatches on status **and** code, never the status alone. A test sends both and asserts the two different outcomes.
- C-13.8 **Quota and usage diverge by design.** A request that reached Gemini but failed, was safety-blocked, returned text only or was cancelled consumes one quota unit, writes no `usage_log` row (OD-19(d)) and costs 0 in the session total (OD-4). A user Retry after such a failure consumes another unit.
- C-13.9 **AC-42 amendment (also in the cumulative table).** AC-42 is read as: "Image generation is limited to 30 requests per hour per client IP address; a request counts once it has reached the Gemini call, whatever its outcome; the 31st such request within the fixed one-hour window is rejected with HTTP 429 and a visible message." The Story's "31st generation ... is rejected" is therefore broadened: failed and cancelled upstream calls also consume quota.
- C-13.10 **Tests (stubbed Gemini, injectable clock, no network).** 30 counted then the 31st rejected; each counted class of C-13.2 consumes exactly one unit and each uncounted class consumes none; BFF retries consume one unit; two IPs are isolated; the window resets after one hour; concurrent requests cannot exceed 30; the 429 triggers exactly one client request and zero upstream calls.

**Blocking: false** (decided; the window anchor and the Retry-button default are flagged for `HUMAN_SPEC_APPROVAL`).


**Question.** (a) What counts as one "generation": every POST to the generate route, only successful ones, retries inside the BFF
(AC-37), the user's Retry button, cancelled and safety-blocked calls? (b) Fixed hourly window or sliding window? (c) Is the counter in
process memory (reset on restart, per-replica) or persisted? (d) Which routes are limited (is `countTokens` or the Files API upload
counted)? (e) What are the status code and the visible message text and language for the 31st request, and does Retry-After apply?

**Why it cannot be inferred.** Checked AC-42 and `server/index.js`: no rate limiting exists in the BFF today, so there is no precedent to
mirror; `package.json` has no rate-limit library (a new dependency would be a human decision). Related to OD-2, which now fixes only
**how the client IP key is derived** (`trust proxy` 1), not what is counted.

**v2 cross-note.** (d) now also covers the new `/api/image-studio/upload` endpoint and the automatic generation retry after a 410
(OD-7): whether those count is undecided.

**v3 cross-note.** Still undecided whether these count toward the 30 per hour: the `countTokens` call before a generation, the
`/api/image-studio/upload` call, the automatic retry after a 410 (OD-7 C-7.3), and requests rejected by the OD-11 middleware (411/413)
or by the missing-key 501 (OD-3). OD-4 now fixes that only successful generations are written to `usage_log`; that is a logging rule,
not a rate-limit rule.

**v4 cross-note.** Also undecided: whether the OD-24 protected-history 413 (rejected before any generation), the OD-25 pre-parser 413
and the client's repeat of a request whose response was lost (OD-23 C-23.3, a replay) count toward the 30 per hour. A replay
after a lost response may have been generated and billed once already.

**Impact if unresolved.** "The 31st generation within the hour is rejected" cannot be tested without a definition of "generation" and of
the window; an in-memory counter silently resets on every Railway deploy.

**v5 cross-note.** (1) The number of `countTokens` calls per user action (OD-32) changes what "one generation" can mean if
`countTokens` is counted. (2) The failure classes of OD-29 (non-retryable upstream errors, exhausted retries) need a counting rule: does a
request that ended in an error count? (3) OD-28: a request whose only new content is an "Edit this version" attachment.

**v5 re-classification: blocking: true (was false).** Honest assessment against the blocking rule. AC-42 is a core cost and security
criterion ("the 31st generation within the hour is rejected with a visible message"). The log's own v4 text says it cannot be tested
without a definition of "generation" and of the window; the Specification (FR-59) says "until OD-13 is answered the 30-per-hour
behaviour cannot be fully tested; the test-writer must not invent the definitions", and every one of sub-questions (a) to (e) is
a value the Specification writer would have to guess. Sub-question (c), process memory versus persisted state versus a library, is also a
dependency or storage choice: the BFF has no rate-limiting code and `package.json` no library (checked in v1), so a library would be a new
dependency and a persisted counter a store/schema decision, both reserved to a human by AGENTS.md sections 1 and 7.8. A wrong guess
could therefore breach a rule, not only a test. It stays an owner decision; this stage records it and proposes nothing.

**v5 blocking text (answered in v6; superseded by the v6 block above).** What the human must give (one answer covers the entry): (a) what counts - successful 200s only, or every
authorised POST to the generate route, and whether BFF retries, the Retry button, a 410 repeat, Cancel and safety blocks add to the
count; (b) fixed or sliding one-hour window; (c) process memory (resets on restart, per replica) or persisted, and whether a
dependency is acceptable; (d) whether `countTokens`, the upload endpoint and the rejected requests (411/413/501) are counted;
(e) status code, message text and language of the 31st rejection, and whether `Retry-After` is sent. Realistic leads, none a
decision: count successful generations only with a fixed in-memory window (smallest change, resets on deploy, no dependency);
count every accepted generate request (stricter for cost, penalises failed calls); persist in the existing SQLite usage store
(survives deploys, schema/store change).

## OD-14 - UI language and Ukrainian literals (AC-16, AC-18, AC-46) - RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS (v7; v8: the proposed texts are confirmed, OD-41)

**v8 cross-note.** The readings of C-14.3 (CF-1), C-14.4 (CF-3), C-14.5 (CF-4) and C-14.6 (CF-2) are confirmed with the register (OD-49) and the proposed texts are confirmed in OD-41 (exact ids there).

### v7 resolution (human decision)

*The text below this v7 block is the earlier record, kept for history; this v7 block supersedes its pending state. The "owner's standing convention" lead in the v2/v5 cross-notes is superseded by this decision.*

**Human decision (v7, verbatim).** "OD-14 (Мова інтерфейсу): Усі повідомлення, імена пресетів, тексти помилок локалізуються відповідно до поточного значення uiLanguage (EN/UA)." (English rendering: All messages, preset names and error texts are localised according to the current `uiLanguage` value (EN/UA).)

**Verification.** `uiLanguage` exists as `signal<'en' | 'uk'>('en')` in `src/app/app.component.ts` line 424, persisted in `localStorage` as `seo_gen_ui_lang` (lines 663, 689), toggled at line 774; `TRANSLATIONS[uiLanguage()]` is the existing mechanism. The human's "UA" is the code `'uk'`. The default is `'en'`, so a first-time operator sees English. AGENTS.md section 7.5 (no hard-coded language lists outside `STORE_REGISTRY`) is not triggered: this is a UI-language matter, not a store-language list.

**Conditions the Specification MUST carry.**
- **C-14.1** (FR-77, Definitions "studio text") Every studio text (messages, error texts, preset names, chip labels, announcements, notices, the SynthID notice, the native-alert text, control labels) is resolved from the `uiLanguage` value at the moment it is rendered or announced; a toggle re-renders every visible text.
- **C-14.2** (SD-29, FR-77) Every studio text has a non-empty `en` and a non-empty `uk` value; a test over the text keys fails when a key exists in one language only; no silent fallback to the other language.
- **C-14.3** (CF-1) The Story's Ukrainian literals (AC-18 chips, AC-46 announcements, AC-36 wording) and the human-supplied Ukrainian strings (OD-13, OD-24, OD-26, OD-29) are the `uk` values; the corresponding `en` values are proposals (OD-41). The EN preset names come from AC-17 and the OD-3 message from the human.
- **C-14.4** (CF-3, SD-21, SD-29) **Reading awaiting confirmation:** model-facing prompt text (preset templates, chip instructions) is not localised; it stays single-language English, because the human named messages, preset names and error texts. A consequence: a version made from a chip or preset has an English `alt` under `'uk'` (AC-47: `alt` is the prompt).
- **C-14.5** (CF-4) **Reading awaiting confirmation:** control labels and static chrome (Cancel, Retry, New session, Edit this version, ...) are in scope; the Story's English labels are the `en` values and the `uk` values are proposals.
- **C-14.6** (CF-2) The default language is `'en'`; stated so the human can confirm that the English proposals are what a first-time operator sees.
- **C-14.7** Data is language-neutral: turns, versions, preset ids, file names and the `task_label` do not depend on the language (no localised preset name in `task_label`).

**Not decided here:** the wording of the proposed texts (OD-41).

**Blocking: false** (decided).

**v6 cross-note.** The human supplied new Ukrainian literals: the five preset names (OD-26), "Помилка API" (OD-29), the rate-limit text (OD-13) and, in v4, the OD-24 message. Every string the human has given is Ukrainian; the owner's standing convention (user-facing messages in Ukrainian) is consistent with that. What is still open is the single convention: does the studio follow the app's UI-language toggle, which of the UA and EN preset names is displayed, and whether the English UI shows English or Ukrainian messages. Strings the Specification must still write itself (the OD-8 size error, the OD-19 safety explanation, the trim notice, the empty-response error) are fixed by it in Ukrainian and flagged. OPEN, non-blocking: one convention answers all of it.


**Question.** Which language do the interface strings, the preset names and the quick-edit chips use? AC-16 gives presets a UA and an EN
name, while AC-18 and AC-46 fix literal Ukrainian strings ("Прибрати фон", "Генерую…", "Версію v3 готово"). Is the studio bound to the
app's existing UI-language toggle (`uiLanguage` `'en' | 'uk'`, persisted as `seo_gen_ui_lang`)? In which language is the follow-up
instruction sent to the model for each chip, and are the error and rate-limit messages (AC-7, AC-37-39, AC-42) Ukrainian only?

**Why it cannot be inferred.** Checked `src/app/app.component.ts`: `uiLanguage` and `TRANSLATIONS` exist, so the app has a bilingual UI
mechanism, but the Story does not say the studio uses it, and AC-18/AC-46 name only Ukrainian text. AGENTS.md section 7.5 forbids
hard-coded language lists outside `STORE_REGISTRY`; two preset languages are a UI-language matter, not a store-language list, but the
Story does not say so.

**v2 cross-note.** The OD-3 decision quotes an **English** message ("Gemini API key is required for Image Studio") while AC-18 and
AC-46 fix Ukrainian literals; the owner's standing convention is that user-facing messages are Ukrainian. The language of this message
belongs here and is not decided.

**v3 cross-note.** The user-visible messages decided or implied in v3 also need a language: the 501 missing-key text (OD-3), the
AC-24/AC-29 trim notices (OD-7), the 411/413 rejections (OD-11) and the expired-file re-upload progress (OD-7). Not decided here.

**v4 cross-note.** The human's OD-24 message is a Ukrainian literal ("Сесія занадто велика. Будь ласка, почніть нову сесію з останньої
версії"). The AC-24 / AC-29 trim notice must now also say the removed versions are permanently deleted (OD-23 C-23.6). Whether
these appear in English under the English UI setting is still undecided here.

**v5 cross-note.** OD-26 asks for the Ukrainian preset names; which of the UA and EN names is displayed (this entry) decides whether
the EN names are ever shown. The messages the v5 decisions add (OD-29 error texts, OD-13 31st-request message) need a language here too.
Stays non-blocking: the language rule is one convention that can be answered for all strings at once. The owner's standing convention
(user-facing messages in Ukrainian, noted in the v2 cross-note) is not written in the Story or in AGENTS.md, so it is a lead and not a
decision.

**Impact if unresolved.** Under the English UI setting the chips and announcements would be wrong or untested; the preset-name display
rule (which of the two names is shown) is undefined; tests would assert one language only.

**Blocking: false.**

## OD-15 - Location of `PROMPT_PRESETS` and chip instructions (Rule 3), `{product}` source - RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS (v7; v8: chip file decided, D-14 / D-15 confirmed through CF-23, OD-42)

**v8 cross-note.** The chip instructions live in `src/prompt-core/prompt-presets.ts` (OD-42); D-14 and D-15 are confirmed through the register entry CF-23 (OD-49); C-15.4 (CF-22) and C-15.5 (CF-23) are confirmed.

### v7 resolution (human decision)

*Partial resolution for (a) the presets, (b), (c) the source and (d). The text below this v7 block is the earlier record, kept for history. The location of the chip instructions and the `{product}` timing / empty case are not decided and are carried in OD-42.*

**Human decision (v7, verbatim).** "OD-15 (Розміщення пресетів і {product}): Пресети розміщуємо у новому файлі src/prompt-core/prompt-presets.ts. Значення для {product} береться з того ж поля вводу назви товару, що й для імені файлу (AC-33). System instruction не надсилається (лише промпт користувача)." (English rendering: The presets go in a new file `src/prompt-core/prompt-presets.ts`. The value for `{product}` comes from the same product-name input field as the file name (AC-33). No system instruction is sent (only the user's prompt).)

**Verification.** `src/prompt-core/prompt-presets.ts` does not exist today. `src/prompt-core/` is one of the two places AGENTS.md section 3 Rule 3 / section 7.4 allow prompt text, so the placement satisfies Rule 3 and `arch-guard.sh` Rule #3. It is not on the FROZEN list (AGENTS.md section 9: `task-a.ts`, `task-b.ts`, `task-c.ts`, `master-system-prompt.ts`, `output-validator.ts`), so the section 9 stop is not triggered. It is inside the coverage `include` (`src/prompt-core/**/*.ts`, `vitest.config.ts`), so the section 5 floors apply to it. It is a new file in an existing directory, not a new directory or dependency (AGENTS.md sections 1 and 7.8 not triggered). The Story's Surface list names `src/app`, `src/services`, `src/utils`, `server/**` only, so the file is a Story-scope extension (CF-22). AC-33 names the product-name input ("the product name is entered by the user, otherwise `image` is used").

**Conditions the Specification MUST carry.**
- **C-15.1** (FR-78, FR-29, FR-30) `PROMPT_PRESETS` lives in `src/prompt-core/prompt-presets.ts`, modelled on `STORE_REGISTRY`: exactly the five entries of OD-26, each with `id`, `uk` name, `en` name, a template containing `{product}` and the AC-17 defaults (aspect ratio, resolution, model); the registry test imports from that path; no preset template literal exists in a component or service.
- **C-15.2** (FR-79) `{product}` takes the value of the product-name field that also supplies the file-name product segment (one field for both uses); the value is used as typed for the prompt (SD-14 applies to the file name only).
- **C-15.3** (FR-80) No system instruction is sent: for a free-text message and for each of the five presets the outbound Gemini request carries no system-instruction field (test with a stubbed Gemini). The new image method on `GeminiProvider` must not inherit one from the text path.
- **C-15.4** (CF-22, NFR-13) The Story's Surface is extended with `src/prompt-core/prompt-presets.ts`; the coverage floors apply to it.
- **C-15.5** (CF-23) The earlier FR-75 property "no unreplaced `{product}` in `alt`" is replaced by the FR-79 substitution tests.
- **C-15.6** (d) Editable template: already answered by the Story (AC-15: the preset fills the form and stays editable).

**Not decided here:** where the chip instructions live (Rule 3 allows `src/prompts/` or `src/prompt-core/`), when `{product}` is substituted (D-14) and what an empty field yields (D-15): OD-42.

**Blocking: false** (decided).

**v6 cross-note.** The preset ids and UA names are now fixed (OD-26); where the registry lives, the system instruction, the `{product}` source and the editable-template question are unchanged and still OPEN, non-blocking.


**Question.** (a) In which directory do `PROMPT_PRESETS` and the chip follow-up instructions live: `src/prompt-core/` (like

## OD-16 - Session retention, quota and list semantics (AC-25, AC-26) - RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS (v7: (a), (b) cap value, (c) alert, (e) Files API files; v8: every residue is decided, OD-36, OD-37, OD-43, OD-44)

**v8 cross-note.** Cap behaviour: B, oldest by last activity, silent, stored sessions (OD-36). Quota failure while storing an upload's sent bytes: keep the attachment without a re-upload source (OD-37). Two tabs: last write wins (OD-43). Activity, boundary and commit outcome: SD-32, SD-33, SD-34 confirmed (OD-44). C-16.1, C-16.3 (CF-13), C-16.4 and C-16.6 (CF-14) are confirmed. C-16.2 is replaced by OD-36 C-36.1 to C-36.7. AC-25 and AC-26 are amended further by OD-36 (the overrides table of this log).

### v7 resolution (human decision)

*Partial resolution. The text below this v7 block is the earlier record, kept for history. The decided parts are recorded here; four residues are carried as their own entries and two of them are blocking (OD-36, OD-37).*

**Human decision (v7, verbatim).** "OD-16 (Зберігання, квота, сесії): Зберігаємо 7 днів від останньої активності (застарілі видаляються при ініціалізації/завантаженні). Максимальна кількість сесій у списку — 20. При помилці квоти IndexedDB показуємо нативний alert браузера. "New session" просто залишає старі файли у Files API (вони очистяться на стороні Google)." (English rendering: We keep sessions 7 days from the last activity (stale ones are deleted on initialisation / load). The maximum number of sessions in the list is 20. On an IndexedDB quota error we show the browser's native alert. "New session" simply leaves the old files in the Files API (they are cleaned up on Google's side).)

**Verification.** AC-25 says only "kept for 7 days" with no reference point; AC-26 says the previous session "remains available in a session list". There is no IndexedDB use anywhere in `src/` today, so there is no storage wrapper or convention to mirror. The cap of 20 states a number but no behaviour for a 21st session; every model switch and blocked-state exit creates a session row automatically (OD-17 C-17.6), which makes that gap a real contract hole (OD-36). The human's quota decision names the dialog but not the outcome for the data being written (OD-37 for uploads; SD-34 for the response commit).

**Conditions the Specification MUST carry.**
- **C-16.1** (FR-48, SD-32, SD-33) Retention is 7 days from the last activity; stale sessions are deleted when the stored sessions are initialised or loaded (page load and each later load of the list), never by a timer; deleting a session deletes its whole record (turns, versions, sent bytes, downloaded flags, cost total). **Derivations awaiting confirmation (OD-44):** what counts as "activity" (SD-32) and the strict boundary (SD-33).
- **C-16.2** (FR-86) The list holds at most 20 sessions. **The behaviour at the 21st is OD-36 (blocking); no default is offered and none may be chosen by the Specification.**
- **C-16.3** (FR-81, CF-13) On an IndexedDB quota error the client shows the native `window.alert` with T-20 in the current `uiLanguage`, once per failing user action, for every write that can hit the quota (the FR-44 commit, the FR-16 store of the sent bytes, the FR-33 downloaded flag, session creation); it is not a `role="alert"` element and updates no `role="status"` region; focus goes to the input after dismissal; `window.alert` is spied in tests. **Reading awaiting confirmation (CF-13):** the native dialog is a third channel next to OD-21's `role="alert"` and plain text.
- **C-16.4** (FR-81, SD-34) **Derivation awaiting confirmation (OD-44):** a quota failure on the FR-44 commit leaves the session exactly as before, the turn is a failed turn whose content returns to the composer, and no Retry button is offered (a Retry would pay again while storage is full). A flag write leaves the flag unset; a session creation creates no session. The sent-bytes store is OD-37.
- **C-16.5** (FR-49) "New session" leaves the Gemini Files API files the previous session referenced: the BFF makes no delete call (test with a stubbed Files API asserts it); the files expire on Google's side. The previous session keeps its own cost total and flags (OD-17 (e)).
- **C-16.6** (CF-14) AC-25 is amended ("kept for 7 days" becomes 7 days from the last activity; what survives is the retained session); AC-26 is sharpened (the list obeys the 7 days and the cap).

**Not decided here (separate entries):** the 21st session (OD-36, blocking), a quota failure when storing an upload's sent bytes (OD-37, blocking), two tabs on one session (OD-43), confirmation of SD-32 / SD-33 / SD-34 (OD-44).

**Blocking: false** for the decided parts; see OD-36 and OD-37, which are blocking.

**v6 cross-note (OD-17, OD-22, OD-23).** (1) Every model switch and every blocked-state exit creates a new session row (OD-17 C-17.6), so session rows and IndexedDB usage grow faster; sub-questions (a) to (c) (retention clock, list cap, quota failure) matter more but remain open. (2) Sub-question (e): "New session" leaves the previous session, with its own cost total and its own downloaded flags, in the list (AC-26, OD-17 (e)); nothing is carried into the new session. What "clears the current history" does to Files API files is still open. (3) The downloaded flag lives in the session record and is deleted with it (OD-22). Still OPEN and non-blocking.


**Question.** (a) Is the 7-day retention measured from creation or from last activity, and when is expiry applied (on load, on a timer)?
(b) Does the session list (AC-26) obey the same 7 days, and is there a cap on the number of sessions? (c) What happens when IndexedDB
quota is exceeded (2K/4K versions are large), including the user-visible message? (d) Behaviour with two tabs on the same session?
(e) What does "clears the current history after confirmation" do to the cumulative cost (AC-44) and to Files API files already uploaded?

**Why it cannot be inferred.** Checked: no IndexedDB use exists in `src/` (so there is no storage wrapper or convention to mirror), and
the Story states only "7 days". The Files API keeps files for a shorter period than 7 days, which is why AC-28 needs re-upload (see
OD-7).

**v2 cross-note.** The OD-7 proposal makes the client's IndexedDB copy of the **original** images the only source for re-upload, so (c)
quota failure and (a) expiry now also determine whether an expired Files API file can be recovered.

**v3 cross-note.** The session now also holds the running cost total (OD-4 C-4.6), persisted in IndexedDB; (e) therefore also decides
whether "New session" / "clears the history" resets the total.

**v4 cross-note.** OD-23 is resolved (lead A): the client's stored turns are the retained turns, so there is no second list. Two
consequences for this entry: (1) the IndexedDB write of a response and the deletion of the trimmed turns is one transaction (OD-23
C-23.3), so a quota failure on commit leaves the session unchanged, which this entry's (c) must say how the user is told; (2) two tabs
on one session (d) can race a trim, because a turn deleted in one tab may still be sent by the other, the BFF only echoes ids it was
sent.

**Impact if unresolved.** Quota errors and expiry timing are exactly where persistence bugs appear; the tests would have to invent the
clock and the failure message.

**Blocking: false.**

## OD-17 - Flash/Pro branch semantics (AC-27) - RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS (v6)

### v6 resolution (human decision, conditions)

*The text below this v6 block is the v5 record of the question, kept for history. Where it says OPEN or `Blocking: true`, this v6 block supersedes it.*

**Human decision (v6, verbatim).** "OD-17: Нова гілка = нова сесія. (a) Створення гілки створює новий рядок у списку сесій (AC-26). (b) Нумерація в новій сесії з v1. (c,d,e) Стрічка, ZIP, вартість охоплюють лише поточну сесію. (f) Перемикання моделі заборонено (кнопка неактивна), якщо в полі вводу є невідправлений текст або нові завантаження. (g) У заблокованому стані (OD-24) "Edit this version" пропонується на всіх збережених (retained) версіях у стрічці." (English rendering: A new branch is a new session. (a) Creating a branch adds a new row to the session list (AC-26). (b) Numbering restarts at v1 in the new session. (c, d, e) The strip, the ZIP and the cost cover the current session only. (f) Switching the model is forbidden (the button is inactive) while the composer holds unsent text or new uploads. (g) In the blocked state (OD-24), "Edit this version" is offered on all retained versions in the strip.) The human is the decider; this stage chose nothing.

**Tension point 3 (caller): when does "Edit this version" attach within the same session, and when does it start a new one?** The human's answer settles the branch; the Story (AC-22) and the human's OD-24 text settle the rest. There is exactly one reading, derived (not separately asked) and flagged for `HUMAN_SPEC_APPROVAL`:

| Situation | What happens | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Normal state | "Edit this version" **attaches that version to the next message of the same session**; no new session; AC-22 is unchanged. | Story AC-22; the human's OD-24 text limits the new-session behaviour to the blocked state. |
| Blocked state (after `PROTECTED_HISTORY_EXCEEDS_LIMIT`, OD-24) | The **same action** creates a **new session** (a new AC-26 row) seeded with the chosen version; offered on **every retained version** (g). It is the way to continue; the blocked session keeps its history for viewing and download. | OD-24 (human) and OD-17 (a), (g). |
| Model switch Flash <-> Pro (AC-27) | A **new session** seeded with the latest version; allowed only when the composer holds no unsent text and no new uploads (f). | OD-17 (a), (f) and AC-27. |

**Verification.** (1) The three situations do not overlap: AC-22 never creates a session, AC-27 and the blocked state always do. (2) (f) implies the seed arrives in the **composer** of the new session as an attached image (the old composer content cannot be carried, hence the guard); the new session starts with no turns. (3) AC-13 gives a version number to "each generated image", so a seed (an input, not a generated image) is not a version: the first generated image of the new session is v1, which matches (b). (4) AC-26 already says the previous session stays available in the list, so (a) needs no new list semantics.

**Conditions the Specification MUST carry.**
- C-17.1 The three-row table above, with the UI making the behaviour visible (the OD-24 message already tells the user that "Edit this version" starts a new session; in the normal state the action is an attachment to the next message).
- C-17.2 **The seed.** It enters the new session's composer as an attached image (sent inline, not counted toward the 5, OD-28), becomes part of the first user turn U1 when sent, and is **not** a version of the new session (not in its strip or ZIP, not numbered). As part of U1 it is protected like any original (OD-7 C-7.4) and is **inline**, so it counts toward the 18 MB inline measure for the whole life of that session (OD-27 C-27.6). The C-23.7 headroom arithmetic and the OD-24 exit loop check must include it: a seed that alone exceeds the limits ends in the same terminating error and the Specification states that case (OD-24 C-24.6). With 4K versions this can recur; V-6 (iii) decides whether it can.
- C-17.3 **Turn ids and numbering.** Turn ids are unique within a session; each session has its own id space; a seeded session reuses no id (this completes OD-23 C-23.2). Version numbers are per session, start at v1 and are never renumbered: after OD-23 cuts the surviving numbers are non-contiguous (v1, v5); the next number is the highest number ever assigned in the session plus one, which equals the highest retained number because the latest version is always protected; a failed, cancelled or non-history turn consumes no number. *Derived from AC-13 / AC-46 (the announcement carries the real number); the human did not state the gap rule.*
- C-17.4 **Scope.** The strip (AC-23), "Download all versions" (AC-35), file names (AC-33) and the cost total (AC-44) cover the current session only. The cost of earlier sessions is not added to a new session's total (accepted; the OD-4 total is an estimate and not authoritative). Two sessions may each produce a `..._v1_...` file for the same product name; a single ZIP never mixes sessions, so names inside a ZIP do not collide.
- C-17.5 **The model-switch guard (f).** The control is inactive while the composer holds unsent text or new uploads. **Flagged defaults, not decisions:** an unsent AC-22 attachment and a failed turn awaiting Retry (its content stays in the composer, OD-19) also count as unsent content; the control is also inactive while a generation runs (the input is locked, AC-11). In the blocked state the switch follows the same guard; "Edit this version" in the blocked state is **not** subject to the composer guard, because the human restricted only the model switch, and the rejected text stays in the old session's composer (OD-19(f)).
- C-17.6 **Session list.** Every switch and every blocked-state exit adds a row (AC-26). No parent link is required. The 7-day retention, the number of rows and the IndexedDB quota are OD-16 (open); this decision increases the pressure on them (recorded in the OD-16 cross-note).
- C-17.7 AC-27, AC-13, AC-23, AC-35, AC-44 and AC-22 are restated for a single linear history per session, which removes the need for a branch scope in those criteria.

**Blocking: false** (decided; the three-row reading and the flagged defaults are for `HUMAN_SPEC_APPROVAL`).


**Question.** After a mid-session model switch, is the "new branch" a new session in the AC-26 list or a branch inside the same session?
Does version numbering (AC-13) continue (v4) or restart (v1)? Does the strip (AC-23) show both branches? Does "Download all versions"
(AC-35) include every branch, and how are files named (AC-33)? Does the cost total (AC-44) span branches? Is the switch allowed with an
unsent draft or an attached image?

**Why it cannot be inferred.** Checked AC-13, AC-23, AC-26, AC-27, AC-33, AC-35: each is written for a single linear history; none
defines a branch. No repo source defines the term.

**v4 cross-note.** OD-24 makes "Edit this version" (in the blocked state) start "a new branch/session" seeded with a version and
leave the old originals behind. Whether that is a branch inside the session or a row in the AC-26 list, whether version numbering
restarts, and which versions are offered the action are all this entry's questions; the OD-24 exit cannot be fully specified until it
is answered. Also: after OD-23 cuts, version numbers of the surviving versions are not contiguous (v1, v5); the numbering rule
(AC-13) needs this entry's answer.

**v5 additions.** (g) **In the OD-24 blocked state, which retained versions offer "Edit this version"?** OD-24 C-24.6 required it "at
least on the latest version" and called the rest "the Specification's call"; the v4 cross-note above made the same thing an OD-17
question. It is a product behaviour (which escape routes exist from a blocked session), so it is asked here once and the Specification
must not choose it. (h) Per C-23.2 the turn-id scope depends on this entry: unique within the session, or within a branch, and
whether a seeded branch re-uses ids. The numbering answer also fixes the file names of AC-33.

**Impact if unresolved.** A tester cannot assert "v-number after switch"; the ZIP content and file names are undefined.

**v5 re-classification: blocking: true (was false).** The v4 text itself says "the OD-24 exit cannot be fully specified until it is
answered". The human decided OD-24 in terms of a "new branch/session" that "leaves the old originals behind"; that decision cannot become
a requirement without knowing what a branch is. The Specification's FR-41 and FR-44 defer every property (session or branch, numbering,
strip, ZIP, file names, cost total, unsent draft), AC-13, AC-23, AC-26, AC-27, AC-33, AC-35 and AC-44 are written for a linear history, and
- **C-23.2** (turn-id scope) cannot be finished. A wrong guess would make AC-27 and the OD-24 exit untestable or self-contradictory.

**v5 blocking text (answered in v6; superseded by the v6 block above).** What the human must give: (a) a new branch is a new row in the AC-26 session list, or a branch inside the same
session; (b) version numbering restarts at v1 or continues; (c) the strip shows the current branch only or all; (d) "Download all
versions" covers the current branch or every branch, and the file name rule; (e) the cost total is per branch or per session; (f)
the switch is allowed with an unsent draft or attached image; (g) as above. Realistic leads, none a decision: **branch = new session
in the AC-26 list** (each history is already linear, turn ids and numbering are per session, the cost total is per session, no
multi-branch strip; the simplest model and the one OD-24's wording "new branch/session ... leaving the old originals behind" fits);
**branch inside one session** (a tree view and per-branch downloads; more UI, every AC above needs a branch scope).

## OD-18 - "Native resolution, no re-compression" vs "PNG default, converted" (AC-31, AC-32, AC-35) - RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS (v7; v8: the residues D-17, D-19 to D-22, SD-40, clipboard and ZIP are confirmed, OD-45)

**v8 cross-note.** Consistency with OD-38 (the upload re-encode keeps the original format): OD-18 (d) converts a differing API answer to "the input's format"; the input's format is read from the user's original file (D-19, confirmed); the re-encode of OD-38 keeps that same format, so the two rules never disagree (the one exception, an encoder returning another type, is rejected by OD-38 C-38.4). The quality of (f) and the white fill of (g) govern the *download* conversion; they are not decisions about the upload re-encode (OD-38 P-9). C-18.6 (CF-17), C-18.7 (clipboard: always PNG) and C-18.10 (SD-40) are confirmed (OD-45, OD-49).

### v7 resolution (human decision)

*The text below this v7 block is the earlier record of the question (including the v5 reassessment), kept for history. The human's answers replace it; where it says OPEN, or says the conflict is conditional on V-6, this v7 block supersedes it. The human's first statement was replaced by the follow-up; only the corrected rule stands.*

**Human decisions (v7, verbatim).** Original statement, then corrected by the human: "(примусова конвертація в PNG, а AC-31 каже «без перестиснення») - взагалі то потрібно щоб було так: який формат файлу зображення на вході, такий же має бути і на виході". (English rendering: (forced conversion to PNG, while AC-31 says "without re-compression") - actually it should work like this: whatever the image file format is on input, the same must be on output.) Answers to the follow-up questions: (a) text-to-image with no input: default PNG; (b) several inputs of different formats: the format of the FIRST user-uploaded image in the current message; (c) "Edit this version" attached version: inherit the format of the version being edited; (d) the API returns a different format than the input: convert on the client to the input's format; (e) the AC-32 selector stays; input = output only sets its DEFAULT; the user's manual choice wins; (f) JPEG quality stays 90; (g) transparency when converting to JPEG: fill with white #FFFFFF. Naming: "замість JPEG треба JPG" (English rendering: instead of JPEG it must be JPG) - display name and file extension `.jpg` everywhere user- or file-facing.

**Verification.** AC-31, AC-32, AC-33, AC-34, AC-35 read from the Story. `jszip` is already a runtime dependency (`src/utils/zip-generator.ts`), so the ZIP needs no new package. The client already encodes through a canvas at quality 0.9 (`canvas.toDataURL('image/jpeg', 0.9)`, `src/app/app.component.ts` line 1348), which is the human's quality 90. The human's rule (d)/(e) removes the v5 "conditional contradiction": AC-31's "without re-compression" can no longer hold when the API answers in another format than the input's, and the human chose conversion over byte fidelity in that case. **Blocking re-assessment: stays false, and the v5/v6 trigger "blocking if V-6 (i) records a non-PNG format" is retired.** A JPG or WEBP input with a PNG API answer now always converts; PNG-in with PNG-out stays byte-identical; the delivered-bytes table of FR-82 covers every pair of formats, so the conflict is settled by decision, not by a vendor fact. V-6 (i) remains a recorded fact that says how often the waiver applies and which fixtures to build. The case split below goes beyond the literal answers in a few places; those places are flagged and collected in OD-45.

**Conditions the Specification MUST carry.**
- **C-18.1** (FR-82 input format, first match wins) (1) the message carried at least one user-uploaded image: the format of the first one in attachment order (b); (2) else the message carried an attached version or seed: inherit the input format of the version being edited (c); (3) else, if the session holds a version, the message edits the latest version and inherits its format, and if it holds none (text-to-image) the format is PNG (a). The text-only follow-up of (3) and the upload-plus-attached-version precedence of (1) are readings the answers do not cover (D-20, D-21: OD-45).
- **C-18.2** (FR-55, FR-82 target) The AC-32 selector stays; the input format only sets its default; the operator's choice before downloading wins (e). One value per version (D-22: OD-45).
- **C-18.3** (FR-82 delivered bytes) When the API format S equals the target T the API bytes are delivered unchanged, byte for byte; when they differ the client decodes and encodes at the native pixel dimensions, never resized: JPG at quality 90 (f), with every transparent pixel filled white #FFFFFF (g); PNG as a lossless encode; WEBP at a quality the human did not give (D-17: OD-45).
- **C-18.4** (CF-16, FR-54) A conversion happens in exactly two situations: the API format differs from the input format (d), or the operator changes the selector (e). In both, AC-31's "without re-compression" is waived; the native pixel dimensions always hold. This is stated in FR-54, FR-82 and the overrides table.
- **C-18.5** (FR-56, SD-39) The extension follows the resulting format: `.png`, `.jpg`, `.webp`; `.jpeg` is never used.
- **C-18.6** (FR-58, CF-17) The ZIP contains each version in its own target format, so one ZIP may hold mixed formats (reading awaiting confirmation: one selector per version, not one global selector).
- **C-18.7** (FR-57, AC-34) The clipboard copy stays PNG (the human was silent on it) and converts when the API format is not PNG (reading awaiting confirmation, OD-45).
- **C-18.8** (FR-82 tests, hand-off) The test list must cover S equals T for PNG, JPG and WEBP and all six cross conversions: PNG to JPG, PNG to WEBP, JPG to PNG, JPG to WEBP, WEBP to PNG, WEBP to JPG (including a transparent WEBP to JPG with white fill). Test (iv) as written ("PNG source with alpha, the API returning a JPG") is internally inconsistent, because a JPG answer carries no alpha; it asserts only the PNG signature and the dimensions and must be reworded.
- **C-18.9** (FR-82 failure path) A failed decode or encode shows an error, downloads nothing and leaves the downloaded flag unset. Whether every supported browser can encode WEBP through a canvas was **not verified here**; the planner must account for it (an unsupported encode is this failure path).
- **C-18.10** (JPG naming, SD-40, FR-9, FR-55, FR-56, FR-58, FR-82, T-1) "JPG" is the name everywhere a person or a file sees it: the selector label, the accepted-format wording of AC-2 (T-1 lists "PNG, JPG, WEBP" in both languages), the file extension, the ZIP entries, error and notice texts and the Specification's own wording. The Story's "JPEG" in AC-2, AC-32 and AC-33 is amended. Technical identifiers (the MIME type `image/jpeg`, magic bytes `FF D8 FF`) are the Specification's reading of "user/file-facing" and are awaiting the human's confirmation (OD-45).
- **C-18.11** Story amendments: AC-31 (re-compression clause waived on conversion), AC-32 (default = input format), AC-33 (`{ext}`), AC-34, AC-35 (each ZIP entry in its version's target format); see the cumulative overrides table.

**Not decided here:** OD-45.

**Blocking: false** (decided; re-assessed above).

**v6 cross-note.** Unchanged. Still OPEN and non-blocking; the re-open trigger is unchanged and now listed with OD-27's: if V-6 (i) records any non-PNG MIME type, this entry becomes blocking in the next run.


**Question.** AC-31 says a version downloads at the native resolution "without re-compression", AC-32 says the default format is PNG
"converted on the client". If the API returns JPEG or WEBP, is the default download the original bytes or a PNG conversion? Which format
are the files inside the ZIP (AC-35), and is the AC-32 selector applied to the ZIP? What does the selector's WEBP/JPEG conversion
guarantee about resolution?

**Why it cannot be inferred.** Checked AC-31, AC-32, AC-35: the Story does not say what format the API returns, and the repository holds
no sample response. "Without re-compression" and "default PNG converted" can only both be true if the API always returns PNG.

**Impact if unresolved.** The two criteria can contradict each other; a test for "no re-compression" depends on knowing the source
format. `jszip` is already a runtime dependency (used by `src/utils/zip-generator.ts`), so AC-35 needs no new package; this entry is only
about format.

**v5 reassessment (kept non-blocking; reason stated).** The review noted that FR-48 and FR-49 state "no requirement between the
two" and that the entry's own text says the criteria "can contradict each other". Assessed honestly: the contradiction is **conditional**.
If the API always returns PNG, "download the bytes as returned" and "PNG is the default" are the same act and both criteria hold; the
conversions to WEBP/JPEG are an explicit user choice that AC-32 itself calls "converted" (so a lossy re-encode there does not breach
AC-31 on a sensible reading). The conflict arises only if the API returns JPEG/WEBP for some resolution. That is a vendor fact that no
spec and no owner can establish offline, and it is now recorded by V-6 (extended in v5 to record the returned MIME type per resolution).
A tests-only reading is available (AC-31 asserted against a stub returning PNG; AC-32 conversions asserted separately), so the
Specification can proceed without inventing a value. **Condition:** if V-6 records any non-PNG format, this entry is re-assessed as
blocking in the next clarification run and the human answers, before the download requirements are final, (a) whether the default
one-click download is the bytes as returned or a PNG conversion.

**Still undecided regardless of V-6 (owner input, non-blocking, leads not decisions):** (b) the format of the files inside the ZIP of
AC-35 and whether the AC-32 selector applies to the ZIP. Lead: the ZIP follows the selector (one rule for single and bulk download);
alternative: the ZIP always contains the bytes as returned (no conversion).

**Blocking: false.**

## OD-19 - Text-only, safety-blocked and failed turns vs history (AC-38, AC-39, AC-40) - RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS (v6)

### v6 resolution (human decision, conditions)

*The text below this v6 block is the v5 record of the question, kept for history. Where it says OPEN or `Blocking: true`, this v6 block supersedes it.*

**Human decision (v6, verbatim).** "OD-19: (a,b) Відповіді лише текстом (AC-39) та блокування безпеки (AC-38) — non-history events, в історію не потрапляють. Історія завжди U1 M1 ... Uk Mk. Retry відправляє той самий хід. (c) Cancel = failed turn, не потрапляє в історію. (d) HTTP 200 без зображення не пише рядок в usage_log (вартість 0). (e) Безпека: BFF повертає HTTP 200 з конвертом помилки/попередження (safety). (f) OD-24 413: текст і вкладення залишаються в composer." (English rendering: (a, b) Text-only replies (AC-39) and safety blocks (AC-38) are non-history events and do not enter the history; the history is always `U1 M1 ... Uk Mk`; Retry re-sends the same turn. (c) Cancel is a failed turn and does not enter the history. (d) An HTTP 200 without an image writes no `usage_log` row (cost 0). (e) Safety: the BFF returns HTTP 200 with an error/warning envelope. (f) After the OD-24 413 the text and attachments stay in the composer.) The human is the decider; this stage chose nothing.

**Verification.**
- *Tension point 4 (OD-4 "billing only for HTTP 200" versus AC-43 / AC-44).* OD-4 said "only successful generations (HTTP 200)". A text-only reply and a safety block are also HTTP 200, so the OD-4 wording was ambiguous (OD-4 C-4.3 forbade the Specification to resolve it). The human now fixes it: a row is written only for a **200 that contains at least one image**. OD-4 is refined accordingly (OD-4 v6 note). Amendments are listed in C-19.5.
- *The trim contract is now well defined.* The history is strictly alternating pairs ending at the latest model turn; the "turns after Mk" case the review found (FR-36) cannot occur, so the trim unit (M_i, U_i+1), the exact `trimmedTurnIds` list (OD-23 C-23.3) and the measured remainder (OD-24 C-24.1) are defined for every reachable history. OD-7 C-7.4's two boundary questions (first pair, latest pair) remain the Specification's to state and flag.
- *Transport.* A safety block as a 200 means Angular's `HttpClient` hands it to the Zod schema (AC-14) as a success, never to `retryTransport`. AC-14's schema therefore needs distinct variants; a 200 safety envelope must not be classed as an "invalid response".
- *Envelope.* OD-29 says `{ error: string }` for every failure; the safety block is, in the human's words, an "envelope of error/warning" returned with 200. The field names are not given; they are the Specification's to fix (C-19.4).

**Conditions the Specification MUST carry.**
- C-19.1 **History shape.** The retained history sent to the model is always `U1 M1 ... Uk Mk` (accepted image turns only), followed by the new user turn. A text-only reply, a safety block, a failed turn (any error, an empty or truncated response, OD-30) and a Cancel never enter it. Retry re-sends the **same** user turn (same content, same attachment references; re-upload after expiry as in OD-7); an edited message is a new turn. The AC-40 text "a failed turn never enters the history" is satisfied by construction for all of these; AC-21 ("every request carries all prior turns") is read as the retained accepted turns (already narrowed by OD-23).
- C-19.2 **UI treatment of non-history events.** They are shown in the chat as UI-only events (AC-38 explanation and rephrase suggestion; AC-39 text with a Retry action) and are never sent back. Whether they survive a page reload is not stated; **flagged default (not a decision):** they are not stored in the IndexedDB session, so a reload shows only the accepted turns. The failed user turn's content stays available for Retry (composer or pending state; the Specification fixes which and states it consistently with C-17.5).
- C-19.3 **Cancel.** A Cancel is a failed turn: no history entry, no `usage_log` row, no cost, nothing deleted (no cut is applied, OD-23 C-23.3). It still counts against the rate limit if the Gemini call had started (OD-13 C-13.2). The user's unsent content is not lost; **flagged default:** it returns to the composer, as in (f).
- C-19.4 **Response variants (AC-14).** The generate response schema is a discriminated union with a closed discriminator: success (images, `costUsd`, `trimmedTurnIds`), text-only (text, `trimmedTurnIds`) and safety (explanation, `trimmedTurnIds`), all HTTP 200; every failure is a non-200 `{ error: string }` (OD-29). Field names are the Specification's to fix. A 200 that matches none of the variants is an invalid response (AC-14). Per OD-23 C-23.5, which stays flagged for the human, the client applies the cut listed in `trimmedTurnIds` on **every** 200 that carries it, including the two non-history variants; the loss without a new version is the item already flagged.
- C-19.5 **Usage and AC amendments.** A `usage_log` row is written only for a 200 with at least one image: AC-43 reads "every HTTP 200 generation response that contains at least one image logs `usageMetadata` (input and output tokens, model, resolution)"; AC-44's running total and its `GET /api/usage` rows cover those responses only; AC-19's preset half covers those responses only (OD-4 C-4.7 stays: `task_label`). Text-only, safety, failed and cancelled calls leave no row and cost 0 although Google may bill them, so the session total under-reports real spend (already stated in OD-4). One row per request (cost = price x number of images, OD-30), not one per image.
- C-19.6 **OD-24 413.** The text and attachments of the rejected message stay in the composer (human (f)); no turn is added, nothing is deleted, no row is written, and the request is not counted by the rate limit (OD-13 C-13.2).
- C-19.7 **Retry after a safety block.** A safety block suggests rephrasing (AC-38); the same-turn Retry stays available, an edit is a new turn. A repeated safety block is counted every time (OD-13).

**Blocking: false** (decided; the flagged defaults C-19.2 and C-19.3 are for `HUMAN_SPEC_APPROVAL`).


**Question.** AC-40 says a failed turn never enters the history. Is a text-only model response (AC-39: no image, the text shown, no
version) a "failed turn" or a normal turn? Does the user's message stay in the history after a safety block (AC-38 "does not corrupt the
history") and after a Retry? Does a retry resend the same user turn or a new one, and are cancelled turns (AC-11) treated as failed?

**Why it cannot be inferred.** Checked AC-38 to AC-40: they use "history" for the data resent to the model (AC-21) without saying
whether UI-visible text and history are the same list.

**v3 cross-note.** (1) OD-4 says only successful generations (HTTP 200) are logged; AC-39 (text only, no image) is likely also an HTTP
200, so whether it writes a usage row (cost 0, tokens real) is open here and must not be picked by the Specification (OD-4 C-4.3).
(2) OD-23 is resolved (lead A): the displayed list and the sent list are the same retained list, so "history" means the retained
turns; the question remains only for turns that never entered it.

**v4 cross-note.** (3) Whether the OD-23 cut is applied on an HTTP 200 that created no version (text-only AC-39, and a safety block if
it is returned as a 200 AC-38) is set in OD-23 C-23.5 as "yes, on every 200" and flagged; the safety-block status code itself is
open here. (4) Whether the text and attachments of a message rejected by the OD-24 413 stay in the composer, are lost, or move into
the new session is not decided.

**v5 cross-note.** (5) The review found the turns after the latest version undefined: FR-36 derives the trim unit (M_i, U_i+1) only for a
strictly alternating sequence U1 M1 ... Uk Mk that ends at the latest-version model turn Mk, and says "treatment of any turns after Mk
(for example text-only replies) depends on OD-19". If a text-only reply or a retried user message can sit after Mk, those turns are in
neither the protected set nor a trim unit, so the trim, the exact `trimmedTurnIds` list (OD-23 C-23.3) and the measured remainder
(OD-24 C-24.1) are not defined for them. (6) Sub-case routed to OD-30: a response that is neither a safety block nor a clean image or
text (a truncated or "other" finish reason). Answer OD-30(a) with this entry.

**Impact if unresolved.** The next request's contents differ depending on the guess, and AC-21 (carry every prior turn) conflicts with
AC-40 for a text-only model reply.

**v5 re-classification: blocking: true (was false).** The v4 text records the conflict between two acceptance criteria (AC-21 vs AC-40)
and OD-4 C-4.3 forbids the Specification to pick the usage-row rule for a 200 without an image. More importantly, this entry defines the
content of the history, and the two decisions the human has already taken (OD-7 pair units, OD-23 exact trimmed ids and atomic apply) are
precise only for a defined sequence shape. The Specification writer cannot state FR-36, FR-37 or FR-57 without choosing it.

**v5 blocking text (answered in v6; superseded by the v6 block above).** What the human must give: (a) is a text-only model reply (AC-39) a normal turn that stays in the history (and is
then part of the sequence, and may be a protected or a trimmable turn) or a failed turn that never enters it; (b) does the user's
message stay after a safety block (AC-38) or after a text-only reply, and on Retry is the same user turn re-sent or a new one;
(c) does a cancelled turn (AC-11) count as failed; (d) does a 200 without an image write a `usage_log` row (cost 0, real tokens) -
OD-4 C-4.3; (e) the transport status of a safety block (200 with a finish reason, or a 4xx); (f) what happens to the text and
