attachments of a message rejected by the OD-24 413 (stay in the composer, lost, or move to the new session). Realistic leads, none a
decision: **text-only reply and safety block are non-history events** (history = accepted image turns only; the sequence is always
U1 M1 ... Uk Mk; Retry re-sends the same user turn; a 200 without an image writes no row) - the simplest reading of AC-40 and the one
FR-36 already presumes; **text-only replies stay in the history** (preserves model context for follow-ups, needs a trim rule for turns
after Mk and a decision on AC-40).

**v5 blocking text (answered in v6; superseded by the v6 block above).**

## OD-20 - Criteria that depend on model output (AC-20, AC-47, also AC-10) - RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS (v7; v8: the residues are decided, OD-46)

**v8 cross-note.** The empty-prompt `alt` is the human's literal (T-21); "100 plus `...`, only when cut" is confirmed through CF-9 and SD-35 (OD-46, OD-49); C-20.2 (SD-38) and C-20.3 (SD-35, CF-9) are confirmed.

### v7 resolution (human decision)

*The text below this v7 block is the earlier record, kept for history; this v7 block supersedes its pending state.*

**Human decision (v7, verbatim).** "OD-20 (AC-20 та AC-47): AC-20 (збереження композиції) перевіряється лише мануально. Для AC-47 alt генерується детерміновано: просто беремо промпт і відрізаємо його до 100 символів (з додаванням ...)." (English rendering: AC-20 (composition preserved) is verified manually only. For AC-47 the `alt` is generated deterministically: we simply take the prompt and cut it to 100 characters (adding ...).)

**Verification.** AGENTS.md section 5 forbids network and model in the gate, so "the rest of the composition preserved" can never be asserted in the suite; the human's waiver is therefore the honest outcome. AGENTS.md has no rule forbidding a human waiver of an automated test for one criterion; section 6 item 7 requires only that the AC-to-test matrix reflect what was built, so the waiver must be visible in the matrix (condition 2). AC-47 is a different matter: a deterministic rule can be tested, so it is not waived.

**Conditions the Specification MUST carry.**
- **C-20.1** (Automated-test waivers W-1, hand-off) AC-20's clause "the rest of the composition preserved" is verified manually only; the requirement of an automated test for that clause is waived by the human. The waiver's authority is **this entry (OD-20)** and the Specification must cite it by id in the W-1 heading (review hand-off N-6).
- **C-20.2** (CF-8, FR-34, W-1, SD-38) The AC-to-test matrix row for AC-20 reads "MANUAL (waived by the human, OD-20)". FR-34's assertions stay automated because they test a different, deterministic property: given a session holding v2, a text-only correction sends a request containing v2 as the model turn's image plus the new text, the returned image is stored as v3, and version numbers follow FR-23. Missing manual evidence never blocks reconciliation (OD-6). The manual procedure and the pass criterion (SD-38) are the Specification's derivation, awaiting confirmation.
- **C-20.3** (FR-75, SD-35, CF-9) AC-47's `alt` is the prompt cut to 100 characters with `...` appended: deterministic, not model-generated, not waived. **Readings awaiting confirmation (OD-46):** characters are Unicode code points; the source is trimmed and interior whitespace collapsed before measuring; the ellipsis is three ASCII dots; `...` is appended only when the text is actually cut (the Specification currently lists this implicitly; review hand-off N-5 asks for it to be a named reading), giving 103 code points at most.

**Not decided here:** OD-46 (empty prompt, the 100-versus-103 and "appended only when cut" readings, image-only messages).

**Blocking: false** (decided).

**v6 cross-note.** Unchanged and still OPEN, non-blocking. The new decisions add no criterion that depends on model output.


**Question.** How are AC-20 ("the model edits v2 and returns v3, with the rest of the composition preserved") and AC-47 ("alt text that
is a short summary of its prompt") verified? Is AC-20 satisfied by a request-shape assertion with a stubbed Gemini (v2 present in the
request), and is AC-47's "short summary" a deterministic rule (truncate to N characters, strip the template) or a model-generated
summary?

**Why it cannot be inferred.** Checked AGENTS.md section 5: tests are deterministic with no network, so image content can never be
asserted in the gate; "rest of the composition preserved" is a model-quality property. AC-47 gives no length or method.

**Impact if unresolved.** These criteria cannot be falsified as written, so the AC-to-test matrix would carry a test that asserts
something weaker than the criterion (the failure mode RECONCILIATION checks for).

**Blocking: false.**

## OD-21 - `role="status"` "exactly twice" vs errors, Cancel and notices (AC-46) - RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS (v7; v8: the residue is confirmed, OD-47)

**v8 cross-note.** The channels the human did not name (SD-36) are confirmed (OD-47); C-21.3 (CF-10), C-21.4 (CF-12) and C-21.5 (CF-13) are confirmed with the register (OD-49).

### v7 resolution (human decision)

*Partial resolution for the outcomes the human named. The text below this v7 block is the earlier record, kept for history. The channels of the outcomes the human did not name are carried in OD-47.*

**Human decision (v7, verbatim).** "OD-21 (Анонси role="status"): Помилки, Cancel та повідомлення про обрізання використовують role="alert" (або є звичайним візуальним текстом), щоб не ламати правило "рівно двічі" для role="status" процесу генерації. Після помилки фокус повертається в поле вводу." (English rendering: Errors, Cancel and the truncation message use `role="alert"` (or are plain visual text) so as not to break the "exactly twice" rule for the generation's `role="status"`. After an error focus returns to the input field.)

**Verification.** AC-46 requires exactly two `role="status"` announcements ("Генерую…" then "Версію vN готово"); AC-48 says "after a generation finishes, focus remains in the input field". The human's channels make AC-46 testable for a success. They leave two things for the Specification to read: AC-46 for a generation that ends without a version (there is no "готово"), and AC-48's "remains" against the fact that the input is disabled while a generation runs (FR-21) and in the blocked state (FR-47). Both readings are recorded below, flagged, because they amend AC-46 and AC-48.

**Conditions the Specification MUST carry.**
- **C-21.1** (FR-83, FR-74) Errors, the Cancel notice and the trim notice (T-9) use `role="alert"` or plain visual text, never `role="status"`; the Image Studio contains no `role="status"` or `aria-live="polite"` element other than the generation region.
- **C-21.2** (FR-76, AC-48) After an error focus returns to the input field. **Reading awaiting confirmation:** the same holds after Cancel and after a timeout (an error); the human named errors only.
- **C-21.3** (CF-10, FR-21, FR-47, SD-37) **Reading awaiting confirmation (amends AC-46):** a successful generation makes exactly two `role="status"` updates; a generation that ends without a version makes exactly one ("Генерую…") followed by an alert, and the status region is then cleared (clearing is not an announcement). The input is re-enabled when a generation ends; in the FR-47 blocked state only Send is disabled, so that the focus rule can hold.
- **C-21.4** (CF-12, FR-45) The trim notice follows a successful generation whose polite "готово" announcement it can interrupt; applied as decided, the order is not controlled by the Specification (reading awaiting confirmation).
- **C-21.5** (CF-13) The native quota alert (OD-16) is a third channel and adds no `role="status"` update.

**Not decided here:** OD-47 (safety block, text-only response, FR-47 message, attach rejections, status clearing).

**Blocking: false** (decided).

**v6 cross-note (OD-19, OD-30, OD-13).** More outcomes now need an announcement rule under "exactly twice": the 200 safety envelope and the 200 text-only response (non-history events), the failed turns, the rate-limit 429, the "Помилка API" error, the OD-8 size error and a multi-image response (the second announcement names the highest number). Still OPEN and non-blocking: it is one accessibility rule that can be answered for all of them.


**Question.** AC-46 allows exactly two announcements ("Генерую…" and "Версію vN готово"), none in between. What announces a timeout, an
error, a Cancel, a safety block, a text-only response, the AC-24/AC-29 trimming message, the rate-limit rejection and the AC-3/AC-7
attach rejections? Does the second announcement fire when no version is created? Where does focus go after an error (AC-48 states only
"after a generation finishes")?

**Why it cannot be inferred.** Checked AC-11, AC-24, AC-29, AC-37-39, AC-42, AC-46, AC-48: all describe visible messages, none says how
they are announced, and AC-46's "exactly twice" would be violated by any additional live region update.

**v3 cross-note.** One request can now owe up to two trim notices (size trim then token trim, OD-7 C-7.5), plus the 501 missing-key,
411/413 and expired-file messages (OD-3, OD-11, OD-7); how each is announced under "exactly twice" is open here.

**v4 cross-note.** Adds the OD-24 blocked-state message and the permanent-deletion wording of the trim notice (OD-23 C-23.6).

**Impact if unresolved.** Either error paths are silent for screen-reader users or AC-46's test (count of status updates) fails by
design.

**Blocking: false.**

## OD-22 - Storage of "preset used" and "downloaded" (AC-19) - RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS (v6; preset half settled by OD-4)

### v6 resolution (human decision, conditions)

*The text below this v6 block is the v5 record of the question, kept for history. Where it says OPEN or `Blocking: true`, this v6 block supersedes it.*

**Human decision (v6, verbatim).** "OD-22: Запис лише на клієнті — булевий прапорець у локальному стані IndexedDB при завантаженні (файл, ZIP, копіювання); без нових таблиць/ендпоінтів BFF." (English rendering: Recorded on the client only - a boolean flag in the local IndexedDB state when the image is downloaded (file, ZIP, copy); no new tables or BFF endpoints.) The human is the decider; this stage chose nothing.

**Tension point 5 (caller): AC-19's intent cannot be evaluated server-side.** AC-19 says "the log records which preset was used and whether the resulting image was downloaded", and the Story's intent (recorded in the v1 entry) is to judge how useful a preset is. A flag held only in one browser's IndexedDB is never sent to the BFF, is absent from `usage_log` and `GET /api/usage`, cannot be joined with the server-side preset record (`task_label`), cannot be aggregated across users or devices, and disappears with the session (OD-23 trim, the 7-day expiry, the user's clearing site data). **The "downloaded" half of AC-19 is therefore narrowed from a log to a per-device UI flag. This is an accepted limitation recorded here, not a silent loss.** Preset usefulness can be judged on the server only by generation counts per preset (the `task_label` half, successful generations).

**Conditions the Specification MUST carry.**
- C-22.1 **AC-19 amendment.** AC-19 is restated as two parts: (i) the server records the preset (and resolution) of each HTTP 200 generation that contains an image, in `usage_log.task_label` (OD-4, OD-19); (ii) the client stores, per version, a boolean "downloaded" flag in the IndexedDB session state. The Story's "the log records ... whether the image was downloaded" is waived to (ii), with the limitation above. This closes the v5 requirement that the second half of AC-19 carry a requirement or an explicit waiver.
- C-22.2 **When it is set.** On a completed single-file download, on a completed ZIP download (every version included in the ZIP is flagged), and on a completed copy to the clipboard (AC-34), not on the click. A failed save or a failed clipboard write leaves it unset (the Specification states the success signal for each).
- C-22.3 Nothing is sent to the BFF: no new endpoint, no new table, no schema change (human). The flag lives in the same session record, so it is deleted when its version is trimmed or the session expires; it is not preserved after OD-23 deletions.
- C-22.4 What the UI does with the flag (a badge, a warning before a trim, nothing) is not decided and not required by any AC; the Specification does not invent a use. The flag is covered by a test of the three triggers.

**Blocking: false** (decided).


**Question.** Where is AC-19 recorded ("The log records which preset was used and whether the resulting image was downloaded")? A
server table, the existing console log, or the client only? A download happens in the browser; the BFF does not see it unless the client
reports it.

**Why it cannot be inferred.** Checked `server/usage/store.js`: `usage_log` has no preset or download column; `generation_log` has a
different grain; the existing precedent is the client reporting an outcome to `POST /api/usage/generation`. No repository source says
the app reports downloads anywhere. Related to OD-4 (the no-migration rule means a new table, not a new column).

**v2 cross-note.** If the OD-4 schema part stands (preset and resolution in `task_label`), the **"which preset was used"** half of
AC-19 is carried by the usage row of each generation, with no extra mechanism. The **"was the image downloaded"** half is not: a
download is a client event the BFF never sees, so it still needs a reporting mechanism or a client-only record. That half stays open.

**v3 cross-note.** OD-4 (v3) narrows the preset half: it is recorded in `task_label` only for successful generations, so a failed or
cancelled attempt leaves no preset record. The "downloaded" half remains open.

**Impact if unresolved.** A new reporting endpoint, a new table, or only a client-side note are three different features; "the log" is
undefined for the download half.

**v5 reassessment (SPEC_REVIEW finding "AC-19 downloaded half").** Confirmed: the Specification (FR-29) states no requirement for the
download half, so AC-19 has a half with no FR. This is a genuine owner decision (client-only record, a new reporting endpoint, or a new
table; the last two change the BFF surface and the usage store). Three further facts for the owner: the download is a client event the BFF
never sees; a "download" has several forms (single file, any format, copy to clipboard AC-34, ZIP AC-35) and the Story does not say which
counts as "downloaded"; and per OD-23 a version can be permanently deleted before it is ever downloaded.

**Blocking: false.** Why it stays non-blocking in v5: it concerns one half of one criterion, no other requirement is shaped by it, and the
smallest reading (a client-side record) needs no BFF or schema change. The Specification must still carry a requirement for the
half or an explicit waiver before `HUMAN_SPEC_APPROVAL`; leaving AC-19's second half with no requirement and no waiver is not acceptable.

---

## OD-23 - How the client learns what the stateless BFF trimmed (promoted from OD-11(b); AC-21/24/25/29/30) - RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS

### v6 cross-note: the human's two explicit confirmations

The human explicitly confirmed (relayed in English by the caller; no Ukrainian verbatim was supplied, as for the v4 decision):
1. **Deleting the user's own mid-session uploads when a pair is trimmed is intended.** The pair unit (OD-7, FR-36) removes the user turn as well as the model turn, and a removed user turn may carry images the user attached mid-session. The v5 cross-note (1) is closed by this confirmation.
2. **The deletion of trimmed versions is irreversible and no download is offered first** (the question the v4 entry left for `HUMAN_SPEC_APPROVAL`). The notice (C-23.6) stays: it must say that the removed versions **and messages, with the images those messages carried**, are permanently deleted from this device, and how many.

Effect on the Specification: FR-39's notice wording is fixed once for versions, messages and attachments (hand-off 11); no "download before removal" feature is to be specified. Two edges stay as they were and are not changed by this confirmation: C-23.5 (the cut is applied on every 200 that carries the list, including the non-history variants of OD-19; flagged), and the C-23.1 default for a malformed list (the whole response is an error; flagged). The headroom arithmetic C-23.7 now uses the OD-27 B measure (originals weigh a reference) and must include the AC-22 attachment copy and a session seed (OD-27 C-27.3, C-27.6, OD-17 C-17.2).


**Question.** Because the BFF keeps no session and the client re-sends its full IndexedDB history on every turn, by which one
mechanism does the client learn which turns the BFF trimmed (AC-24, AC-29), so that the next request body stays below the 30 MB
cap (AC-30)?

**Human decision (v4, verbatim; chosen lead A).** "OD-23 (blocking) — chosen: variant A. The BFF trims per AC-24 (80% tokens) /
AC-29 (18 MB) and must tell the client what it removed: a successful (HTTP 200) response carries an extra array, e.g.
`trimmed_turn_ids: [...]`. The client, on receiving it, permanently deletes those turns from its local IndexedDB, so the next
request body shrinks and never hits the 30 MB pre-parser cap (OD-11)." (The caller relayed the decision in English; the human's
original wording was Ukrainian and was not re-supplied.) The human is the decider; this stage chose nothing.

**Why it was blocking (v3, kept for the record).** The BFF is stateless (`server/` has no session store); the client re-sends the
full history (AC-21, AC-25); the BFF trims only its outbound copy (AC-24, AC-29); AC-30 / OD-11 reject a body over 30 MB before the
BFF can read it. Lead A removes the divergence by making the client's stored history equal to the retained history, which needs no
server state: the BFF's trim is a pure function of the request body, and the client applies the result.

**Verification (the caller's items).**
- **Mechanism is sound and needs no BFF state.** Consistent with `server/` having no session store, with AC-25 (IndexedDB is the
  only persistent copy) and with Rule 5 (no existing mode is touched). It changes the generate response contract, so AC-14's Zod
  schema covers the new field. A non-200 response carries no list, so nothing is deleted on an error.
- **Contradiction with AC-21 (history fidelity).** AC-21 ("every request carries all prior turns") is narrowed to "all
  **retained** turns"; it was already narrowed by AC-24 / AC-29 on the wire, and now also at rest. The `thoughtSignature` fidelity
  of the retained turns is unchanged (OD-7). Recorded as an override.
- **Contradiction with AC-25 (persistence).** The persisted session is the retained session; reload shows no deleted turn. Not a
  conflict with "survives a reload / 7 days", but the stored content is smaller than the Story implies. Recorded.
- **Contradiction with AC-23 / AC-35 / AC-22 (strip, ZIP, Edit this version).** These three promise "all session versions", "the
  whole session" and "every earlier version". Under A a trimmed version is **permanently gone** from the device: it leaves the strip,
  the ZIP and the "Edit this version" action. This is a deliberate, server-initiated, irreversible data loss of generated work
  that the user may not yet have downloaded. The human chose "permanently deletes"; the stage records it and does not soften it.
  Recorded as overrides; the AC-24 / AC-29 notice must say the removal is permanent (see C-23.6). Whether the user is offered a
  download of the versions about to be removed is **not** decided and not invented here; it is listed for the human at
  `HUMAN_SPEC_APPROVAL`.
- **AC-26 / AC-27 (session list, branches).** Not contradicted. Trimming acts on the session being sent; other sessions in the AC-26
  list are untouched; with AC-27 each branch is its own history and its own turn-id space. Switching model seeds the new branch
  with the latest version only, which is itself a size reset. Branch/session semantics remain OD-17.
- **The first oversized request (caller's central question).** Can the very first request after many turns already exceed the
  30 MB pre-parser cap, so that the BFF never gets to trim it?
  - **Lost response, offline, abort, reload: no.** If the response never reaches the client (connection drop, Cancel, tab closed,
    client offline), no cut is applied and no version is added, so the client state is exactly the pre-request state. The next
    request is a replay of a body that was already accepted under the cap, and the BFF's trim is deterministic, so it trims the same
    turns again. A lost response never makes the body bigger. The cost is paid twice in that case (the BFF may have logged a usage
    row, OD-4 C-4.6), not a size problem.
  - **Non-atomic apply: yes, unless closed.** If the client stores the new model version but crashes before deleting the listed
    turns, the next body is the old uncut history plus one full-size version. This is why the apply must be one transaction (C-23.3).
  - **Headroom: yes, this is reachable.** The BFF's 18 MB size trim is computed on the outbound request **before** the model
    answers. The client's next body is the retained history **plus the version just generated** plus the new user turn. The
    margin between the 18 MB trim target and the 30 MB cap is therefore the room for one generated version and one new turn. The
    units are not aligned: 18 MB (AC-29, OD-7) is the request assembled for Gemini, 30 MB (AC-30, OD-11) is the JSON body on the
    wire, where binary images are base64 (about 4/3). In the worst reading 18 MB of binary is about 24 MB on the wire, leaving about
    6 MB for a 4K version (image sizes are not measured anywhere in this repo and the 4K size is a vendor fact). If a generated
    version can exceed the margin, a long session reaches a body over 30 MB after a **successful** turn whose cut was applied
    correctly. That is the residual failure mode of A; it is closed only by arithmetic the Specification must show (C-23.7).
  - **First-upload path (relevant to the new turn).** AC-3 allows 5 attachments of up to 5 MB (AC-4) = 25 MB binary = about 33 MB as
    base64, which alone exceeds the 30 MB cap if sent in one JSON body. AC-28 moves user uploads to the Files API (so the generate
    body carries a `file_uri`), but the first-upload endpoint is undefined (OD-7 C-7.6). Its request granularity must keep every
    body below the cap (C-23.8).

**Conditions the Specification MUST carry.**
- C-23.1 **Contract.** Every HTTP 200 response of the generate route carries the removed-turn list: a field whose name the
  Specification fixes (the human wrote `trimmed_turn_ids` as an example; the existing API uses camelCase, so `trimmedTurnIds`
  follows OD-4 C-4.5), always present, an empty array when nothing was removed. It is part of the Zod response schema (AC-14): array
  of non-empty strings, with a bounded length. The Specification states what a malformed list does: the literal AC-14 reading
  (the whole response is an error, a paid image is not shown) or "drop the list, keep the image"; the literal reading is the default
  and the alternative is flagged for the human at approval. Both halves of a removed user+model pair are listed (OD-7).
- C-23.2 **Turn identity.** Every turn in the request body carries a stable, client-generated, session-scoped id that is persisted in
  IndexedDB; the BFF only echoes ids it received and never invents or renumbers one. The Specification defines the id format and
  its scope (per session, and per branch under AC-27).
- C-23.3 **Atomic apply.** The client appends the new model turn and deletes the listed turns in a **single IndexedDB transaction**,
  and updates in-memory state only after the commit. A response that is not applied (Cancel, abort, reload, offline, schema failure,
  commit failure) leaves the session exactly as before, so the next request is a safe replay. The Specification states that the BFF
  trim is a deterministic function of the request body (no clock, no randomness), because replay relies on it, and tests it.
- C-23.4 **Client guard.** The client ignores ids that are not in its local session, and never deletes the first user turn (the
  originals) or the latest model version even if a response lists them; such a response is handled as an invalid response (C-23.1
  default) and logged. The Specification defines the handling.
- C-23.5 **When it applies.** The client applies the cut on **every** HTTP 200 that carries the list, whether or not a version was
  created (a text-only response AC-39, and a safety block if it is returned as a 200, AC-38, OD-19). Non-200 responses never delete
  anything. Whether this is the intended behaviour for a 200 without a version is flagged for the human at approval (the cut is a
  loss with no new version to show for it, but the body-size reason for the cut is unchanged).
- C-23.6 **Story-text amendments** (also in the cumulative overrides table): AC-21, AC-23, AC-22, AC-35, AC-25 as above; the
  AC-24 / AC-29 user notice must state that the removed versions are **permanently deleted from this device**, and how many; the
  strip, "Edit this version" and "Download all versions" cover retained versions only. The notice itself is one user-visible message
  per request, or two if both limits fired (OD-21).
- C-23.7 **Headroom arithmetic.** The Specification fixes the unit of each of 18 MB, 30 MB and the 20 MB inline cap (decoded bytes
  vs base64 JSON bytes), states the largest generated version and the largest new user turn it assumes, and shows
  `retained-history-after-trim + largest response version + largest new turn <= 30 MB` with a stated margin. The largest response
  version comes from check **V-6** (measured, not assumed). If the inequality cannot be shown for a 4K response with the human's
  18 MB and 30 MB, the Specification writer MUST NOT choose a different number: that is a new Open Decision for the human (the cap,
  the trim target or the 4K allowance changes).
- C-23.8 **Request granularity.** No request to `/api/image-studio/*` may legitimately exceed the cap: the Specification states the
  first-upload granularity (for example one image per request) so that 5 attachments of up to 5 MB do not form one over-cap body.
- C-23.9 **Tests (stubbed Gemini, no network).** (a) BFF: the returned list equals exactly the turns removed, for the size rule, the
  token rule, both together, and the OD-7 fallback; same input gives the same list. (b) Client: a 200 with a list deletes exactly those
  turns atomically; a response that is not applied changes nothing. (c) A simulated long session with deterministic maximum-size
  versions keeps every request body below the cap on every turn, and the test fails if the headroom inequality is broken.
- C-23.10 The residual failure (a body rejected by the pre-parser despite A) is **OD-25**; it is not closed here.

**v5 cross-note (consequences surfaced by SPEC_REVIEW; decision unchanged).** (1) The pair unit removes the **user turn** as well as the
model turn (OD-7, FR-36). A removed user turn may carry images the user attached mid-session (other than the first turn), so the
permanent deletion also removes those uploaded images, not only generated versions. The human decided "permanently deletes" for versions;
whether deleting a user's own later uploads is intended is not stated. It is listed with the other permanent-deletion items for the human at
`HUMAN_SPEC_APPROVAL` and is not a new decision here. (2) The FR-39 notice wording for a removed pair (versions and messages, with or
without their attachments) is the Specification writer's to fix once (1) is confirmed. (3) C-23.2 (turn-id scope) is completed by
OD-17 (blocking in v5). (4) The headroom arithmetic C-23.7 now also depends on OD-27: the 18 MB measure of file-referenced originals
changes what the retained history weighs.

**Blocking: false** (decided; the open edges are conditions, V-6 and OD-25).

## OD-24 - The protected history alone exceeds the size or token limit (AC-24, AC-29) - RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS

**v6 note (OD-27, OD-17, OD-19, OD-13).** (1) The measured remainder of C-24.1 is now defined: `file_uri` originals weigh their reference only (OD-27 B); the byte test sees inline content, the token test sees everything. (2) The exit (C-24.6) is settled: in the blocked state **every retained version** offers "Edit this version", which creates a **new session** (OD-17 (a), (g)); the old session stays for viewing and download. (3) The text and attachments of the rejected message stay in the composer (OD-19(f)); no turn, no deletion, no row, and no rate-limit unit (OD-13 C-13.2). (4) The 413 and the 413 `REQUEST_BODY_TOO_LARGE` are two of the BFF-reserved responses of OD-29 C-29.1. (5) The seed of a blocked-state exit is inline and protected in the new session (OD-17 C-17.2); a seed that alone exceeds the limits ends in the same error, which C-24.6 already requires the Specification to state. The statuses and decisions of v4 are unchanged.


**Question.** What happens when, after removing every droppable turn, the protected set (first user turn with originals, the latest
model version, and the new user turn with its attachments) still exceeds 18 MB or 80% of the model's input limit?

**Human decision (v4, verbatim as relayed in English).** "OD-24 (non-blocking) — rule: if even the protected remainder (original
uploads + latest version) exceeds 18 MB or 80% of the token limit, the BFF stops processing and returns a special error, e.g. HTTP
413 Payload Too Large with body `{ error: "PROTECTED_HISTORY_EXCEEDS_LIMIT" }`. The client UI intercepts it and, instead of the
usual trimming message, shows: "Сесія занадто велика. Будь ласка, почніть нову сесію з останньої версії", blocking further text
edits in the current branch until the user clicks "Edit this version" (which creates a new branch/session, leaving the old
originals behind)."

**Verification.**
- **Consistent with the Story and AGENTS.md.** AC-24 / AC-29 say originals and the latest version are always kept; the rule adds the
  terminating case they lacked. No dependency, no FROZEN file, no architecture rule is touched. The message is Ukrainian, which
  matches the owner's convention (the language question for the other messages stays OD-14).
- **413 is distinguishable only by the body, not by the status (caller's question).** The OD-11 pre-parser rejection (body over
  30 MB) is expected to use 413 too (C-11.2: the human gave no code for it; 413 is the standard one and `express.json` itself uses
  it). `server/index.js` has no error-handling middleware (checked: no `(err, req, res, next)` handler), so a 413 raised by the
  global `express.json` (a lying `Content-Length` between 30 and 50 MB) reaches the client as Express's default non-JSON body, not as
  `{ error: string }`. Three different 413 situations therefore exist, and the status alone cannot tell them apart.
- **Retry interplay (caller's question).** `isUpstreamTransportFailure` in `src/services/http-retry.ts` retries only status 0, 500,
  502, 503, 504, and never a response that carries the `{ error: string }` envelope. A 413 is in neither set, so `retryTransport`
  does not retry it. AC-37's BFF retry covers upstream 429/5xx only; the 413 is raised by the BFF before any upstream call, so it is
  not retried there either. Verified from the source; the Specification must still pin it with a test (C-24.4).
- **AC-40 (caller's question).** A failed turn never enters the history. A 413 is a non-200, so under OD-23 the client deletes
  nothing and adds neither the user turn nor a model turn. What happens to the text the user typed and the attachments is OD-19
  territory and is not decided here.
- **The "Edit this version" exit changes AC-22.** AC-22 attaches a version to the **next message of the same session**. Here the same
  label starts a new branch/session seeded with a version and leaves the originals behind; this is AC-27's seeding semantics under
  AC-22's name. Whether the new thing is a branch in the same session or a new row in the AC-26 list is OD-17 (open) and this
  decision depends on it.
- **The stated counts omit the new user turn.** The human's wording lists the originals and the latest version; the new turn (text
  and attachments) is sent and can never be trimmed, so it belongs in the measured remainder (OD-7 C-7.4).

**Conditions the Specification MUST carry.**
- C-24.1 **Measured remainder** = first user turn (originals) + latest model version + the new user turn with attachments, checked
  against the 18 MB size limit and then the 80% token limit, in that order (OD-7 C-7.5). Exceeding either returns the error.
- C-24.2 **Before any generation.** The check runs before the Gemini generate call: nothing is generated, billed or logged
  (OD-4: only HTTP 200 writes `usage_log`). Whether the request counts toward the rate limit is OD-13.
- C-24.3 **Discriminator.** The client dispatches on status **and** the body code, never the status alone: status 413 with
  `error` strictly equal to `PROTECTED_HISTORY_EXCEEDS_LIMIT` is the protected-history state. The OD-11 pre-parser rejection MUST
  carry a different, distinct code (the Specification defines the constant, for example one for "request body too large") in the same
  `{ error: string }` envelope, and the client treats any 413 without the protected code (including Express's non-JSON 413) as the
  generic error of OD-25, never as the protected state. The codes are closed constants shared through the Zod schema. `error`
  doubles as the human-readable string in the existing envelope, so the client must never render a code to the user; it shows the
  literal below. If the human prefers a separate `code` field, that is a change of the human's example and is raised, not made, by the
  Specification.
- C-24.4 **Not retried.** The 413 is not retried by `retryTransport` or by the BFF and shows no Retry button (the same reasoning as
  OD-3 N-3.1). A test asserts exactly one request is made.
- C-24.5 **UI.** The message is the human's literal: "Сесія занадто велика. Будь ласка, почніть нову сесію з останньої версії"
  (displayed instead of the trimming notice); language handling under the English UI is OD-14 and the announcement is OD-21. While
  the state holds, sending a text message in the current branch is disabled; viewing, the strip, download, copy and "New session"
  stay available. The Specification states whether the blocked state survives a reload (persisted flag) or is rediscovered by the
  next send (stateless, the next 413 re-creates it); either is consistent, the choice is flagged at approval.
- C-24.6 **Exit.** "Edit this version" is the only action that re-enables sending. In this state it must be offered at least on the
  latest version (the message names "the last version"); whether older retained versions also offer it is **not** the Specification's
  call: **v5 amendment** - it is OD-17 sub-question (g), to be answered by the human (the v4 text said "the Specification's call under
  AC-22", which contradicted the v4 OD-17 cross-note; the contradiction is removed here). The new branch/session is seeded with the chosen version as its input and does **not** carry the old originals;
  the seed plus the first new turn is itself subject to C-24.1, and a seed that alone exceeds the limit ends in the same error, so the
  Specification states that case (no infinite loop of the exit). Depends on OD-17.
- C-24.7 Cross-references: the 413 handling and its test live with OD-11 (C-11.5) so that both 413 cases are tested side by side.

**v5 note (decision unchanged).** C-24.1's "measured remainder" is only well-defined once **OD-27** (how a `file_uri` original counts)
is answered; the review showed that one reading makes this 413 fire on a legitimate first message that the exit of C-24.6 cannot
recover. C-24.6 is amended above (OD-17 g). The 413 is part of the error taxonomy of OD-29.

**Blocking: false** (decided; but its exit and its measure depend on OD-17 and OD-27, both blocking in v5).

## OD-25 - Recovery when the generate body is rejected above 30 MB despite OD-23/A (AC-30, OD-11) - RESOLVED (v7)

### v7 resolution (human decision)

*The text below this v7 block is the earlier record, kept for history. The "Lead (not a decision)" below (reuse the OD-24 blocked state) was **not** adopted; the human accepted the derivable default instead.*

**Human decision (v7, verbatim).** "OD-25 (Відхилення тіла понад 30 MB): Приймаю PD-25 (показуємо стандартну помилку без кнопки Retry, хід не йде в історію)." (English rendering: Rejection of a body over 30 MB: I accept PD-25 (we show the standard error without a Retry button; the turn does not enter the history).)

**Verification.** PD-25 is exactly the "derivable default" the v5/v6 entry recorded. It is consistent with the OD-29 taxonomy and with `src/services/http-retry.ts`, which does not retry a 413. No contradiction found.

**Notes the Specification carries (consequences, not new questions).** (FR-52) A 413 whose `error` is not `PROTECTED_HISTORY_EXCEEDS_LIMIT` (that is, `REQUEST_BODY_TOO_LARGE`, or Express's own non-JSON 413) is a generic non-retryable error showing T-8: no Retry button, the failed turn does not enter the history, nothing is deleted, "New session" remains available; a 413 is not in the status set `retryTransport` retries (test asserts it); the request is not a counted request (OD-13).

**Blocking: false** (decided).

**v6 cross-note (OD-29).** Still OPEN and non-blocking. The derivable default (a non-retryable generic error, no Retry, no history entry, nothing deleted) is consistent with the OD-29 taxonomy: an over-30 MB rejection or Express's own non-JSON 413 is shown as the generic error; the request is not counted by the rate limit (OD-13(d)).


**Question.** If a request to the generate route is rejected by the OD-11 pre-parser (413, body over 30 MB, or Express's own 413), what
does the client do: show a generic non-retryable error only, enter the same blocked state and exit as OD-24, or something else?

**Why it cannot be inferred.** Checked the human's OD-23 and OD-24 answers: OD-23/A is meant to make this unreachable, and OD-24 covers
only the protected-history error returned by the BFF; neither says what happens when the body is rejected before the BFF can read it.
It is reachable only if the C-23.7 headroom arithmetic fails or a session predates the cut; nothing in the repo defines a recovery
(`src/services/http-retry.ts` does not retry a 413, `server/index.js` has no error handler).

**Impact if unresolved.** The Specification would invent a client recovery for a paid, irreversible-feeling failure. The derivable
default, until the human answers, is the minimal one: show a non-retryable error (the existing envelope, no Retry button, the
failed turn does not enter the history, nothing is deleted); the user can still use "New session" (AC-26). The Specification may
state that default but must flag it as unconfirmed.

**Blocking: false.** Lead (not a decision): reuse the OD-24 blocked state and "Edit this version" exit, since the client already
knows how to recover a too-large session; the human decides whether that is acceptable.

**v5 note.** Stays non-blocking: a derivable default exists (the Specification carries it as a labelled PROPOSED DEFAULT in FR-46) and the
case is reachable only if the headroom arithmetic of OD-23 C-23.7 fails. Its status code and error code are also part of the error
taxonomy of OD-29.

---

## v5 entries (new in this run)

The entries below come from the SPEC_REVIEW loop-back. For each: the question, what was checked, the impact if unresolved, and
`blocking`. Lines starting "Lead (not a decision)" or "Default (not a decision)" are aids for the human, never answers.

## OD-26 - Ukrainian names (and ids) of the five presets (AC-16, AC-17; Specification O-1) - RESOLVED (v6)

### v6 resolution (human decision)

*The text below this v6 block is the v5 record of the question, kept for history. Where it says OPEN or `Blocking: true`, this v6 block supersedes it.*

**Human decision (v6, verbatim).** "OD-26: UA назви: "Білий фон", "Лайфстайл", "Банер", "Соцмережі", "Деталь крупним планом". IDs: white-bg, lifestyle, banner, social, detail-closeup." (English rendering: Ukrainian names "White background", "Lifestyle", "Banner", "Social networks", "Close-up detail"; ids `white-bg`, `lifestyle`, `banner`, `social`, `detail-closeup`.) The human is the decider; this stage chose nothing. The supplied values equal the unconfirmed lead the v5 entry recorded.

**Verification.** The five EN names are Story AC-17's (White background, Lifestyle, Banner, Social, Detail close-up), one-to-one with the five ids in AC-17's order. The ids are lowercase kebab-case and contain no `|`, so they are safe in the `task_label` format `image-studio|<presetId>|<resolution>` (Specification SD-8). A search of `src/` and `server/` finds no existing `PROMPT_PRESETS`, `white-bg` or "Лайфстайл", so nothing collides.

**Notes the Specification carries (no further decision).** (1) The ids are stable keys: they appear in `usage_log.task_label` and the dashboard buckets (OD-4 C-4.7, OD-35), so renaming one later changes historical grouping. (2) The registry holds both names per preset (AC-16); which one the UI shows is OD-14 (open). (3) The prompt-template wording, the `{product}` handling and the registry location remain the Specification's (OD-15, open); the template text is derivable from the AC-17 directives and flagged for approval. (4) The registry test asserts exactly these five ids, UA names and the AC-17 defaults.

**Blocking: false** (decided).


**Question.** What are the Ukrainian display names of the five MVP presets - White background, Lifestyle, Banner, Social, Detail
close-up - and what are their five `id` values?

**Why it cannot be inferred.** Checked Story AC-16 ("each preset has `id`, a UA name and an EN name") and AC-17 (English names, defaults
and directives only), the Story's References (the source note is not in the repository) and the Story's own Open questions (none covers
this). Searched `src/` and `server/` for a `PROMPT_PRESETS` registry and for Ukrainian preset vocabulary: neither exists (the only match
for a related Ukrainian word is an unrelated test fixture). The nearest Ukrainian vocabulary is the four chips of AC-18. The only trace
of an id is the OD-4 `task_label` example `image-studio|white-bg|2K`, which that entry marks as an example. The Specification (FR-27, O-1)
confirms the names are not in the Story.

**Impact if unresolved.** AC-16 requires each preset to carry a UA name and AC-17 says the registry contains "exactly these presets":
the registry test cannot be written without literal strings, and shipping user-facing copy that the Specification writer invented is the
guess AGENTS.md section 11 forbids. The ids are visible beyond the registry: they go into `task_label` (one dashboard bucket per preset,
OD-4 C-4.7). Which of the two names the UI shows is OD-14, but both must exist.

**v5 blocking text (answered in v6; superseded by the v6 block above).** The human must give: the UA name of each of the five presets, and either the five ids or "the Specification may
derive them from the EN name". Lead (not a decision, unconfirmed, for the human to accept or replace): UA names "Білий фон", "Лайфстайл",
"Банер", "Соцмережі", "Деталь крупним планом"; ids `white-bg`, `lifestyle`, `banner`, `social`, `detail-closeup` (the first follows the OD-4
example). The prompt-template wording is derivable from the AC-17 directives and stays a Specification-fixed value flagged at approval.

## OD-27 - How `file_uri` originals count toward the size and token measures (AC-3, AC-4, AC-28, AC-29, OD-24 C-24.1; Specification O-2) - RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS (v6)

### v6 resolution (human decision, conditions)

*The text below this v6 block is the v5 record of the question, kept for history. Where it says OPEN or `Blocking: true`, this v6 block supersedes it.*

**Human decision (v6, verbatim).** "OD-27: (a) Варіант (B): до ліміту 18 MB та захищеного залишку рахується лише серіалізований розмір самого посилання file_uri (кількасот байт). Практичне обмеження для оригіналів — ліміт токенів. Ліміт 18 MB обмежує inline-дані (згенеровані версії). (b) AC-22 "Edit this version": прикріплена версія відправляється inline. (c) AC-3/AC-4 змінювати не потрібно." (English rendering: (a) variant B: only the serialized size of the `file_uri` reference itself (a few hundred bytes) counts toward the 18 MB limit and the protected remainder. The practical limit on originals is the token limit. The 18 MB limit bounds inline data (generated versions). (b) AC-22 "Edit this version": the attached version is sent inline. (c) AC-3 / AC-4 need not change.) The human is the decider; this stage chose nothing.

**Tension point 1 (caller), checked.**
- *What now drives `PROTECTED_HISTORY_EXCEEDS_LIMIT` (OD-24).* The measured remainder (C-24.1) is: first user turn with its originals + latest model version + the new user turn. Under B the byte test (18 MB) sees only **inline** content in that set: the latest generated version, an AC-22 attachment and, in a seeded session, the seed; originals contribute only the reference. The token test (80%) still sees the originals, because `countTokens` is expected to count image content (V-7). So the 413 is driven by (i) tokens (originals + retained inline versions + the new turn) and (ii) inline bytes. The dead end of v5 (five 5 MB originals = 25 MB > 18 MB on the first message, with no version to start the exit from) disappears: a first message's remainder is a few reference strings plus text.
- *Do five large uploads fit the token limit?* The Story's limits are 131 072 tokens (Flash) and 65 536 (Pro), so 80% is 104 857 and 52 428. Five originals fit as long as each costs less than about 10 000 tokens (52 428 / 5) on Pro, leaving room for the rest. That per-image cost is not stated anywhere in the repository (the `@google/genai` typings only show that media-resolution levels exist: LOW 64, MEDIUM 256, HIGH 256 with reframing tokens, and a per-part ULTRA_HIGH with no figure), so the fit is an inference to be **measured under V-7 (i)**, not a fact. The AC-3 / AC-4 numbers therefore stand (human (c)).
- *Upload endpoint granularity (30 MB / one image per request).* Specification SD-7 uploads one image per request as base64 JSON. A 5 MB image is about 6.7 MB as base64, under the 30 MB cap (OD-11) and under the Story Context's "up to 7 MB per base64 image". Five uploads are five separate bodies, none near the cap (OD-23 C-23.8 holds). The 410 re-upload (OD-7) has the same granularity.
- *Consequence for the generate body.* The client's generate body carries `file_uri` references, not bytes, for originals; the 30 MB cap is therefore driven by inline versions and an inline attachment.
- *Vendor risk.* B assumes referenced content does not count toward the vendor's request cap. Nothing in the repository establishes this; V-6 (ii) records it. **If V-6 (ii) shows referenced content counts, this entry is re-opened as blocking in the next run** (the 18 MB measure and the first-message dead end return).
- *Inline versions versus the 7 MB figure.* Every generated version is resent inline, and the AC-22 attachment is inline (human (b)); a 4K version may exceed the Story's "7 MB per base64 image" or the 18 MB measure. That is a vendor fact, recorded as V-6 (iii) (extended in v6); if it fails the headroom arithmetic, the human decides (OD-23 C-23.7 pattern), the Specification does not choose a number.

**Conditions the Specification MUST carry.**
- C-27.1 **The measure.** For the 18 MB trim (AC-29) and the OD-24 byte test: inline image bytes (generated versions, the AC-22 attachment, a session seed) + the serialized size of each `file_uri` part + text. The Specification fixes the unit of the inline measure (decoded bytes or base64 JSON bytes, V-6) and makes the Definitions section and FR-34 agree.
- C-27.2 **The token test is unchanged in form.** It counts originals through `countTokens` (OD-32, V-7); the OD-24 413 may therefore still fire on tokens alone, with an exit that depends on OD-17.
- C-27.3 **Attachment (b).** The AC-22 version is sent inline in the new user turn, never via the Files API; AC-28 ("user-uploaded images") applies to user uploads only. Per AC-21 the copy stays in that user turn and is resent inline on later requests while the turn is retained, so a version attached once is carried twice (as the model turn and in the user turn) until trimmed. This is the worst case for the 18 MB measure and is included in the C-23.7 arithmetic. *Derived from AC-21; the human did not say whether the stored user turn keeps the copy.*
- C-27.4 **Originals stay recoverable.** The history stores the `file_uri`; the originals' bytes stay in IndexedDB for the 410 re-upload (OD-7), at most 5 MB each (AC-4, OD-8).
- C-27.5 **No Story number changes.** AC-3 (5 images) and AC-4 (5 MB, 2048 px) stand; the 18 MB number stands and bounds inline data.
- C-27.6 **Seeded session.** The seed is inline in the protected first user turn (OD-17 C-17.2), so a seeded session's protected remainder is seed + latest version + new turn, all inline.
- C-27.7 **Pre-implementation checks.** V-6 (ii) and (iii) and V-7 (i) and (iv) feed this decision; their results are recorded where OD-33 says, before IMPLEMENTATION.

**Blocking: false** (decided; re-open trigger: V-6 (ii)).


**Question.** For the 18 MB request-size trim (AC-29, FR-34), the 80% token trim (AC-24, FR-35) and the OD-24 protected-remainder check
(C-24.1, FR-40), what does a user original that the history holds as a Files API `file_uri` contribute: (A) its **decoded size**,
(B) the **serialized size of the reference** (a few hundred bytes), or something else? And, as the same measurement question, is the
version attached by "Edit this version" (AC-22) sent inline (counted in the request bytes) or uploaded to the Files API first?

**Why it cannot be inferred.** Checked: Story AC-28 (originals go to the Files API once, the history stores the `file_uri`), AC-29 ("the
BFF computes the request size"), AC-3 and AC-4 (up to 5 images, each downscaled when over 5 MB); the Story Context ("total inline request
size 20 MB (larger goes through the Files API)", which suggests referenced files are not part of that cap); OD-24 C-24.1 and OD-7
- **C-7.4** ("originals" in the protected set); the Specification Definitions (18 MB "measures the serialized request the BFF assembles for
Gemini"). The two readings contradict each other inside the log and the Specification. `server/` holds no session store and the generate
request carries only the `file_uri`, so the BFF cannot learn a decoded size unless the client sends it (untrusted metadata) or the BFF
makes an extra Files API lookup per original (a network call; not decided). No repository source says which the vendor applies; V-6
(extended in v5) records whether file-referenced content counts toward the 20 MB cap at all.

**The contradiction in numbers.** Under (A), five originals at the AC-3 / AC-4 maximum (5 MB each, before OD-8) are 25 MB, which already
exceeds 18 MB on the **first** message: FR-40 would return `PROTECTED_HISTORY_EXCEEDS_LIMIT` for a legitimate request, and the
OD-24 exit ("Edit this version") cannot recover it because a first message has no version to start the exit from, so a core feature
would be unusable in its normal five-image case. Under (B) the 18 MB measure is almost empty for originals (they weigh nothing), so it bounds only the
inline generated versions (and an inline AC-22 attachment); "originals are always kept" then costs nothing in bytes, and the real bound
on originals is the token limit (a vendor fact: V-7 / OD-34). The two readings change which requests fail, so the Specification
cannot pick one.

**Impact if unresolved.** FR-34, FR-40, FR-47 (headroom, OD-23 C-23.7) and the OD-24 exit rest on the measured remainder. A wrong
choice either blocks the first message of a legitimate session (A) or lets the BFF assemble a request that the vendor rejects for size
or tokens (B, if V-6 shows referenced content counts). It also changes the OD-23 headroom arithmetic and the request contract (A adds a
client-supplied size field to the generate request).

**v5 blocking text (answered in v6; superseded by the v6 block above).** The human must give: (a) A or B (or another rule) for originals; (b) for the attachment of "Edit this version":
inline or Files API; (c) if A: whether the 18 MB number, the AC-3 count or the AC-4 size must change, since 5 x 5 MB cannot fit
(changing them amends the Story and is the human's). Realistic leads, none a decision: **B**, with the token limit as the practical bound on
originals (matches the Files API purpose and the Story Context, no new request field; depends on V-6 / V-7 confirming the vendor does the
same); **A**, with the client sending each original's decoded size (conservative; needs an amendment of AC-3 / AC-4 / the 18 MB number or the
first message fails).

## OD-28 - Does the "Edit this version" attachment count toward 5, and do AC-4 / AC-5 apply to it? (AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-22; Specification O-3) - RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS (v6)

### v6 resolution (human decision, conditions)

*The text below this v6 block is the v5 record of the question, kept for history. Where it says OPEN or `Blocking: true`, this v6 block supersedes it.*

**Human decision (v6, verbatim).** "OD-28: Вкладення "Edit this version" не входить у ліміт 5 (AC-3); AC-4/AC-5 застосовуються лише до завантажень користувача; згенерована версія відправляється як є." (English rendering: The "Edit this version" attachment does not count toward the limit of 5 (AC-3); AC-4 / AC-5 apply only to user uploads; the generated version is sent as is.) The human is the decider; this stage chose nothing.

**Verification.** Consistent with OD-27 (b) (inline, no Files API) and with AC-28 (which concerns "user-uploaded images"). The effect is that one message can carry 5 uploads plus the attached version. The Story's "up to 14 reference images" (Context) is not exceeded by a single message (6), but whether that vendor limit counts images carried by the history is open and recorded in V-7 (iv).

**Conditions the Specification MUST carry.**
- C-28.1 AC-3 counts user uploads only: a 6th **upload** is rejected with the AC-3 message whether or not a version is attached; 5 uploads plus an attached version are accepted. AC-4 and AC-5 are narrowed to user uploads (clipboard and drag-and-drop included, AC-1); AC-5's "every image" now reads "every uploaded image". A test covers 5 + attachment and 6 uploads.
- C-28.2 **Flagged default (not a decision):** at most **one** version is attached at a time (a second "Edit this version" replaces the first), following the singular wording of AC-22. Without a cap the 5-image rule could be bypassed by repeatedly attaching versions; the human's text does not give a number.
- C-28.3 The generated version is sent without downscaling or re-encoding, including a 4K one, so the request size and the OD-27 / OD-23 arithmetic (C-27.3, C-23.7) include its full inline size. That a generated image carries no EXIF is an assumption the Specification states; it is not verified in the repository.
- C-28.4 The attachment is outside the Files API (OD-27 (b)) and is not listed in the first-upload path (FR-15).

**Blocking: false** (decided).


**Question.** (a) AC-3 allows "at most 5 images ... to one message": does the version attached by "Edit this version" count toward
the 5 (so a message with 5 uploaded images has no room for it)? (b) AC-4 downscales an image "with a long side > 2048 px or a size > 5 MB"
and AC-5 strips EXIF "from every image before it is sent": do they apply to a generated version that is attached (a 4K version has a long
side of 4096 px; a generated image has no EXIF)? Downscaling it changes what AC-22 says is attached ("that version") and what the model edits.

**Why it cannot be inferred.** Checked AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-22 and FR-10 / FR-32: AC-22 says only that the version is attached "as an
input image of the next message"; AC-3 counts "images attached to one message"; neither mentions the other. Question (b) was not raised by
the Specification and is added by this stage from the same reading of AC-4 ("an image with ...").

**Impact if unresolved.** The 5-image boundary test has two valid answers (4 uploads plus an edit attachment); and the edited version is
either downscaled (a 4K edit becomes a 2K input, lowering the output quality of the edit chain) or sent as is (larger request, interacts with
OD-27).

**Blocking: false.** Why: a narrow boundary of two criteria; the Specification can carry both readings as test cases once answered and
no other requirement is shaped by it, but it must be answered or accepted before `HUMAN_SPEC_APPROVAL`. Default (not a decision): the
attachment counts toward the 5, and AC-4 / AC-5 apply to uploads only, not to generated versions.

## OD-29 - Failure paths FR-54 does not define (AC-37; FR-15, FR-31, FR-35 cite FR-54) - RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS (v6)

### v6 resolution (human decision, conditions)

*The text below this v6 block is the v5 record of the question, kept for history. Where it says OPEN or `Blocking: true`, this v6 block supersedes it.*

**Human decision (v6, verbatim).** "OD-29: Конверт {error: string} для всіх збоїв. Не-429 4xx прокидаються з фіксованим текстом ("Помилка API") без Retry. Вичерпані повтори повертають оригінальний статус з кнопкою Retry. Збої countTokens/upload — той самий цикл 1/2/4 с." (English rendering: the `{ error: string }` envelope for every failure. A non-429 4xx is passed through with the fixed text "Помилка API" and no Retry. Exhausted retries return the original status with a Retry button. `countTokens` / upload failures use the same 1 / 2 / 4 s cycle.) The human is the decider; this stage chose nothing.

**Verification.**
- `server/index.js` `sendError` answers every failure as `res.status(error.status || 500).json({ error: describeError(error) })`; the human's rule keeps the envelope but fixes the text for a non-429 4xx (the upstream text is not sent to the browser, which also removes the v5 concern about leaking prompt or configuration detail).
- `src/services/http-retry.ts` never retries a response carrying `{ error: string }`, so every envelope listed here is shown once, with or without a Retry button, and the retry is the user's.
- **Status collisions.** Passing the upstream status through can produce a status the BFF itself reserves: 410 (file missing, OD-7), 413 (`PROTECTED_HISTORY_EXCEEDS_LIMIT` and `REQUEST_BODY_TOO_LARGE`, OD-24 / OD-11), 411, 429 (the OD-13 limiter) and 501 (missing key, OD-3). An upstream 410 that is not the file-missing class, if passed through as 410, would make the client re-upload and repeat for nothing. The fix is the OD-24 C-24.3 pattern.
- **AC-37 versus the repository's `withRetry`.** `server/utils/retry.js` counts `maxRetries` as the number of attempts, uses a 3000 ms base plus jitter, a separate policy budget of 6 for 429 / 503, and deliberately does not retry a bare 500. AC-37 requires up to **3 retries** at **1 -> 2 -> 4 s** on 429 and 5xx. The two do not match; a test written against the `withRetry` defaults would assert the wrong attempt count.

**Conditions the Specification MUST carry.**
- C-29.1 **Taxonomy** (all in `{ error: string }`):
  - BFF-originated and reserved, each with a **closed code constant** in `error` and its own handling: 411 `CONTENT_LENGTH_REQUIRED`, 413 `REQUEST_BODY_TOO_LARGE`, 413 `PROTECTED_HISTORY_EXCEEDS_LIMIT`, 410 file missing, 429 rate limit (OD-13), 501 missing key. The Specification adds codes for the 410 and the 429 (SD-2 lists only the others); the 501 text and code follow OD-3.
  - Upstream 4xx other than 429 and other than the OD-7 file-missing class: the upstream status is passed through, `error` is the fixed "Помилка API", **no Retry button**, no BFF retry; the failed turn keeps its content for the user (OD-19).
  - Upstream 429 and 5xx: the BFF retries up to 3 times at 1 / 2 / 4 s (AC-37); once exhausted it returns the **original status** with a distinct closed code and the Retry button.
  - An empty or truncated 200 (OD-30): a BFF error, status fixed by the Specification, with the Retry button.
- C-29.2 **No collision.** The client dispatches on status **and** code. An upstream-derived response never carries a reserved code, and a reserved status coming from upstream (410, 413, 501, 411) is not interpreted as the BFF's own signal; the client re-uploads only on the 410 that carries the file-missing code. A test feeds an upstream 410 that is not file-missing and asserts no re-upload.
- C-29.3 **The retry loop is specified, not inherited.** The Specification (and the planner) define a loop that gives exactly 3 retries (4 attempts) at 1 / 2 / 4 s on 429 and 5xx and states whether a bare 500 is retried (AC-37 says 5xx; the repository's util says no); it either parameterises `withRetry` accordingly or states why it does not.
- C-29.4 **`countTokens` and upload** use the same taxonomy and the same loop. A `countTokens` failure after retries, or a non-retryable one, ends the action with no generate call (SD-11), writes no row and is not counted by OD-13; an exhausted 429 / 5xx shows the Retry button, a non-retryable 4xx does not. An upload failure leaves nothing attached to the history (FR-57).
- C-29.5 The error texts are Ukrainian (the human's literal and the Specification's other fixed texts); the English-UI question is OD-14 (open).
- C-29.6 Server-side logging of the upstream detail stays as `describeError` does today, without the API key or prompt payload (AGENTS.md section 7); the browser never receives the raw upstream text.
- C-29.7 **Open, not decided here:** whether the generate call goes through `retryTransport` at all (each call is paid and the file's own header calls it non-idempotent) stays in OD-5 (open); an Express-default non-JSON error (for example a 413 from `express.json`) is shown as the generic non-retryable error (OD-25 default).

**Blocking: false** (decided).


**Question.** (a) For an **upstream 4xx other than 429** that is not the file-missing class of OD-7 (a 400 invalid request, a 401 / 403 key or
permission error, a 404 for a model), does the BFF pass the upstream status through or map it to a fixed status, what does the user see
(a fixed message, or the upstream text), is a Retry button shown, and is the call retried? (b) After the **3 retries are exhausted**
(AC-37), what status and body does the BFF return, and is it distinguishable from a first-attempt failure? (c) Do `countTokens`
(SD-11) and the first upload / re-upload (FR-15, FR-16) follow the same taxonomy and the same 1 / 2 / 4 s backoff, or does a failure there end
the user action at once? (d) Does a request that ended in an error count toward OD-13?

**Why it cannot be inferred.** Checked AC-37 (429 / 5xx, three retries, then "an error shown with a Retry button"; nothing for other
statuses or for the status of the final error); Specification FR-54, FR-15, FR-31, FR-35, SD-11 (each says "under FR-54" while FR-54 defines
only upstream 429 and 5xx); `server/index.js` `sendError` (passes `error.status || 500` through with the described error text) and
`src/services/http-retry.ts` (the client retries only status 0 / 500 / 502 / 503 / 504 that carry no `{ error: string }` envelope).
So the existing pieces make a pass-through status with an envelope safe from client auto-retry, but nothing says it is the intended
design for these cases, nor whether the user sees upstream text (which could expose prompt or configuration detail).

**Impact if unresolved.** The Specification writer would invent the classification table; a wrong choice either offers a Retry button for an
error that cannot succeed (wasting the user's time and the rate-limit budget of OD-13) or hides a configuration error (a 401 / 403 shown
as a generic retryable failure).

**Blocking: false.** Why: a derivable lead exists from decisions already recorded, so the Specification can carry a labelled PROPOSED
DEFAULT (as it does for OD-5 and OD-25) instead of guessing silently: OD-3 N-3.1 and OD-24 C-24.4 establish that a BFF error in the
`{ error: string }` envelope is not retried by the client and shows no Retry button when retrying cannot help. Lead (not a decision):
envelope on every failure; non-429 4xx passed through with a fixed message (not raw upstream text) and **no** Retry button; exhausted
429 / 5xx returned with an upstream-class status in the envelope and the Retry button AC-37 requires; `countTokens` and upload 429 / 5xx
use the same 1 / 2 / 4 s loop, other failures end the action at once; a failed request counts per OD-13. The language of the texts is OD-14.

## OD-30 - A non-safety non-image response, and a response with more than one image (AC-10, AC-13, AC-38, AC-39, AC-46) - RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS (v6)

**v8 cross-note (SD-15 accepted).** C-30.1 below says the empty / truncated-response error "carries no reserved code". The human accepted SD-1 to SD-40, and **SD-15** fixes the closed code `EMPTY_RESPONSE` (HTTP 502, with Retry), following the clarification report v6 hand-off 7. C-30.1's "carries no reserved code" is therefore **amended**: the empty or truncated response is HTTP 502 with `error` equal to `EMPTY_RESPONSE`. SD-23 (the closed sets, including the choices not forced by the typings) is accepted likewise.

### v6 resolution (human decision, conditions)

*The text below this v6 block is the v5 record of the question, kept for history. Where it says OPEN or `Blocking: true`, this v6 block supersedes it.*

**Human decision (v6, verbatim).** "OD-30: Порожня/обрізана відповідь = failed turn (помилка BFF, без usage, з Retry). Кілька зображень: кожне — окрема версія (vN, vN+1), анонсується найвищий номер, latest = останнє в масиві, вартість = ціна × кількість." (English rendering: An empty or truncated response is a failed turn (a BFF error, no usage, with Retry). Several images: each is its own version (vN, vN+1), the highest number is announced, latest is the last in the array, cost = price x count.) The human is the decider; this stage chose nothing.

**Tension point 8 (caller): failed turn versus the usage row and the rate-limit count.** An empty or truncated response is a response that reached Gemini, so it **counts** toward the 30 per hour (OD-13 C-13.2) while writing **no** usage row and costing 0 (OD-19(d)); the user's Retry is a new request and counts again. Quota and `usage_log` diverge by design (OD-13 C-13.8).

**Conditions the Specification MUST carry.**
- C-30.1 **Classification, in this order** (a derivation from the decisions of OD-19 and OD-30, flagged for approval where marked): (1) a safety finish reason -> a 200 safety envelope (OD-19(e)), non-history; (2) a response that contains at least one image part that passes the schema (AC-14) -> a success (versions, cost, a usage row); **flagged default:** an image together with an abnormal finish reason is still a success, because billing is per image and the image is usable; (3) text only with a normal finish -> a 200 text-only response (AC-39), non-history; (4) anything else - no candidate, an empty candidate, text only with an abnormal finish, a malformed or cut-off body - a **failed turn**: a BFF error in the envelope with the Retry button, no history entry, no row, no cost. The status of the error in (4) is the Specification's to fix; it carries no reserved code (OD-29 C-29.2).
- C-30.2 **Several images.** Each image part is its own version, numbered consecutively in API order (vN, vN+1); the AC-46 announcement carries the **highest** number; the "latest version" of the protected set (OD-7 C-7.4) is the last image in the array; the cost is the unit price times the number of images (FR-61), one `usage_log` row per request (not per image); one request is one unit of the rate limit.
- C-30.3 **Turn-level unit.** A trim or a protection acts on a **turn**, not on a version: the model turn holding several images is kept or removed whole (its parts and `thoughtSignature` fields are not split), so protecting the latest version protects every image of that turn; the strip, the ZIP and "Edit this version" list each image separately. Removing a turn deletes all its versions (OD-23), and `trimmedTurnIds` lists turns.
- C-30.4 **No cap is invented.** The number of images per response is unbounded in the Specification; the OD-23 C-23.7 headroom arithmetic uses the largest response measured under V-6 (total image bytes of one response), and a cap would be a human decision (new OD if the arithmetic needs one).
- C-30.5 The AC-46 "exactly twice" test for a multi-image response asserts the second announcement names the highest number; the paths with no new version (text-only, safety, failure) are OD-21 (open).

**Blocking: false** (decided).


**Question.** (a) A 200 from Gemini whose candidate has a `finishReason` other than a normal stop or a safety reason (for example max
tokens, "other", recitation), or is empty, truncated or malformed before FR-23's schema applies: is it a **failed turn** (AC-40; error,
no history entry, Retry), a **text-only** turn (AC-39), or something else; which HTTP status does the BFF return; does it write a usage
row or cost? (b) A response that contains **more than one image**: does each image become its own version (v3, v4) in API order; which
number does "Версію vN готово" (AC-46) carry; which is the "latest version" of the protected set (OD-7 C-7.4); is there a cap on images per
response?

**Why it cannot be inferred.** Checked AC-38 to AC-40 (safety block and text-only only), AC-10 / AC-13 (images in API order, each gets a
version number), AC-46 (one "Версію vN готово"), Specification FR-22 (numbers "each generated image"), FR-61 (cost per image count),
FR-55 / FR-56. Each requirement assumes at most one image or one of the two named failure kinds; none states the other cases. The
review (section 5) found both gaps.

**Impact if unresolved.** (a) a truncated or "other" reply is classified by guess, which changes the history (OD-19) and the usage log;
(b) the announcement, the numbering, "latest version" for trimming and the cost for a two-image response are undefined, and the AC-46
test ("exactly twice") cannot say what the second announcement contains.

**Blocking: false.** Why: derivable leads exist and the cases are rare edges of criteria that are otherwise defined. Answer (a) together
with OD-19. Lead (not a decision): (a) treat as a failed turn with a BFF-originated envelope error, no usage row, Retry shown; (b) each
image part is one version, numbered consecutively in API order, the announcement carries the highest number, "latest version" is the last
image in API order, cost = unit price x image count (FR-61 already states this); no cap is invented.

## OD-31 - Story Scope `.env.example` "model IDs" vs OD-1 (Story Scope, Q1) - RESOLVED (v6)

### v6 resolution (human decision)

*The text below this v6 block is the v5 record of the question, kept for history. Where it says OPEN or `Blocking: true`, this v6 block supersedes it.*

**Human decision (v6, verbatim).** "OD-31: Ніяких env-перевизначень; каталог — єдине джерело ідентифікаторів моделей." (English rendering: No env overrides; the catalog is the single source of model identifiers.) The human is the decider; this stage chose nothing.

**Verification.** `.env.example` has `OPENAI_MODEL` and `ANTHROPIC_MODEL_THINKING` / `ANTHROPIC_MODEL_FAST` but no Gemini model variable; the Gemini models already live only in `src/prompt-core/model-catalog.json`. The decision is consistent with OD-1 (catalog), with AGENTS.md section 6 item 7 (`.env.example` changes only when a setting is added: none is) and with the Story Context ("Model IDs live in configuration, not in code"). `GEMINI_API_KEY` is already documented in `.env.example`, so no new line is needed for the key.

**Note.** The Story Scope line "`.env.example` (... model IDs)" is **overridden** (cumulative table): there is no model-ID entry in `.env.example`. Correcting an ID after V-3 is a catalog edit plus the pricing key (OD-4 C-4.2).

**Blocking: false** (decided).


**Question.** Does the Story's Scope line "`.env.example` (new/required `GEMINI_API_KEY` usage, model IDs)" mean that an environment variable
for the image-model IDs must exist (an override of the catalog), or are the IDs only in `src/prompt-core/model-catalog.json` as OD-1 decided?

**Why it cannot be inferred.** Checked the Story (Context: "Model IDs live in configuration, not in code"; Scope row above), OD-1 (the human
chose the catalog; condition 6: `.env.example` is updated "if an image-model env override is introduced ... None is required by the
decision"), Specification FR-7 (adds no image-model variable) and the review's finding that the "model IDs" part of the Story line is
silently dropped rather than recorded as an override.

**Impact if unresolved.** If an override is wanted (for example to correct an ID without a code change after V-3), it is a second
configuration source next to the catalog, with its own validation and an `.env.example` entry (AGENTS.md section 6, item 7). If it is not
wanted, the Story line must be recorded as an override. Either way the Specification cannot state it without guessing.

**Blocking: false.** Default (not a decision, awaiting the human): no env override; the catalog is the single source of the IDs, and the
"model IDs" part of the Story line is recorded as an override in the cumulative table above (flagged pending).

## OD-32 - How many `countTokens` calls one user action may make (AC-24; Specification FR-35) - RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS (v6)

### v6 resolution (human decision, conditions)

*The text below this v6 block is the v5 record of the question, kept for history. Where it says OPEN or `Blocking: true`, this v6 block supersedes it.*

**Human decision (v6, verbatim).** "OD-32: Один виклик countTokens на кожен retained turn окремо (конкурентно, k викликів), сума для пошуку межі обрізання, краще на BFF." (English rendering: One `countTokens` call per retained turn, separately (concurrently, k calls); the sum is used to find the cut point; preferably on the BFF.) The human is the decider; this stage chose nothing. It is option (2) of the v5 entry.

**Tension point 6 (caller), checked.**
- *The sum is an estimate, not the whole-request count.* The sum of per-turn counts need not equal one count of the whole history (system instruction, per-request or per-turn overhead, per-image accounting). The 80% test therefore compares a **sum**; the 20% margin is the accepted buffer. V-7 (extended in v6) measures the gap and whether the same input returns the same count on repeat (the OD-23 replay relies on a deterministic trim, C-23.3).
- *Latency and the 120 s timeout (OD-5).* The calls are concurrent, so the added latency is about that of the slowest call, plus the 1 / 2 / 4 s cycle of OD-29 if one call is retried (about 7 s more). That is small against 120 s unless the count of turns is large; but the deadline rule itself is OD-5, which stays open and is not settled by this entry.
- *Vendor limits.* k concurrent calls per generation, each possibly retried, count against Gemini's requests-per-minute for `countTokens` (a vendor fact, V-7 (v)). The number of retained turns is bounded by the trims themselves, so k stays bounded in a normal session, but no bound is stated by the human.

**Conditions the Specification MUST carry.**
- C-32.1 The BFF counts each retained turn separately and concurrently, plus the **new user turn** (OD-24 C-24.1 includes it in the measured remainder; so k + 1 calls), sums the counts, and finds the cut by removing the oldest unit while the sum exceeds 80% (pair units, OD-7). The turn-by-turn counting runs after the byte trim (OD-7 order: 18 MB, then 80%).
- C-32.2 **Concurrency is bounded.** The Specification states an upper bound on simultaneous `countTokens` calls (its value is the Specification's to fix and flag; the human gave none) so a long session cannot burst past the vendor rate limit. Each call follows the OD-29 loop; one call failing after retries ends the action (SD-11).
- C-32.3 The summed count is the figure compared with 80% of the model's input limit; the Specification states that the sum is not a whole-request count and that the margin covers the difference. If V-7 shows the gap exceeds the margin, that is a new decision for the human, not a guess.
- C-32.4 The trim must remain a pure function of the request body plus the (deterministic) token counts (NFR-5 / OD-23 C-23.3); a test with a stubbed `countTokens` asserts the same input yields the same `trimmedTurnIds`.
- C-32.5 The calls are made by the BFF (the human prefers the BFF; the client has no key, AGENTS.md Rule 4), are not counted by the rate limit (OD-13(d)), and add nothing to `usage_log`.
- C-32.6 OD-5 cross-note: the worst-case time of one user action now includes the k + 1 `countTokens` calls, the first-upload calls, a possible 410 re-upload and the generate retries; OD-5 stays open with its PROPOSED DEFAULT.

**Blocking: false** (decided).


**Question.** FR-35 trims the oldest units "until the count is at most 80%". How many `countTokens` calls does one user action make: one
call per removed unit (a loop), one call on the full request and one verification call, one call per retained turn (computed locally), a
bounded number N with a stated outcome when N is reached, or another scheme?

**Why it cannot be inferred.** Checked AC-24 ("The BFF counts the history tokens (`countTokens`)", no cardinality), FR-35 / SD-11, OD-5 and
OD-13 (both leave the effect of `countTokens` on the deadline and on the rate limit open), and `server/` (no existing `countTokens` use).
`countTokens` returns a total for what is sent, so per-turn weights are not known without more calls.

**Impact if unresolved.** The call count sets the worst-case latency inside the 120 s deadline (OD-5), whether `countTokens` can be
counted toward the 30 per hour (OD-13), the number of vendor requests per paid action, and the determinism the replay of OD-23 relies on
(the scheme must be a pure function of the body, NFR-5).

**Blocking: false.** Why: a performance and accounting choice for one rule; answer it together with OD-5 and OD-13. Options, none a
lead: (1) recount after each removed unit (worst case: number of pairs + 1 calls, simplest, unbounded); (2) count every retained turn
once, compute the cut locally (k + 1 calls, deterministic, one round); (3) one count, remove a computed estimate, one verification count
(2 calls, an estimate); (4) a hard cap N, with a defined result when it is reached.

## OD-33 - Where the V-1 to V-7 results are recorded (Specification O-5) - RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS (v6)

### v6 resolution (human decision, conditions)

*The text below this v6 block is the v5 record of the question, kept for history. Where it says OPEN or `Blocking: true`, this v6 block supersedes it.*

**Human decision (v6, verbatim).** "OD-33: Результати V-1..V-7 — незареєстрований файл docs/evidence/US-10.1-pre-implementation-checks.md, який людина заповнює до IMPLEMENTATION." (English rendering: The V-1..V-7 results go into an unregistered file `docs/evidence/US-10.1-pre-implementation-checks.md`, which the human fills in before IMPLEMENTATION.) The human is the decider; this stage chose nothing. It is option (C) of the v5 entry.
