**Verification.** `docs/workflow/artifact-paths.yaml` has no key for this file; by the registry's own rule it is an **external input**. `docs/evidence/` currently holds only clarification reports. `tools/validate-harness.mjs` checks registry keys against `stage-map.yaml`; it does not scan `docs/evidence/` for unregistered files (a search for "evidence" in it found nothing), so an unregistered file does not break `npm run validate:harness`. No `docs/workflow/` file needs to change.

**Conditions the Specification MUST carry.**
- C-33.1 The file is human-authored and external: no skill creates or edits it (a skill writing an artifact it does not own is a defect, `artifact-lifecycle.md` section 3), and it has no staleness contract, so it carries no version front matter requirement.
- C-33.2 For each of V-1 to V-7 the file records the date, the method, the observed result and, where the check selects between designs, the selection (V-1: pair removal or the collapse fallback; V-3: the IDs; V-6: the sizes and MIME types; V-7: the token figures). The Specification and the plan name the file as a **precondition of IMPLEMENTATION**.
- C-33.3 **Accepted process risk.** Nothing in the pipeline mechanically checks that the file exists before IMPLEMENTATION; the practical checkpoint is the human plan approval, where the plan lists the checks. The Specification must not claim a mechanical guard.
- C-33.4 The V-1 to V-7 list is the one in the verification table of this file (V-6 and V-7 extended in v6).

**Blocking: false** (decided).


**Question.** The Specification proposes `docs/evidence/US-10.1-pre-implementation-checks.md` for the result of each pre-implementation
check, but `docs/workflow/artifact-paths.yaml` has no key for it. Where are the results recorded, and what (if anything) verifies they
exist before IMPLEMENTATION?

**Why it cannot be inferred.** Checked `artifact-paths.yaml` (no matching key; the registry's rule: "a consumed artifact with no `owner`
here is an external input"; every key must be reachable from a stage, enforced by `npm run validate:harness`), `stage-map.yaml` ownership,
and AGENTS.md sections 7.8 / 12 (edits under `docs/workflow/` are reserved to a human).

**Impact if unresolved.** V-1 selects the trimming strategy, V-2 the 410 recognizer, V-3 the IDs, V-5 the prices, V-6 / V-7 the headroom
and token measures: if their results have no registered home, no skill owns writing them, no stage consumes them, and IMPLEMENTATION
could start without them (the staleness contract of `artifact-schema.md` cannot apply to an unregistered file).

**Blocking: false.** Why: a process item for the harness owner; the checks take place after approval, and the Specification can name the
place as "per OD-33". Options, none a decision: (A) add a registry key and wire it into `stage-map.yaml` (a `docs/workflow/` change the human
approves; `validate:harness` must stay green); (B) record the results inside an existing artifact that has a key and a stage (for
example an appendix of the implementation plan), at the cost of mixing evidence with design; (C) keep the proposed file unregistered, as
an external input authored by the human, with the Specification stating the human confirms it before IMPLEMENTATION.

## OD-34 - Fallback if `countTokens` rejects image and `file_uri` parts (AC-24; Specification O-4) - RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS (v6)

### v6 resolution (human decision, conditions)

*The text below this v6 block is the v5 record of the question, kept for history. Where it says OPEN or `Blocking: true`, this v6 block supersedes it.*

**Human decision (v6, verbatim).** "OD-34: Якщо countTokens ігнорує зображення — естимувати (напр. +258 токенів за зображення чи file_uri) і додавати до підрахунку тексту." (English rendering: If `countTokens` ignores images, estimate them (for example +258 tokens per image or `file_uri`) and add that to the text count.) The human is the decider; this stage chose nothing. It is option (a) of the v5 entry.

**Tension point 7 (caller): is "+258 tokens per image" verifiable?** No. A search of the installed `@google/genai` typings (`node_modules/@google/genai/dist/genai.d.ts`) finds no 258 figure. The typings do show that the per-image token cost depends on a media-resolution setting: `MediaResolution` LOW = 64 tokens, MEDIUM = 256, HIGH = 256 ("zoomed reframing"), and a per-part `PartMediaResolutionLevel` with an ULTRA_HIGH level that has no stated figure. Nothing in the repository, the Story or the typings gives a cost for the Gemini 3.x image models, for a `file_uri` part, or for a model-generated image. The human wrote "напр." (for example). **The figure 258 is therefore a labelled placeholder, not a constant the Specification may fix.**

**Conditions the Specification MUST carry.**
- C-34.1 **Placeholder.** The Specification names the estimate `IMAGE_TOKEN_ESTIMATE` (or similar) as a **configuration value with a recorded source**, whose initial value 258 is marked "human-supplied example, unverified, from older documentation; not supported by anything in this repository". Its final value (per model, possibly per resolution or media-resolution setting, and for inline, `file_uri` and generated images separately if V-7 shows they differ) comes from **V-7 (i)**, recorded under OD-33, before IMPLEMENTATION.
- C-34.2 **When it applies.** Only if V-7 shows that `countTokens` rejects or ignores image and `file_uri` parts. The choice is made once from the V-7 result and recorded; it is not a runtime heuristic. If `countTokens` counts images correctly, the estimate is unused. The estimate is added per image part and per `file_uri` part to the text count (human), applied per turn within the OD-32 scheme.
- C-34.3 **Risk stated.** An underestimate lets a request exceed the model's real input limit; the 20% margin of the 80% rule is the buffer, and a vendor rejection surfaces under OD-29 ("Помилка API", no Retry). An overestimate trims versions earlier than needed, which is a permanent deletion (OD-23). The Specification states both and tests the arithmetic with the placeholder replaced by a fixture value.
- C-34.4 If V-7 shows `countTokens` accepts the parts but with a count that cannot be used (OD-32 gap beyond the margin), that is a new decision for the human, not a guess.

**Blocking: false** (decided; the value awaits V-7).


**Question.** If check V-7 shows that the live `countTokens` does not accept a history containing inline image parts and `file_uri`
parts for these models (or returns a count that excludes them), what is the token-limit path: (a) estimate image tokens from a documented
per-image cost and count the text only, (b) apply only the size trim (AC-29) and drop the token trim, (c) end the action with an error
(SD-11 would make every image session unusable), or (d) other?

**Why it cannot be inferred.** Nothing in the repository, the Story or the installed `@google/genai` typings states whether `countTokens`
supports these parts for the image models; it is a vendor fact (V-7, new in v5, folded in as the review recommended). The Specification
SD-11 treats a `countTokens` failure as an action error.

**Impact if unresolved.** If V-7 fails and no fallback is decided, AC-24 cannot be implemented as written and the Specification's FR-35 is
untestable; a silent estimate would change when users lose versions (a permanent deletion, OD-23).

**Blocking: false.** Why: this is only needed if V-7 fails; the Specification carries V-7 as a pre-implementation check, and a failed
V-7 is raised to the human before IMPLEMENTATION as a new decision (the C-23.7 pattern), not guessed. No lead is offered.

## OD-35 - The dashboard grouping change against NFR-6 / Rule 5 (AC-44; Specification FR-63) - RESOLVED (v6)

### v6 resolution (human decision)

*The text below this v6 block is the v5 record of the question, kept for history. Where it says OPEN or `Blocking: true`, this v6 block supersedes it.*

**Human decision (v6, verbatim).** "OD-35: Зміни дашборду (FR-63) приймаються; нові кошики додаються до існуючих без зламу старих." (English rendering: The dashboard changes (FR-63) are accepted; new buckets are added to the existing ones without breaking the old ones.) The human is the decider; this stage chose nothing.

**Verification.** `src/app/components/dashboard/dashboard.component.ts` builds `taskLabels` from every distinct `taskLabel`, filters by it and groups with `groupBy(..., r => r.taskLabel || '—')`; adding rows with new labels adds buckets and adds image spend to the totals without altering existing rows, so the change is additive (AGENTS.md section 3 Rule 5).

**Notes the Specification carries.** (1) NFR-6 and FR-63 are reconciled: existing views behave as before for existing labels; the visible additions are the new `image-studio|<presetId>|<resolution>` buckets and the image spend in the totals. A dashboard test with mixed old and new rows asserts the old buckets are unchanged. (2) The optional stable marker in the `mode` column (OD-4 C-4.7's open sub-question) is moot: image-studio rows are recognised by their `task_label` prefix, and no marker is added. (3) `queryUsage` still filters `task_label` by exact match (OD-4 C-4.7).

**Blocking: false** (decided).


**Question.** Image-studio usage rows share `usage_log` with the existing modes, so the existing dashboard (`dashboard.component.ts`:
`taskLabels`, `byTask`, the task filter and the cost totals) will show up to about 5 x 3 new `task_label` buckets and add image spend to its
totals. Does the human accept that visible change to an existing view, or must the dashboard stay as it is (for example by excluding
image-studio rows or by a stable marker in the existing `mode` column, the open sub-question of OD-4 C-4.7)?

**Why it cannot be inferred.** Checked OD-4 C-4.7 (records the visible change, leaves the `mode` marker open), the Specification FR-63 (admits
a "visible change to an existing view") against NFR-6 (existing views behave as before), and `src/app/components/dashboard/
dashboard.component.ts` (groups by `r.taskLabel`, the task list is every distinct label, no mode filter). AGENTS.md section 3 Rule 5:
"Don't break existing features".

**Impact if unresolved.** The Specification states two incompatible things (FR-63, NFR-6); the planner cannot tell whether a dashboard change
is in or out of scope; a reviewer could reject either.

**Blocking: false.** Why: the change is additive (new buckets and totals, no existing mode's rows are altered), which is arguably not a
breach of Rule 5, and the human only has to confirm it. Default (not a decision): accept the additive change and state it in the Specification
as an accepted, visible effect.

The entries below come from the SPEC_REVIEW v4 loop-back. They record the questions Specification v4 itself marks as needing the human, and

## OD-36 - What happens when a 21st session would be created (Specification Q-16a; AC-25, AC-26, AC-27; FR-47, FR-48, FR-49, FR-50, FR-86) - RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS (v8)

### v8 resolution (human decision)

*The text below this v8 block is the earlier (v7) record of the question, kept for history; where it says OPEN or `Blocking: true`, this v8 block supersedes it.*

**Human decision (v8, verbatim).** "OD-36 (21-ша сесія): Рішення: B (Витіснити найстарішу за останньою активністю). Витіснення відбувається мовчазно (без підтверджень чи повідомлень), це просто ковзне вікно (rolling window) історії. Ліміт 20 стосується саме збережених у IndexedDB сесій. Автоматичне створення гілок/сесій (FR-50, FR-47) просто виштовхуватиме найстарішу неактивну сесію зі списку." (English rendering: B, evict the oldest by last activity. The eviction is silent, with no confirmation or message; it is simply a rolling window of the history. The limit of 20 concerns the sessions stored in IndexedDB. The automatic creation of branches / sessions (FR-50, FR-47) will simply push the oldest inactive session out of the list.)

**What the human decided (the three sub-answers of the v7 entry).** (1) Policy **B**: evict the stored session with the oldest last-activity stamp. (2) The cap counts **stored** sessions (not rows shown). (3) **Silent**: no confirmation, no notice. Option C (refuse) is not chosen, so the model switch (FR-50) and the blocked-state exit (FR-47) are never blocked by the cap, which removes the trap the Specification flagged in CF-15.

**Verification.**
- **The premise "the session in use is the most recently active" is false under the confirmed SD-32 (OD-44).** The stamp is set at creation and renewed only by a committed FR-44 write (an accepted user turn with its model turn), a cut, or a downloaded-flag change. Opening, selecting and viewing are not activity, and a turn in flight commits nothing until its response arrives. So the session the operator is standing in can be the *oldest* stored session: an old session reopened from the list and then left by "New session"; the source of a model switch (FR-50); the blocked session of an FR-47 exit (the 413 committed nothing); a session whose first reply is still in flight (FR-21 keeps "New session" and the session list enabled during a generation, and SD-22 then discards the paid response of a session that no longer exists). Read literally, B could delete exactly the session that the same action says "stays in the session list". The human's own word "неактивну" (inactive), the confirmed T-23 text ("Start a new session? The current session stays in the session list."; confirmed in OD-41), AC-26 and FR-47 ("the old session keeps its history for viewing and download") all point the same way; the exclusion is therefore carried as a **condition (C-36.4)**, not left to chance. What it protects is a Specification derivation, labelled, with its alternative named (P-1 in OD-50).
- **Expiry and eviction agree.** Expired sessions (FR-48) are deleted silently on load; a stale tab can still show an expired session (SD-33). The oldest-stamp session is the one most likely to be expired already, so B evicts expired rows first in practice, and the silent eviction mirrors the silent expiry.
- **Consent.** The human's OD-23 confirmations covered trimming versions out of a session, not deleting a whole session (the v7 entry said so). The human's explicit "silent" choice here is the consent for the whole-session, irreversible deletion without a notice: turns, versions (including never-downloaded ones), sent bytes, downloaded flags and the session's running cost total (FR-48 "Deleting a session deletes its whole record"). The Gemini Files API files are left as ever (OD-16 C-16.5). Server-side usage rows remain.
- **Visible consequences (accepted by the decision, listed so approval is informed).** (i) Twenty creations in a row (New session, model switches, blocked-state exits) push out every older session. (ii) A session is created even when the previous one holds no turn (FR-49 as written), so repeated "New session" confirmations push real sessions out with empty ones (proposal P-5). (iii) In a second tab the list is stale until its next load, and a response for a session evicted by the other tab is discarded (SD-22; OD-43). (iv) AC-25's 7 days becomes an upper bound.
- **AC-26 amendment (the AC consequence asked for).** "The previous session remains available in a session list" becomes: it remains in the list until it expires (7 days from its last activity) or is evicted as the least recently active of the 20 stored sessions, never in the same action that leaves it (C-36.4). AC-25 gets "at most 20 stored sessions"; AC-27's "new branch" is a new session row that may evict. Overrides table rows are added in this log.
- **No storage convention exists in `src/`** (no IndexedDB use), so atomicity (C-36.6) is a requirement on the new code, modelled on FR-44's single transaction.

**Conditions the Specification MUST carry.**
- **C-36.1** (FR-86, FR-49, FR-50, FR-47) Creating a session (the three paths: New session, model switch, blocked-state exit) while the number of **stored** sessions is at the cap deletes the stored session with the oldest last-activity stamp (SD-32), whole record (FR-48), as part of the creation. There is no confirmation, no notice, no message, no `role="alert"` or `role="status"` update and no `window.alert`. There is no refusal path and nothing blocks FR-47 or FR-50.
- **C-36.2** (FR-86, FR-48) "20" counts sessions stored in IndexedDB; the session list shows every stored session (at most 20). The 7-day expiry (FR-48) and the cap are independent rules; either may remove a session.
- **C-36.3** (FR-86 tests, injectable clock) With 20 stored sessions whose stamps differ and whose creation order differs from their stamp order: a creation deletes the session with the oldest **stamp** (not the oldest created), 20 remain, the list shows 20 rows without the deleted one, and no alert / confirm / `role` update happens; the same through each of the three creation paths; a session renewed by a committed write is not the one deleted; with fewer than 20 stored, nothing is deleted. These tests distinguish B from option A.
- **C-36.4** (FR-86, FR-49, FR-50, FR-47, SD-22; **Proposal (not a decision) P-1, awaiting confirmation**) The eviction candidate is chosen among the stored sessions **other than** (a) the session the operator is leaving or using when the creation is triggered (for a model switch or a blocked-state exit this is also the source of the seed), (b) a session with a generation in flight in this tab, and (c) the session being created. If no other session exists the cap is not exceeded and nothing is deleted. Alternative named, not applied: no exclusion (the literal oldest stamp), which can delete the session in use and conflicts with AC-26, FR-47 and T-23.
- **C-36.5** (FR-86; **Proposal P-2, awaiting confirmation; the human gave no tie-break**) Equal stamps are broken by a Specification-fixed rule, stated in the Specification as an SD item for approval, so that a test with a fixed clock (all stamps equal) is deterministic. The log does not choose the rule; options are in OD-50 P-2.
- **C-36.6** (FR-86, FR-81, FR-44; **Proposal P-3, awaiting confirmation**) The deletion and the creation are one IndexedDB transaction, and the seed (FR-47, FR-50) is read from its source session before any deletion. A quota or other failure of the creation leaves **no** session deleted (OD-16 C-16.4: a session creation that fails creates no session; the eviction must not outlive it).
- **C-36.7** (FR-86; **Proposal P-4**) The rule is "while the stored count would exceed 20, delete the next candidate", so a count above 20 left by two tabs (OD-43) heals at the next creation.
- **C-36.8** (FR-47, FR-49, FR-50, FR-48, FR-86) Every "see Q-16a" in the Specification is replaced by C-36.1 to C-36.7 (hand-off). FR-47's "the old session keeps its history for viewing and download" and FR-49's "the previous session remains available" are qualified by C-36.4 and by later evictions.
- **C-36.9** Accepted consequences are listed under the accepted limitations: OD-36 (silent whole-session deletion; creations push out older sessions), with the visible consequences above.

**Blocking: false** (decided). CF-15 is closed by this entry.


**Question.** The human fixed the list at 20 sessions (OD-16) and gave no behaviour at the limit. When a 21st session would be created, what happens?
Three sub-answers are needed: (1) the policy; (2) whether "in the list" means the sessions shown or the sessions stored; (3) if a session is evicted,
whether the operator is told or asked.

**Options (the human picks one for (1), one for (2), and one for (3) if (1) is A or B).**
- (1) **A. Evict the session with the oldest creation time** to make room for the new one.
- (1) **B. Evict the session with the oldest last-activity time** (SD-32) to make room.
- (1) **C. Refuse the creation with a message.** Consequence the Specification flags: the model switch (FR-50) and the blocked-state exit (FR-47) create sessions automatically, so a refusal would block them. The blocked state is stateless and its only exit is a new session (FR-47), so under C the operator in that state could not recover except by waiting for the 7-day expiry, because neither the Story nor the Specification defines an action that deletes a single session (grep of both files: only the expiry deletion of FR-48). The human could add: "C-hybrid, refuse only the manual New session and let the automatic creations evict" (then (1) A or B applies to those).
- (1) **D. Another rule**, stated by the human.
- (2) **Shown** (the list shows at most 20 rows while older sessions stay stored) or **stored** (at most 20 sessions exist in IndexedDB).
- (3) **Silent**, **notice** (a message that names the deleted session and says its versions are permanently deleted) or **confirmation** before creation. Constraint: the human's OD-23 confirmations covered trimming versions out of a session, not deleting a whole session, so they do not cover this.

**What was checked.** The Story (AC-25, AC-26, AC-27) and Specification v4 (FR-86: "Not decided by the human (open question Q-16a, no default offered)"; FR-47, FR-49, FR-50; CF-15); the human's OD-16 text ("Максимальна кількість сесій у списку — 20", no more); OD-17 C-17.6 (every model switch and blocked-state exit adds a row); no storage convention exists in `src/` (no IndexedDB use). Nothing in the repository or any earlier decision answers it.

**Impact if unresolved.** No test of the 21st session can be written (FR-86 says so); FR-47, FR-49, FR-50 and FR-48 each end in "see Q-16a". A wrong guess either deletes user data without the human's consent (A, B) or traps the operator in the blocked state (C). It is a data-loss contract, not a wording gap.

**Blocking: true.** Why: the Specification writer would have to choose a deletion or refusal contract, and a refusal could block a recovery path the human already decided (OD-24 exit). **No default may be invented.** Specification Q-16a and CF-15 are this entry.

## OD-37 - Outcome when the sent bytes of an upload cannot be stored (quota) (Specification Q-16c; FR-16, FR-17, FR-81; AC-28) - RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS (v8)

### v8 resolution (human decision)

*The text below this v8 block is the earlier (v7) record, kept for history; where it says OPEN or `Blocking: true`, this v8 block supersedes it.*

**Human decision (v8, verbatim).** "OD-37 (Квота IndexedDB під час збереження завантажень, sent bytes): Рішення: B (Залишити без джерела для повторного завантаження). Ми не повинні блокувати генерацію, якщо локальне сховище переповнене. Запит до Gemini відпрацює нормально через Files API. Якщо ж сесія затягнеться і файл протермінується, користувач просто отримає передбачене повідомлення T-10 і додасть зображення знову." (English rendering: B, keep it without a source for re-upload. We must not block generation if the local storage is full. The request to Gemini will work normally through the Files API. If the session drags on and the file expires, the user will simply get the foreseen message T-10 and add the image again.)

**Verification.**
- **Consistent with OD-12 C-12.2 and FR-16.** Only the sent bytes are ever stored; the store is now best effort. The Files API upload (FR-16) does not depend on the store. The history entry holds the `file_uri` only (FR-16, AC-28 "the history stores the file_uri, not base64"); the sent bytes live in the session's record and go with it (OD-12 C-12.2; deleted with a trimmed pair, OD-23 confirmation 1).
- **Consistent with FR-17.** Its failure path already ends in T-10 with no upload request when the stored sent bytes are absent, so B is mechanically covered; under B it is reached at the **first** `FILE_MISSING`, not the second.
- **The alert still fires.** OD-16 C-16.3 (every write that can hit the quota, FR-16 store included, once per failing user action) is not revoked by OD-37; B as offered kept the alert. Generation continues after it. The native `alert` is modal: script execution pauses until it is dismissed, so the client's 120 s deadline and the response handling resume after dismissal (the deadline tests use an injectable clock, so they are not affected). Two small hand-offs: T-20 ends "try again", which is wrong for this case (nothing needs a retry; the wording was confirmed in OD-41, so a change would need the human; P-8), and "focus goes to the input after dismissal" must target an input that is enabled at that moment (the alert can fire while Send is running and the input is locked, FR-21; P-8).
- **The recovery premise is not true as written (visible consequence).** The retained first user turn `U1` (the originals, with their `file_uri`) is protected and resent on every send (AC-21, AC-24, AC-29, OD-7 C-7.4). After the Files API file has expired (the log records that the Files API keeps files for a shorter period than 7 days, OD-16 question text) the stored `file_uri` is dead; with no stored bytes the client cannot replace it, so **every later send in that session meets `FILE_MISSING` again and shows T-10**. "Adding the image again" puts a new `file_uri` into the *new* turn only; the dead reference in `U1` is resent and the 410 repeats. The deterministic consequence of the decided rules is that T-10 ends the session's ability to send; viewing, downloading, "New session" and, while a version exists, a model switch that seeds a **new session** with the latest version (a seed is inline, FR-37, FR-50) still work, and a new session with a new upload recovers. This is not a Specification defect and is not blocking (the rules fix the outcome), but the human's expectation and T-10's text ("Add the images again", confirmed in OD-41) do not match it. Recorded for confirmation or a different choice in OD-50 **P-6**.
- **Combined effect with SD-34 (confirmed in OD-44).** Storage that is too full to keep the sent bytes will usually also fail the response commit; then the paid generation ends as a failed turn with no Retry and the result is not stored. B stops the *upload* from being blocked; it does not make the generation safe on a full store. Disclosed, accepted by the two confirmations.

**Conditions the Specification MUST carry.**
- **C-37.1** (FR-16, FR-81) When storing an upload's sent bytes fails with a quota error, the T-20 alert is shown (C-16.3: once per failing user action); the attachment stays attached, is uploaded to the Files API and is sent normally. It is neither rejected nor flagged as failed, and the other attachments are unaffected (FR-15). The upload request and the send never wait on, or depend on, the success of the store.
- **C-37.2** (FR-17) For an attachment whose sent bytes are not stored, a `FILE_MISSING` 410 ends at once in T-10: no upload request is made, the turn is a failed turn (FR-64), the failure is not retried. FR-17's existing "stored sent bytes no longer in IndexedDB" path is the same behaviour.
- **C-37.3** (FR-16, AC-28) The history entry stores the `file_uri` only; no base64 and no marker is added to it. AC-28 is amended: transparent recovery holds only while the sent bytes could be stored.
- **C-37.4** (FR-81 test) With `window.alert` spied and the sent-bytes `put` made to throw `QuotaExceededError`: exactly one alert call (T-20 of each language), the upload endpoint is called once, the attachment is present in the generate request, no `role="alert"` element and no `role="status"` update; then a forced `FILE_MISSING` yields T-10 and zero further upload requests.
- **C-37.5** (**Proposal P-7**, reading of C-16.3) A store failure during one attach action (up to five files) gives one alert; a store failure and a later commit failure belong to different user actions (attach, Send) and each may alert.
- **C-37.6** (**P-6, awaiting confirmation**) The aftermath of T-10 for the session's later sends is stated in the Specification as the literal consequence above, with the alternatives of OD-50 P-6, so the reconciliation stage and the operator are not surprised.

**Blocking: false** (decided; the consequence in P-6 is deterministic under the decided rules).


**Question.** The client stores the sent bytes of each uploaded image in IndexedDB as the only source for a re-upload after a `FILE_MISSING` 410 (FR-16, FR-17). When that store fails with a quota error (the native alert of FR-81 is shown), what happens to the attachment?

**Options.**
- **A. Reject the attachment** (it is not attached and not sent; the alert is shown; the other attachments stay, FR-15). The operator can free space and attach again.
- **B. Keep the attachment without a re-upload source** (it is attached and sent; the alert is shown; if its Files API file later expires, the re-upload cannot happen and the user sees T-10, FR-17 failure path, and must add the image again).
- **C. Another outcome**, stated by the human.

**What was checked.** FR-81 ("Not specified (open question Q-16c)"), FR-16 and FR-17 failure paths (a missing stored copy already ends in T-10 with no upload request, so B is mechanically consistent with FR-17); the human's OD-16 text names the alert, not the outcome for the data. SD-34 covers only the response commit (the turn is a failed turn, no Retry), not the upload store. No earlier decision covers it.

**Impact if unresolved.** FR-81's per-write outcome list has a hole for exactly one write; no test can assert whether an attachment exists after the failure. A guess of B silently weakens AC-28 (the expired-file recovery); a guess of A rejects a file the operator could have sent.

**Blocking: true.** Why: it leaves FR-16, FR-17 and FR-81 without a testable outcome for the quota path. **No default may be invented.** Specification Q-16c is this entry.

## OD-38 - Re-encode format of the EXIF-removing step; animated WEBP (Specification Q-12c; AC-2, AC-5, OD-12(c)) - RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS (v8)

### v8 resolution (human decision)

*The text below this v8 block is the earlier (v7) record, kept for history; where it says OPEN, this v8 block supersedes it.*

**Human decision (v8, verbatim).** "OD-38 (Перекодування / анімований WEBP): Анімований WEBP перетворюється на статичний (перший кадр). Формат перекодування має збігатися з оригінальним (PNG в PNG, JPEG в JPEG, WEBP в WEBP), щоб не змінювати розмір файлу непередбачуваним чином." (English rendering: An animated WEBP is turned into a static one (the first frame). The re-encode format must match the original (PNG to PNG, JPEG to JPEG, WEBP to WEBP), so as not to change the file size unpredictably. The human writes "JPEG"; the JPG naming of OD-18 applies to every user- or file-facing name, the MIME type stays `image/jpeg`.)

**Verification.**
- **Consistent with OD-18 (d) and D-19.** OD-18 (d) converts a differing API answer to "the input's format", and D-19 (confirmed in OD-45) reads that format from the magic bytes of the file as the user supplied it. The re-encode now keeps that same format, so "the original file" and "the sent bytes" always carry the same format, and the alternative reading of D-19 (the sent bytes) becomes equivalent. The one exception is a canvas that cannot encode the requested type (below): there the two would differ, which is why C-38.4 forbids sending a mismatching type.
- **The quality of the lossy re-encodes is not stated.** The only number the human has given is 90, for the *download* conversion to JPG (OD-18 (f)) and, confirmed in OD-45, for a WEBP conversion (D-17). A canvas encode needs a number for JPG and WEBP (PNG is lossless and takes none), and the browser's default when none is passed is implementation-defined, so a test cannot be deterministic without one. The log does not record 90 as decided for the upload re-encode; it is **Proposal P-9**, a Specification-fixed value for approval, with the alternative "another number".
- **The 5 MB bound after the re-encode (D-7, CF-20) stays and is confirmed.** Keeping the format reduces the swing the human wanted to avoid, but it does not remove it: a JPG saved at a lower quality than the re-encode quality can grow; a canvas PNG encode of an optimised or palette PNG can be larger than the original; a lossless WEBP re-encoded lossy is smaller, a lossy one at another quality may not be. Any of these can push a file of at most 5 MB over 5 MB, which is rejected with T-2 (C-12.3). V-8 (vi) records how often.
- **Alpha.** PNG and WEBP keep transparency through a canvas re-encode; a JPG source has none. The white fill of OD-18 (g) concerns the *download* conversion to JPG only; the upload re-encode fills nothing.
- **First frame.** Drawing an image to a canvas yields the image's default / first frame (to be confirmed by V-8 (v)), so no animation detection is needed and no rejection happens. The human named no notice; none is added (**Proposal P-10**: silent). An animated PNG (APNG) has PNG magic bytes and takes the same path, so it also becomes its first frame (a reading, P-10); GIF is not an accepted format (AC-2) and is rejected by the magic-byte rule.

**Conditions the Specification MUST carry.**
- **C-38.1** (FR-13, FR-11) The EXIF-removing re-encode outputs the **same format as the user's file**, identified by its magic bytes as supplied (D-19): PNG to PNG, JPG to JPG (`image/jpeg`), WEBP to WEBP. It never forces PNG and never converts between formats; alpha of PNG and WEBP is preserved.
- **C-38.2** (FR-13) An animated WEBP is re-encoded to a **single-frame** WEBP holding the first frame; it is not rejected. A test with an animated fixture asserts the sent bytes contain no animation chunks and that the frame is the first one. In the gate this runs through the injected encoder (C-39.4); the real browser behaviour is V-8 (v).
- **C-38.3** (FR-13; **P-9**) The JPG and WEBP re-encodes use one Specification-fixed quality value for approval; PNG is lossless. The value is named in the Specification, not left to the browser default.
- **C-38.4** (FR-13, FR-15; **P-11, reading awaiting confirmation**) The type of the encoder's output is compared with the required format (the returned Blob type and the magic bytes). A mismatch (a browser that cannot encode WEBP returns PNG, per the HTML canvas serialization rule, to be confirmed by V-8 (ii)) is a re-encode failure: the file is rejected on the client with T-1 and nothing is sent, extending OD-48 C-48.5 / OD-12 C-12.4 ("the metadata cannot be removed"). Sending the fallback type under the original name is not allowed. T-1 reads "Allowed formats: PNG, JPG, WEBP", which would mislead for this case: V-8 (ii) is a new-decision trigger.
- **C-38.5** (FR-12, D-7, CF-20) The 5 MB check is applied to the re-encoded bytes (unchanged and confirmed). A test with the injected encoder returning more than 5 242 880 B for a source of at most 5 MB asserts T-2 and no upload.
- **C-38.6** (FR-82) The input format of a version (FR-82) is unchanged; it is read from the original file (D-19).

**Blocking: false** (decided).


**Question.** The EXIF-removing re-encode (FR-13) produces the sent bytes. In which format, and what happens to an animated WEBP?

**Options.** Format: **A.** the same format as the file as supplied (PNG to PNG, JPG to JPG, WEBP to WEBP); **B.** always PNG (lossless, keeps alpha, usually larger); **C.** PNG when the image has transparency, otherwise JPG; **D.** another rule. Animated WEBP: **i.** rejected with the format error T-1; **ii.** re-encoded as its first frame only, silently; **iii.** first frame only, with a notice; **iv.** another outcome.

**What was checked.** The Specification marks "No default". The format of a download is decided from the user's original file (D-19, FR-82), not from the re-encode, so FR-82 does not depend on this. The re-encode does decide (1) whether alpha survives into the model request, (2) the D-7 outcome (CF-20): a re-encode over 5 MB is rejected with T-2, which is far more likely if a JPG photo were re-encoded as PNG, and (3) what the model sees of an animation. The client already exports canvases with `toDataURL('image/jpeg', 0.9)` (`src/app/app.component.ts` line 1348), a precedent, not a rule.

**Impact if unresolved.** The planner must choose, and the choice changes which files are rejected and whether transparency is lost; the Specification's FR-13 tests are format-agnostic (no EXIF in the sent bytes, decodable, source dimensions) so a Specification can still be approved.

**Blocking: false.** Why (honest closest call): the observable contract of FR-13 and the whole of FR-82 are testable without it, so the Specification writer does not guess a contract. If the human leaves it open, the planner must record the choice. Default: none offered (the Specification offers none).

## OD-39 - EXIF-removal mechanism; a library would be a new dependency (Specification Q-12a; AC-5, OD-12(a)) - RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS (v8)

### v8 resolution (human decision)

*The text below this v8 block is the earlier (v7) record, kept for history; where it says OPEN, this v8 block supersedes it.*

**Human decision (v8, verbatim).** "OD-39 (Механізм зняття EXIF): Використовуємо нативний HTML5 Canvas API на клієнті (відмалювати зображення на canvas і забрати через toBlob / toDataURL). Це автоматично знімає EXIF і не вимагає жодних нових бібліотек чи залежностей." (English rendering: We use the native HTML5 Canvas API on the client (draw the image on a canvas and take it out through toBlob / toDataURL). This removes EXIF automatically and needs no new libraries or dependencies.)

**Verification.**
- **No dependency, as stated.** `package.json` and `server/` list no image library; the client already draws to a canvas and exports with `canvas.toBlob(..., imgTargetFormat(), imgQuality())` (Image Tools mode, `src/app/app.component.ts` lines 1417-1445) and `toDataURL('image/jpeg', 0.9)` (line 1348). AGENTS.md section 1 is not triggered. The BFF stays free of image code (OD-40).
- **Format and quality.** `toBlob` takes the type and, for JPG and WEBP, a quality (C-38.1, C-38.3). The existing Image Tools code never checks the returned `blob.type`; the Image Studio must (C-38.4).
- **Browser behaviour the human's sentence does not cover (not verified here, recorded as V-8 for each operator browser; the repository has no `.browserslistrc`):** (i) **EXIF orientation**: a canvas draw applies the orientation tag in the browsers that honour `image-orientation: from-image`, and drops the tag, so the output can have swapped width and height; browsers have differed on this over time. (ii) **WEBP encode support**: the HTML canvas serialization rule replaces an unsupported type by PNG. (iii) **Colour**: the profile (ICC) is expected to be dropped and wide-gamut pixels converted to sRGB, browser-dependent; PNG text / XMP chunks and JPG segments are not carried over (this is also why the mechanism strips EXIF, GPS and XMP). (iv) **Size limits**: very large originals can fail to decode or draw (blank or error), which is the decode-failure path (T-1, OD-12 C-12.4). (v) the first frame of an animation.
- **Gate-test feasibility (new fact, checked in the repository).** Neither runner has a working canvas: `happy-dom` with no `canvasAdapter` returns `null` from `getContext()` and an empty Blob from `toBlob()` (`node_modules/happy-dom/lib/nodes/html-canvas-element/HTMLCanvasElement.js` lines 128-210); `vitest.config.ts` sets no adapter; no `canvas`, `jsdom` or `sharp` package is installed; the component runner (`angular.json` `test`, builder `@angular/build:unit-test`, runner `vitest`) has no spec that touches a canvas. The Specification's FR-13 / C-12.5 fixture test ("a JPG with GPS EXIF; the bytes handed to the upload endpoint contain none of it") cannot exercise a real canvas in the gate, and a fake encoder cannot prove that a canvas strips EXIF. What the gate *can* prove is the plumbing: the raw file's bytes never reach the upload endpoint or IndexedDB; only the encoder's output does; the encoder is called for every accepted upload including images under 2048 px and 5 MB (the case AC-4 would leave untouched); a decode / encode failure ends in T-1 with nothing sent. The real property (a real canvas output has no EXIF) is a browser fact (V-8 (iii)). That split is a **proposal for the human (W-2 in OD-50)**, in the manner of the AC-20 waiver (OD-20); a test-only dependency that provides a real canvas would be a new dependency and a human decision (AGENTS.md section 1) and would not prove what a real browser does.

**Conditions the Specification MUST carry.**
- **C-39.1** (FR-13, FR-11) The mechanism is a **client-side canvas re-draw and re-encode** (`toBlob`, or `toDataURL`) of every image added through the UI, whatever its size, including images that FR-11 would leave untouched. No library and no dependency is added; no image code is added to the BFF.
- **C-39.2** (FR-11, FR-13) Order: decode (failure: T-1) -> downscale when the long side is over 2048 px (FR-11) -> draw -> encode in the original format (C-38.1) at the fixed quality (C-38.3) -> compare the output type (C-38.4) -> the 5 MB check on the result (C-38.5) -> upload.
- **C-39.3** (FR-13) **Orientation (reading awaiting confirmation, P-12):** "source dimensions" in the FR-11 / FR-13 tests and the long side L of FR-11 mean the **as-displayed** (orientation-applied) dimensions; a fixture with an orientation tag (for example 6) is part of V-8 (i), not of the gate.
- **C-39.4** (FR-13 test, AGENTS.md section 5) The re-encode sits behind an injectable encoder seam. The gate test uses it to assert the plumbing listed above; the matrix row for AC-5 states what the gate proves and what V-8 (iii) proves (**W-2, awaiting the human**). The statement "contain none of the GPS EXIF" is asserted in the gate only on bytes produced by the fake encoder; claiming more would breach AGENTS.md section 6 item 7.
- **C-39.5** (Pre-implementation checks) **V-8** is added to the Specification's pre-implementation checks with its recorded result in the human-authored evidence file of OD-33, which also records the browser list. Its triggers are in the V-8 row.
- **C-39.6** (FR-13, accepted consequences; **P-13**) The mechanism drops the colour profile and may convert wide-gamut pixels to sRGB, and it drops every ancillary chunk and segment (EXIF, GPS, XMP, PNG text): listed with the accepted limitations, as a side effect of the human's choice.

**Blocking: false** (decided). A V-8 failure is a new-decision trigger, not a standing blocker.


**Question.** What removes the EXIF/GPS metadata: (A) a client canvas re-encode (the metadata is dropped as a side effect of re-drawing; no new dependency); (B) a client-side library; (C) a BFF-side image library?

**What was checked.** `package.json` and `server/` list no EXIF or image library (grep); the client already draws images to a canvas (`src/app/app.component.ts` lines 1342-1348, 1417). A new dependency is a human decision (AGENTS.md sections 1 and 7.8). The human's OD-12 decision (the BFF checks the magic bytes of the cleaned bytes) implies the cleaning happens before the BFF, which fits A or B, not C, but does not say so.

**Impact if unresolved.** FR-13 states only the observable result, and its fixture test (a JPG with GPS EXIF) is mechanism-neutral, so nothing blocks; only B or C would need explicit approval and a dependency entry.

**Blocking: false.** Why: A needs no dependency and the observable requirement is testable; the human is asked only if a library is wanted. Default: none offered by the Specification.

## OD-40 - BFF-side enforcement of size and EXIF; the accepted bypass (Specification Q-12b; FR-12, FR-13; OD-12(b)) - RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS (v8)

### v8 resolution (human decision)

*The text below this v8 block is the earlier (v7) record, kept for history; where it says OPEN, this v8 block supersedes it.*

**Human decision (v8, verbatim).** "OD-40 (BFF перевірка розміру / EXIF): Приймаємо обхід. BFF перевіряє лише magic bytes. Те, що прямим API-запитом можна обійти зняття EXIF або клієнтський ресайз — це прийнятний ризик для внутрішнього інструменту." (English rendering: We accept the bypass. The BFF checks only the magic bytes. That a direct API request can bypass the EXIF removal or the client resize is an acceptable risk for an internal tool.)

**Verification.** This is option A of the v7 entry. The product has no authentication (AGENTS.md; the security reviewer's stated premise), so the bypass is open to anyone who can reach the BFF; it is bounded by the 30 MB body cap (FR-51) and the rate limit (FR-66 to FR-68), which does not count uploads. The human's reasoning (internal tool) is recorded as the basis; the accepted risk is passed to SECURITY_REVIEW as a listed accepted limitation, not re-litigated there. Note that the bypass covers three things: the 5 MB bound, the 2048 px downscale and the EXIF removal. It does **not** weaken the magic-byte check, which stays on the BFF (OD-12 C-12.1).

**Conditions the Specification MUST carry.**
- **C-40.1** (FR-9, FR-12, FR-13, FR-51) The BFF's checks on `/api/image-studio/upload` are the magic bytes (415 `UNSUPPORTED_IMAGE_TYPE`) and the 30 MB body cap; there is no BFF check of the 5 MB size, of the pixel size or of EXIF, and no BFF image code or dependency.
- **C-40.2** (Accepted limitations) "A direct API call can send an image over 5 MB (up to the body cap), not downscaled and with its EXIF / GPS metadata, to the model" is listed as an accepted limitation, by the human's decision (OD-40).
- **C-40.3** (AC-4, AC-5 amendment; FR-12, FR-13 wording) AC-4 and AC-5 hold for images added through the Image Studio UI; the Specification's "known bypass" sentences become the accepted limitation. No BFF test asserts a size or EXIF rejection, and the AC-to-test row for AC-4 / AC-5 says "client-side".

**Blocking: false** (decided).


**Question.** Does the BFF also enforce the 5 MB size and the absence of EXIF on `/api/image-studio/upload`, or is client-only enforcement an accepted limitation (a direct API call can bypass FR-12 and FR-13)?

**Options.** **A.** Accept the bypass: the BFF checks the magic bytes only (OD-12, decided) plus the 30 MB body cap (FR-51). **B.** The BFF also rejects an upload whose decoded size exceeds 5 MB (a length check, no image library). **C.** The BFF also rejects bytes that still carry EXIF (needs metadata parsing: a hand-written byte-level scan or a library, OD-39). **D.** B and C.

**What was checked.** FR-12 records the bypass as "known"; the human's OD-8 and OD-12 decisions place the size rule on the client; the BFF has no image code. There is no authentication in the product, so the bypass is by anyone who can reach the BFF, but the 30 MB cap (FR-51) and the rate limit (FR-66 to FR-68, which does not count uploads) bound it.

**Impact if unresolved.** "Stripped from every image before it is sent to the model" (AC-5) is untestable at the BFF for a direct call; recorded as an accepted limitation by default of the Specification's wording.

**Blocking: false.** Why: recording A as an accepted limitation is a complete resolution; the Specification's client-side tests stand. Default (awaiting confirmation): the Specification proposes A by listing the bypass under "Accepted limitations".

## OD-41 - Confirm the proposed English and Ukrainian studio texts and the localised control labels (Specification Q-14a; AC-16, AC-18, AC-36, AC-46) - RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS (v8)

### v8 resolution (human decision)

## OD-42 - File of the chip instructions (Specification Q-15a); `{product}` timing (D-14) and empty case (D-15) (AC-15, AC-18) - RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS (v8)

### v8 resolution (human decision)

*The text below this v8 block is the earlier (v7) record, kept for history; where it says OPEN, this v8 block supersedes it.*

**Human decision (v8, verbatim).** "OD-42 (Локація інструкцій чипів): Інструкції чипів лежать у тому ж файлі, що й пресети (src/prompt-core/prompt-presets.ts)." (English rendering: The chip instructions live in the same file as the presets (`src/prompt-core/prompt-presets.ts`).)

**Verification.** Only sub-question (a) is answered by this sentence. The file is non-FROZEN and lies in the coverage include list `src/prompt-core/**/*.ts` (`vitest.config.ts` lines 55-60), so AGENTS.md section 5 floors apply to the chip instructions too (NFR-13); Rule 3 holds (prompt text in `src/prompt-core/`). Sub-questions (b) D-14 (the substitution moment) and (c) D-15 (the empty product field) are **not restated** by the human; they are settled only through the confirmation of the conflict register (OD-49, CF-23: "`{product}` substitution timing and empty case"), which the human confirmed in full. That is recorded as the route; the human did not discuss D-14 or D-15 individually.

**Conditions the Specification MUST carry.**
- **C-42.1** (FR-78, FR-31, SD-21) The chip instructions (the model-facing text each chip sends) are in `src/prompt-core/prompt-presets.ts` with the presets. FR-78's sentence "the file for the chips is a planning choice" and Q-15a are closed.
- **C-42.2** (NFR-13) The coverage floors of AGENTS.md section 5 apply to the chip instructions as part of that file.
- **C-42.3** (FR-79) D-14 (`{product}` substituted at pick time from the field's value at that moment; later changes do not rewrite the text) and D-15 (an empty field gives the English words "the product") are carried as **confirmed through CF-23 (OD-49)**; their "PROPOSED DEFAULT" labels flip.

**Blocking: false** (decided).


**Question.** (a) Where do the chip follow-up instructions (the model-facing text each chip sends) live: the same file as the presets, another file in `src/prompt-core/`, or a file in `src/prompts/`? (b) D-14: is `{product}` substituted when the preset is picked? (c) D-15: what does an empty product-name field produce?

**Options.** (a) **A.** `src/prompt-core/prompt-presets.ts`; **B.** another file in `src/prompt-core/`; **C.** a file in `src/prompts/`; or leave it to the planner. (b) **A.** at pick time from the field's value at that moment, the text then stays editable and is not rewritten (D-14); **B.** at send time, so the editable text still shows the placeholder until sent. (c) **A.** replaced by "the product" (D-15); **B.** the preset cannot be picked until the field is filled; **C.** the placeholder is left for the operator to edit; **D.** another rule.

**What was checked.** AGENTS.md Rule 3 allows `src/prompts/` or `src/prompt-core/`; the human named a file for the presets only (OD-15). `src/prompts/` holds three FROZEN files (`task-a.ts`, `task-b.ts`, `task-c.ts`) but a new file there is not frozen. AC-33 says a missing product name becomes `image` in the file name, which is not stated for the prompt.

**Impact if unresolved.** (a) is a planning choice with no behavioural effect; (b) and (c) fix what the editable text shows after a pick and are covered by the FR-79 tests only for the defaults.

**Blocking: false.** Default (awaiting confirmation): (a) planner's choice; (b) A (D-14); (c) A (D-15).

## OD-43 - Two browser tabs on one session (Specification Q-16b; AC-25; OD-16(d)) - RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS (v8)

### v8 resolution (human decision)

*The text below this v8 block is the earlier (v7) record, kept for history; where it says OPEN, this v8 block supersedes it.*

**Human decision (v8, verbatim).** "OD-43 (Дві вкладки на одну сесію): Перемагає останній запис (last write wins). Складна синхронізація вкладок не потрібна." (English rendering: The last write wins. Complex tab synchronisation is not needed.)

**Verification.** This is option A of the v7 entry (accepted limitation); no lock, notice or sync is added. It closes OD-16 sub-question (d). The consequences, which interact with the other v8 decisions, are listed so approval is informed: a trim deleted by one tab may still be sent by the other (the BFF only echoes the ids it was sent, OD-16 v4 cross-note); a session evicted (OD-36) or expired (FR-48) by one tab stays visible in the other until its next load, and a response for it is discarded (SD-22) although the generation was paid; two tabs creating sessions at once can leave more than 20 stored sessions until the next creation (C-36.7). "Last write wins" is read at the granularity of the FR-44 transaction: each tab's commit is atomic; whether a later commit can overwrite an earlier tab's added turn depends on how the record is stored, a planning matter (the observable: after two tabs commit, the stored session contains at least the later commit).

**Conditions the Specification MUST carry.**
- **C-43.1** (FR-48, Accepted limitations) Two tabs on one session: last write wins, no cross-tab synchronisation, lock or notice; accepted by the human (OD-43). Q-16b is closed.
- **C-43.2** (FR-44) Each tab's commit remains one atomic transaction (unchanged); the Specification lists the consequences above under the limitation.

**Blocking: false** (decided).


**Question.** If the same session is open in two tabs, what happens?

**Options.** **A.** Accepted limitation: each write is atomic per tab (FR-44) and the last write wins; no cross-tab sync; a trimmed turn deleted in one tab may still be sent by the other, and the BFF only echoes the ids it was sent. **B.** Detect the other tab and show a notice. **C.** Only one tab may hold a session (a lock). **D.** Another rule.

**What was checked.** OD-16 v4 cross-note (the race on a trim); the Specification FR-44 (atomic commit) and FR-48 ("Two tabs on one session remain open (Q-16b)"); there is no IndexedDB convention in `src/`.

**Impact if unresolved.** A rare data race; no requirement is untestable (each tab's behaviour is specified).

**Blocking: false.** Why: A recorded as an accepted limitation is a complete resolution. Default: none offered by the Specification ("No default").

## OD-44 - Confirm what counts as "activity" (SD-32), the expiry boundary (SD-33) and the quota-commit outcome (SD-34) (Specification Q-16d; AC-25) - RESOLVED (v8)

### v8 resolution (human decision)

*The text below this v8 block is the earlier (v7) record, kept for history; where it says OPEN, this v8 block supersedes it.*

**Human decision (v8, verbatim).** "OD-44, OD-45, OD-47, OD-49: Повністю підтверджую запропоновані дефолти та умови (SD-32, SD-33, SD-34, D-17, D-19…D-22, MIME, ZIP, канали повідомлень SD-36 та реєстр CF). Для буфера обміну — завжди копіюємо як PNG." (English rendering: I fully confirm the proposed defaults and conditions (SD-32, SD-33, SD-34, D-17, D-19 to D-22, MIME, ZIP, notification channels SD-36 and the CF register). For the clipboard we always copy as PNG.) The same sentence answers OD-45, OD-47 and OD-49.

**Confirmed, as written in Specification v4:** **SD-32** (the last-activity stamp is set at creation and renewed by a committed FR-44 write, a cut, or a downloaded-flag change; opening, selecting or viewing is not activity; the named alternative "opening also counts" is not applied), **SD-33** (expired when strictly more than 604 800 000 ms old, exactly 604 800 000 ms is kept; expiry only on load, never on a timer, so an open tab can show an expired session), **SD-34** (a quota failure on the response commit leaves the session unchanged, the turn is a failed turn with its content back in the composer, and no Retry button).

**Verification and hand-offs (the confirmation makes these three load-bearing for OD-36).**
- SD-32 decides the eviction order of OD-36, and because *viewing is not activity* the session in use is not guaranteed to be the most recent: this is why OD-36 C-36.4 excludes it.
- **Wording defect for the Specification (SD-32).** Its lead sentence says "every committed write to the session record" while the list names three events (an accepted turn, a cut, a downloaded-flag change). The list is the rule; the Specification states it is exhaustive and that the FR-16 sent-bytes store and an unsent composer change are **not** activity. The creation of the session sets the stamp.
- SD-34 combined with OD-37: see OD-37 (a full store can fail the commit after a generation was paid).

**Conditions the Specification MUST carry.**
- **C-44.1** (FR-48, SD-32 to SD-34) The three values stand as written, the labels "awaiting confirmation" flip to "confirmed by the human (OD-44)", and the boundary tests (604 799 999 / 604 800 000 / 604 800 001 ms) stand.
- **C-44.2** (SD-32) The list of activity events is stated as exhaustive (above).

**Blocking: false** (decided).


**Question.** Does the human confirm or replace: SD-32 (the last-activity stamp is set at creation and updated by a committed user-turn/model-turn write, a cut, or a downloaded-flag change; opening, selecting or viewing is not activity; alternative: opening also counts), SD-33 (expired when strictly more than 604 800 000 ms old, exactly 604 800 000 ms is kept; expiry runs only on load, never on a timer, so an open tab can show an expired session until the next load) and SD-34 (a quota failure on the response commit leaves the session unchanged, the turn is a failed turn with its content back in the composer, and no Retry button)?

**What was checked.** The human said "7 днів від останньої активності" and "застарілі видаляються при ініціалізації/завантаженні" (OD-16) and defined neither "activity" nor the boundary; SD-32 to SD-34 are Specification derivations listed for confirmation.

**Impact if unresolved.** The tests use these values (FR-48 boundaries 604 799 999 / 604 800 000 / 604 800 001 ms); a wrong value makes sessions expire sooner or later than the operator expects.

**Blocking: false.** Default (awaiting confirmation): SD-32, SD-33, SD-34 as written.

## OD-45 - OD-18 residues: D-17, D-19 to D-22, the MIME reading of "JPG" (SD-40), clipboard and ZIP readings (AC-31 to AC-35) - RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS (v8)

### v8 resolution (human decision)

*The text below this v8 block is the earlier (v7) record, kept for history; where it says OPEN or "awaiting confirmation", this v8 block supersedes it.*

**Human decision (v8, verbatim).** The sentence quoted in OD-44: "Повністю підтверджую запропоновані дефолти та умови (SD-32, SD-33, SD-34, D-17, D-19…D-22, MIME, ZIP, канали повідомлень SD-36 та реєстр CF). Для буфера обміну — завжди копіюємо як PNG." (English rendering: ... D-17, D-19 to D-22, MIME, ZIP ... The clipboard copy is always PNG.)

**Exactly what is confirmed (the table of the v7 entry, row by row).**
| Id | Confirmed value |
|---|---|
| D-17 | WEBP encode quality 90 **when a conversion targets WEBP** (a download conversion, FR-82). |
| D-19 | The "input image" is identified by the magic bytes of the file as the user supplied it, before any downscale or EXIF re-encode. |
| D-20 | A text-only follow-up turn (no upload, no attached version) inherits the latest version's format. |
| D-21 | A message with both an upload and an attached version takes the format of the first uploaded image. |
| D-22 | One selector value per version, initial = inherited format, not persisted across reloads; the inherited format is stored with the version; **the ZIP uses each version's own value, so one ZIP may hold mixed formats** (this is the "ZIP" in the human's list). |
| SD-40 | "JPG" is the user- and file-facing name and `.jpg` the extension; the MIME type stays `image/jpeg` and the magic bytes `FF D8 FF` are unchanged (this is the "MIME" in the human's list). |
| clipboard | **Always PNG**, whatever the target format of the version; the client converts to PNG when the API format is not PNG. The alternative (copy in the target format) is not applied. |

**Verification.**
- D-17's number is the human's, but only for a *download* conversion. It is **not** the quality of the upload re-encode, which is OD-38 / P-9 (the human gave no number for it).
- With OD-38 (format preserved), D-19's two readings (original file or sent bytes) give the same format except when a canvas returns a different type, which C-38.4 rejects.
- "Always PNG" for the clipboard confirms FR-57 and CF-16's clipboard clause; the clipboard is not governed by the selector or by the FR-82 table.
- N-2 (review v4) stays a Specification-local hand-off: the FR-82 test list must name PNG to WEBP, WEBP to PNG and WEBP to JPG (with a transparent WEBP and the white fill) and reword test (iv) (OD-18 C-18.8).

**Conditions the Specification MUST carry.**
- **C-45.1** (FR-82, FR-55, FR-58, FR-57, SD-40) The "PROPOSED DEFAULT" labels D-17 and D-19 to D-22, and the "for human confirmation" label of SD-40, flip to "confirmed by the human (OD-45)"; the values are unchanged.
- **C-45.2** (FR-57) The clipboard copy is always PNG; a test copies versions of each source format and asserts a PNG signature each time.
- **C-45.3** (FR-82) D-17 is stated as the quality of a download conversion to WEBP only.

**Blocking: false** (decided).


**Question.** The human's answers (a) to (g) do not cover the following; each has a Specification default awaiting confirmation.

| Id | Question | Default (awaiting confirmation) | Alternative |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-17 | WEBP encode quality when a conversion targets WEBP | 90 (the JPG number) | another number |
| D-19 | How the "input image" is identified | the magic bytes of the file as the user supplied it, before downscale or EXIF re-encode | the sent bytes |
| D-20 | Format of a text-only follow-up turn (no upload, no attached version) | inherits the latest version's format (rule (a) read as "a session with no version") | PNG |
| D-21 | A message with both an upload and an attached version | the first uploaded image wins | the attached version wins |
| D-22 | Selector granularity | one value per version, initial = inherited format, not persisted across reloads; the inherited format is stored with the version; the ZIP uses each version's value (mixed formats possible) | one global selector (one format per ZIP) |
| SD-40 | "JPG" and the technical identifiers | MIME type `image/jpeg` and magic bytes `FF D8 FF` unchanged; "JPG" is the user/file-facing name and `.jpg` the extension | the human wants another meaning |
| clipboard | AC-34 "Copy to clipboard for PNG" | stays PNG, converting when the API format is not PNG | copy in the target format |

**What was checked.** The Story (AC-31 to AC-35), the human's answers (a) to (g), and Specification FR-54 to FR-58, FR-82, CF-16 to CF-18. The Specification marks each of these as labelled and for the human to confirm.

**Impact if unresolved.** Only readings of cases the human did not state; the stated rules are recorded in OD-18 and are testable.

**Blocking: false.** Why: each has a derived, labelled default and the contract of OD-18 does not depend on them. Defaults are listed above; none is a decision.

## OD-46 - `alt` of an empty prompt (Specification Q-20a, D-16); the "100 + `...`" reading (CF-9, N-5); whether an image-only message is valid (AC-8, AC-47) - RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS (v8)

### v8 resolution (human decision)

*The text below this v8 block is the earlier (v7) record, kept for history; where it says OPEN or "awaiting confirmation", this v8 block supersedes it.*

**Human decision (v8, verbatim).** "OD-46 (Empty prompt / alt text): Повідомлення лише з зображенням (без тексту) є валідним. Якщо текстовий промпт порожній, alt (для AC-47) отримує дефолтне значення: "Згенероване зображення" (або "Generated image" для EN)." (English rendering: A message with only an image (no text) is valid. If the text prompt is empty, the `alt` (for AC-47) gets the default value: "Згенероване зображення" (or "Generated image" for EN).)

**What is decided, and by which route.**
- (c) **An image-only message is valid** (OD-46's own sentence).
- (a) **The `alt` of an image whose prompt is empty** is the literal "Згенероване зображення" in `uk` and "Generated image" in `en`. These are exactly the values of T-21 (D-16 proposed them); D-16 is therefore **human-decided**, not a default.
- (b) **"100 plus `...`", appended only when the prompt is actually cut:** OD-46's sentence does not address it. It is settled by two other confirmations: CF-9 (confirmed with the register, OD-49: "`alt` = 100 code points plus `...` (103 in total); `...` only when cut") and SD-35 (accepted with SD-1 to SD-40). The reading "always append" (a short prompt would gain `...`) is not applied. The exact rule: let P be the text sent in the turn (after the FR-79 substitution; for a chip turn, the chip instruction), trimmed, with every interior run of whitespace collapsed to one space; if P has at most 100 Unicode code points `alt` is P; otherwise `alt` is its first 100 code points, with any whitespace left at the end of the cut removed, followed by `...` (three ASCII full stops, U+002E); at most 103 code points. All images of a multi-image response get the same `alt`. A non-empty `alt` is the prompt and is not localised (CF-3).

**Verification (reconciling FR-18, D-16 / FR-75, AC-8, AC-1).**
- **AC-8** ("text-only or text plus images") is **extended** to a third form: images only. **AC-1** (how images are added) is unchanged. **AC-3** (at most five uploads) and FR-10 are unchanged.
- **FR-18** enumerated only text-only and text-plus-images and rejected "neither"; D-16 and FR-75 assumed an image-only message. They now agree: FR-18 lists three valid forms (text only; text plus images; images only) and its failure path stays "neither text nor an image": a message whose text is empty or only whitespace and that has no image is not valid and is not sent. The Send action is enabled when there is non-empty text or at least one image.
- **What counts as an image.** The human wrote "лише з зображенням". The Specification reading (P-14, awaiting confirmation): an uploaded image **or** the attached version of FR-37 counts, so "Edit this version" with no text is a valid message.
- **What is sent.** The turn carries the image parts and no text part; no filler text is invented. Whether the live API accepts such a turn, and what it returns, is a vendor fact: check **V-9**. The BFF request schema must accept a user turn without text when it has an image (hand-off to FR-3 / FR-19); a rejection by the API is a V-9 new-decision trigger.
- **`alt` language.** T-21 is a studio text, resolved from `uiLanguage` when it is rendered (FR-77), so a toggle re-renders it; a prompt-derived `alt` is not touched by the toggle (**P-15**, derived from FR-77).

**Conditions the Specification MUST carry.**
- **C-46.1** (FR-18, AC-8) Three valid message forms as above; the failure path is unchanged. Tests: an image-only message is accepted and sent; a whitespace-only message with no image is rejected and not sent; the attached-version-only message follows P-14.
- **C-46.2** (FR-75, T-21) The `alt` of an image whose P is empty (empty or whitespace-only text) is T-21: "Згенероване зображення" / "Generated image". D-16's label flips to human-decided; T-21's Basis flips to "human (OD-46)". Tests: empty and whitespace-only prompts in each language.
- **C-46.3** (FR-75, SD-35, CF-9) The exact cut rule above, with the boundary tests of FR-75 (99, 100, 101 code points; 101 Cyrillic characters; an astral character as the 100th and the 101st; a space as the 100th; leading and trailing whitespace; an interior run of three spaces). N-5 (review v4) is applied: the Specification names "`...` is appended only when the prompt is actually cut" as a reading, and now records it as confirmed (CF-9, SD-35).
- **C-46.4** (FR-3, FR-19, V-9) The generate request schema accepts a user turn that has images and no text; the pre-implementation check V-9 is added.
- **C-46.5** (FR-75) An image is never rendered with an empty `alt` (unchanged).

**Blocking: false** (decided).


**Question.** (a) What is the `alt` of a generated image whose prompt is empty after trimming? (b) Is `...` appended only when the prompt is actually cut (so a prompt of at most 100 code points is the `alt` unchanged), and is the result 100 code points plus `...` (103 in all) or 100 in all? (c) Is an image-only message (images, no text) a valid message at all?

**Options.** (a) **A.** the fixed text T-21 ("Generated image" / "Згенероване зображення", proposed); **B.** the preset name; **C.** forbid image-only messages (then the question disappears); **D.** another text. (b) appended only when cut: **yes** / **always**; length: **100 plus `...`** / **100 in all**. (c) **valid** / **not valid**.

**What was checked.** The human said "просто беремо промпт і відрізаємо його до 100 символів (з додаванням ...)". Specification SD-35 reads: code points, trim and collapse whitespace first, three ASCII dots, 103 total, whitespace at the cut removed. "Simply" suggests less normalisation than SD-35 applies; that is flagged, not changed. For (c): Specification FR-18 says "A message may be text only ... or text plus images" and its failure path rejects only "neither text nor an image", while D-16 and FR-75 assume an image-only message exists; AC-8 (Story) enumerates text-only and text-plus-images. The Story and the Specification therefore disagree on whether image-only is valid.

**Impact if unresolved.** (a) a generated image without a defined `alt` is an accessibility defect (AC-47); (b) the boundary tests at 99, 100 and 101 code points depend on it; (c) decides whether (a) can occur.

**Blocking: false.** Default (awaiting confirmation): (a) A (D-16); (b) appended only when cut, 100 plus `...` (SD-35); (c) the Specification must make FR-18 and D-16 agree (a hand-off to SPECIFICATION is recorded in the clarification report).

## OD-47 - Live-region channels the human did not name (Specification Q-21a, SD-36; AC-46, AC-48) - RESOLVED (v8)

### v8 resolution (human decision)

*The text below this v8 block is the earlier (v7) record, kept for history; where it says OPEN or "awaiting confirmation", this v8 block supersedes it.*

**Human decision (v8, verbatim).** The sentence quoted in OD-44, which names "канали повідомлень SD-36" (the notification channels of SD-36) among the confirmed items.

## OD-48 - Reviewer observations N-1 to N-11 accepted as clarifications (human) - RESOLVED-WITH-CONDITIONS

**Human decision (v7, verbatim).** "усі застереження приймаються як уточнення" (English rendering: all the reservations are accepted as clarifications). Explicit decisions: N-5 (prompt-level OTHER): "prompt-level OTHER — це ненормальне завершення (abnormal)". N-6 (thought images): "Проміжні thought-частини відкидаються, вони не вважаються версіями, не нумеруються і не тарифікуються". N-7 and N-10: "Якщо файл неможливо декодувати для ресайзу/зрізання EXIF, він просто відхиляється на клієнті з помилкою формату (T-1)". N-11: "відсутність ціни для моделі (500 PRICE_NOT_CONFIGURED) пріоритетніша за звичайну відповідь класу 2". (English renderings: N-5: a prompt-level OTHER is an abnormal completion. N-6: interim thought parts are discarded; they are not versions, are not numbered and are not billed. N-7 and N-10: if a file cannot be decoded for the resize / EXIF stripping it is simply rejected on the client with the format error (T-1). N-11: a missing price for the model (500 `PRICE_NOT_CONFIGURED`) takes precedence over an ordinary class-2 answer.)

**Verification.** The wording of N-1 to N-11 comes from the revision-note table of Specification v4; the original review v3 was revised in place and its text is not retained, so the table is the record used. The typings description of a prompt-level `BlockedReason.OTHER` ("the prompt was blocked for other reasons. For example, it may be due to the prompt's language, or because it contains other harmful content", `node_modules/@google/genai/dist/genai.d.ts` line 1258) shows why a Retry can repeat the block; the human accepted that.

**Conditions the Specification MUST carry.**
- **C-48.1** (all) N-1 to N-11 are clarifications of the Specification's wording: N-1 FR-26 enum test shape (parametrised member by part shape by candidate presence); N-2 "not billed" read as "no cost recorded or shown" (the vendor's billing is not asserted); N-3 the BFF check is always on the re-encoded (sent) bytes; N-4 the pair count is `n` and `k` keeps only its FR-40 meaning; N-8 D-7's visible consequence is stated (CF-20); N-9 SD-25's wording is corrected.
- **C-48.2** (N-6, FR-84, FR-23, FR-26, FR-70, CF-21) A thought image part is discarded: it is not a valid image part for the response classification, not a version, consumes no version number, is not shown and is not priced; a response whose only images are thought parts is classified by its remaining content. **Reading awaiting confirmation (CF-21, D-18):** "discarded" does not remove the thought part from the stored model turn: it stays and is resent unmodified, because AC-21 requires unmodified model turns and a `thoughtSignature` may travel with it, and its inline bytes count toward the 18 MB measure. The alternative (removed from storage and every request) is not applied.
- **C-48.3** (N-5, SD-23, FR-26) A prompt-level `OTHER` (and `BLOCKED_REASON_UNSPECIFIED`, and any unknown value) is an abnormal completion: class 4, HTTP 502 `EMPTY_RESPONSE` with Retry.
- **C-48.4** (N-11, CF-24, FR-26, FR-70) A missing price (HTTP 500 `PRICE_NOT_CONFIGURED`, no Retry, SD-26) takes precedence over an ordinary class-2 `images` answer; FR-26 states the exception. **CF-24 is recorded here.**
- **C-48.5** (N-7, N-10, FR-11, FR-13, FR-15) Decode failure is checked before size; an undecodable file, or one whose EXIF cannot be removed, is rejected on the client with T-1 (recorded in OD-12 C-12.4).
- **C-48.6** (N-9, SD-25) **Not confirmed by the human in v7:** accepting N-9 as a clarification did not confirm that "5 MB" means 5 242 880 B. SD-25 stayed a Specification-fixed value for confirmation at `HUMAN_SPEC_APPROVAL`. **v8: CLOSED.** The human wrote "SD-25: Прямо підтверджую, що 5 MB = 5 242 880 B (1024-based)" (English rendering: I directly confirm that 5 MB = 5 242 880 B, 1024-based) and accepted SD-1 to SD-40. The Specification drops "stays a Specification choice, not confirmed by the human" in SD-25, in the Size units definition and in the overrides row, and cites OD-48 (v8) as the authority. The FR-53 remark that "about 6.7 MB each as base64" does not prove the 1024-based reading stays true and is no longer needed.
- **C-48.2 (v8 note).** Its "reading awaiting confirmation" (CF-21, D-18) is confirmed with the register (OD-49).

**Blocking: false** (decided).
