## OD-50 - Residual readings and proposals that follow from the v8 decisions (AC-5, AC-8, AC-25, AC-26, AC-28, AC-47; FR-13, FR-16, FR-17, FR-18, FR-81, FR-86) - OPEN (non-blocking)

**Question.** The human's v8 answers settle the contracts, but leave points that the Specification must state in some way. Each is a **Proposal (not a decision)**: a value or reading this stage or the Specification offers, with its alternative, to be confirmed or replaced at `HUMAN_SPEC_APPROVAL`. Accepting the Specification without a word on an item does not turn it into a decision (the rule of the earlier reports). The Specification assigns its own SD / D ids when it carries them.

| Id | Source | Proposal (not a decision) | Alternative |
|---|---|---|---|
| P-1 | OD-36 C-36.4 | Eviction never deletes the session the operator is leaving or using, a session with a generation in flight in this tab, or the session being created. | No exclusion (the literal oldest stamp). It can delete the session in use, contradicts AC-26, FR-47 and the confirmed T-23, and discards a paid response (SD-22). |
| P-2 | OD-36 C-36.5 | Ties on the last-activity stamp are broken by a fixed rule written into the Specification as an SD item. The human gave none; this stage does not choose. Candidates: the older creation time, then the smaller session id in code-unit order; or the session id alone. | Any other deterministic rule. |
| P-3 | OD-36 C-36.6 | Eviction and creation are one IndexedDB transaction; the seed is read before any deletion; a failed creation (quota) deletes nothing. | Separate steps (a failed creation could then delete a session and create none). |
| P-4 | OD-36 C-36.7 | "While the stored count would exceed 20, delete the next candidate", so a count above 20 left by two tabs heals at the next creation. | Delete exactly one. |
| P-5 | OD-36 verification (ii) | "New session" creates a row even when the current session holds no turn (FR-49 as written), so repeated confirmations push real sessions out with empty ones. | "New session" on a session with no turn reuses it and creates no row. A new behaviour; not decided by the human. |
| P-6 | OD-37 verification | After T-10 caused by absent sent bytes, every later send in that session meets `FILE_MISSING` again (the protected `U1` keeps the dead `file_uri`); only viewing, downloading, a New session or a model switch seeded with the latest version recover. The Specification states this consequence. T-10 reads "Add the images again", which does not repair the session. | (B) A re-added image replaces the dead references in the retained turns (a new mapping rule, in tension with AC-21 "unmodified"). (C) Change T-10's text to say that a new session is needed. (D) Another rule. |
| P-7 | OD-37 C-37.5 | One alert per failing user action: a store failure while attaching up to five files gives one alert; a later commit failure at Send is another action. | One alert per failing write. |
| P-8 | OD-37, OD-41 | Hand-offs: T-20 is used as written although its "try again" does not fit a store-only failure (changing a confirmed text needs the human); after the alert is dismissed focus goes to the input only if it is enabled at that moment. | Reword T-20 for this case; focus the nearest enabled control. |
| P-9 | OD-38 C-38.3 | Quality of the lossy upload re-encode: the Specification states one number; the only number the human gave is 90 (download JPG, OD-18 (f); WEBP conversion, D-17). Candidate: 90 for JPG and WEBP, none for PNG. | Another number. |
| P-10 | OD-38 | An animated WEBP becomes its first frame silently (no notice text); an APNG has PNG magic bytes and takes the same path, so it also becomes its first frame. | A notice (a new text) or a rejection. |
| P-11 | OD-38 C-38.4 | An encoder output whose type differs from the required format (a browser without WEBP encode) is a re-encode failure: rejected on the client with T-1, nothing sent. V-8 (ii) decides whether T-1's text is acceptable. | Send the fallback type (breaks the format rule); a new text; a library. |
| P-12 | OD-39 C-39.3 | "Source dimensions" and the long side L mean the as-displayed (orientation-applied) dimensions. | The stored pixel dimensions before orientation. |
| P-13 | OD-39 C-39.6 | Accepted side effects of the canvas: ICC profile dropped and wide-gamut pixels possibly converted to sRGB; all ancillary chunks and segments dropped. | A mechanism that keeps the profile (a library; a human decision). |
| P-14 | OD-46 | Whitespace-only text counts as empty; an attached version (FR-37) counts as an image, so "Edit this version" with no text is a valid message; the turn carries no text part. | Only an uploaded image counts; send a filler text. |
| P-15 | OD-46 | T-21 is resolved from `uiLanguage` when rendered (FR-77); a prompt-derived `alt` is never localised. | Fix the `alt` language at generation time. |
| W-2 | OD-39 C-39.4 | **Proposed waiver, in the manner of OD-20 / W-1.** The gate proves the plumbing of the EXIF removal through an injected encoder (the raw bytes never reach the upload or the store; only encoder output does; the encoder runs for every accepted upload; failures end in T-1). That a real canvas output carries no EXIF / GPS is verified by the browser check V-8 (iii), not by an automated test, because neither runner has a working canvas (checked). The AC-5 row of the AC-to-test matrix says so ("client-side; gate = plumbing; real property = V-8"). | (B) A test-only dependency that provides a real canvas (a new dependency, AGENTS.md section 1, human decision; it would still not prove a real browser's output). (C) Another verification. |

**What was checked.** The Specification v4 text of FR-13, FR-16, FR-17, FR-18, FR-47, FR-49, FR-50, FR-75, FR-81, FR-82, FR-86, SD-22, SD-32; the human's v8 answers; `vitest.config.ts`, `angular.json`, `package.json`, `node_modules` and the happy-dom canvas implementation (OD-39); the existing canvas code in `src/app/app.component.ts`. None of these items is answered by an earlier decision.

**Impact if unresolved.** None blocks: each item has a stated proposal the Specification can carry under a label. A wrong proposal changes: which session an eviction removes (P-1, P-2, P-5), the aftermath of an expired file (P-6), the size and quality of a sent image (P-9), the wording a user sees (P-8, P-11), and what the AC-5 test proves (W-2).

**Blocking: false.** Why: a labelled proposal exists for every item; the writer does not guess a contract. The human confirms or replaces them at `HUMAN_SPEC_APPROVAL` (P-6, P-1 and W-2 are the three that change what the operator or the reconciliation stage will experience).
